326 — Distributed Inference Router

Project Number: 326

Project Name: Distributed Inference Router

Owner: Mujir Qureshi

Document: FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md

Status: specification — locked

1. Goal

1.1 Engineering Definition

Build a from-scratch, production-grade control+data-plane inference router in Rust (edition 2021, MSRV 1.78) that sits in front of a fleet of LLM inference backends (vLLM, SGLang, TGI-style replicas) and provides: ingress normalization, KV/prefix-cache-aware routing, admission control + priority queueing, streaming token relay, resilience (retries/hedging/circuit-break), adapter/model-aware dispatch, disaggregated prefill/decode coordination, autoscaling signal emission, and full observability. The router schedules and routes; it does not run the model, manage GPUs, own persistent storage, or implement auth/billing.

1.2 System Boundary (IN scope)

Responsibility Owner crate
Ingress: HTTP/1.1, HTTP/2, gRPC, SSE/chunked relay infr-ingress
Backend registry, model→replica map, adapter inventory infr-registry
Health + load probing, EWMA, ejection state machine infr-health
Routing policy: least-load, prefix-cache, adapter-aware, affinity infr-route
Admission control, bounded priority queues, SLO deadlines, shedding infr-queue
Connection pool, request forwarding, streaming relay, circuit breaker infr-dispatch
Global scheduler: fairness, autoscale signals, disaggregated prefill/decode infr-sched
Metrics (TTFT/TPOT/queue-depth/cache-hit), tracing, structured logs infr-telemetry
Core types shared across all crates infr-core
CLI driver + config infr-cli
Load + chaos harness infr-bench

1.3 Explicit NON-Goals

  • Model inference execution (runs on backends, not here).
  • GPU device management, CUDA/ROCm resource allocation.
  • Authentication, authorization, billing, rate-limiting by API key (hooks exist; logic deferred).
  • Persistent storage of requests, completions, or model weights.
  • Model registry / artifact management (weights download, versioning).
  • Full API-gateway feature set (WAF, TLS termination policy, OAuth).
  • Multi-tenant network isolation or per-tenant egress rules.
  • Training, fine-tuning, RLHF pipeline orchestration.
  • KV-cache population / prefill offload execution (router emits hints; backend acts).

1.4 Deployment Model

[Clients: curl, SDK, LLM app]
      |  HTTP/1.1 | HTTP/2 | gRPC
[infr-ingress:0.0.0.0:8080/8443]   (1–N stateless router pods; L4 LB in front)
      |
[internal async channels]
[infr-queue / infr-sched]           (per-router in-process; no cross-router coordination)
      |
[infr-route / infr-registry / infr-health]
      |  HTTP/2 to backends
[Backend replicas: vLLM / SGLang / TGI]  (fleet; any AZ; same or remote DC)
  • Router is stateless across restarts (registry reloads from config/service-discovery).
  • Horizontal scale: add router pods behind L4 LB; no sticky-session required at L4 (session affinity handled per-router when needed).
  • Vertical: single router handles O(10K) concurrent requests on ≥8-core server.
  • Config reload: SIGHUP or admin endpoint; zero-downtime.

1.5 Target Users

Actor Interaction
Operators Deploy router pods; configure backend fleet, SLOs, admission policies via TOML/YAML; scrape Prometheus metrics
Developers Call OpenAI-compatible HTTP endpoints; observe TTFT/TPOT; integrate streaming SSE
Platform engineers Extend routing policies; wire autoscale signals to HPA/KEDA; add custom health probes
Infra SRE Tune circuit-breaker thresholds, ejection parameters, queue bounds; on-call dashboards

1.6 Phase-0 Deliverable — Crate Scaffold

Numbered list of crates produced in Phase 0 (weeks 1–2):

  1. infr-core — core types: RequestId, Request, BackendId, ModelId, AdapterId, Replica, LoadState, RoutingDecision, Slo, Priority, Lease, InferError.
  2. infr-ingress — front-end listener: HTTP/1.1, HTTP/2, gRPC; request parse, streaming (SSE/chunked) token relay, graceful drain.
  3. infr-registry — backend registry: replica set, model→replica map, adapter inventory, capacity descriptors, config reload.
  4. infr-health — health + load probing: heartbeats, queue-depth/KV-util scraping, EWMA load, ejection state machine.
  5. infr-route — routing policy engine: round-robin/weighted/least-load, prefix-cache-aware (PrefixTree), adapter-aware, session affinity, consistent hashing.
  6. infr-queue — admission control + queueing: per-model bounded queues, priority, SLO deadlines, shedding, backpressure.
  7. infr-dispatch — backend connection pool: multiplexed HTTP/2, request forwarding, streaming relay, retries, hedging, timeouts, circuit breakers.
  8. infr-sched — global scheduler: fairness/quotas, autoscale-signal emission, disaggregated prefill/decode coordination, batching hints.
  9. infr-telemetry — metrics (per-route latency, queue depth, cache-hit rate, TTFT/TPOT), tracing, structured logs.
  10. infr-cli — driver/config.
  11. infr-bench — load + chaos harness.

1.7 Phase Unlock Map

Phase What unlocks
0 Crate scaffold, workspace, CI, core types, config schema
1 infr-ingress: HTTP/1.1+2 parse; SSE relay skeleton
2 infr-registry + infr-health: replica set, health polling, EWMA
3 infr-route: least-load + round-robin; infr-dispatch: conn pool + forwarding
4 infr-queue: admission, bounded queues, priority, SLO shedding
5 infr-route: PrefixTree (prefix-cache-aware routing)
6 infr-dispatch: retries, hedging, timeouts, circuit breakers
7 infr-sched: fairness, quotas, autoscale signals
8 infr-sched: disaggregated prefill/decode coordination
9 infr-ingress: gRPC; adapter-aware routing; LoRA dispatch
10 infr-telemetry: full metrics, tracing, structured logs
11 infr-bench: load harness, chaos injection, SLO regression suite
12 Hardening: NUMA affinity, large-fleet tuning, production runbook

1.9 Timeline

  • Phase 0 (scaffold + CI): weeks 1–2.
  • Phases 1–6 (full request path, basic resilience): weeks 3–14 (~6–14 weeks net).
  • Phases 7–12 (scheduler, disaggregation, bench, hardening): months 4–9.
  • Full production-ready implementation: ~9 months from Phase 0.

1.10 Language & Rationale

Criterion Rust decision
Async networking throughput tokio multi-thread runtime; zero-copy buffers via bytes::Bytes
Tail-latency predictability No GC pauses; deterministic drop; arena-backed pools
Memory safety under high concurrency Ownership + borrow checker; no data races at compile time
HTTP/2 + gRPC hyper 1.x, h2, tonic 0.11 — mature, zero-copy
Connection pool / proxy patterns Proven: linkerd2-proxy, pingora are Rust; same design space
Rejected: Go GC stop-the-world spikes at p99; goroutine scheduler adds latency variance
Rejected: C++ Unsafe memory; build complexity; no async ecosystem parity

1.11 Prerequisites

  • Systems: OS scheduling, epoll/kqueue, TCP/TLS, HTTP/2 framing, gRPC protocol.
  • Concurrency: async/await, tokio runtime internals, lock-free data structures, MPSC/broadcast channels.
  • Distributed systems: failure detection, circuit breakers, consistent hashing, admission control theory.
  • ML-systems: LLM inference serving (continuous batching, KV cache, prefix/radix cache, LoRA adapters, prefill/decode disaggregation).
  • Networking: backpressure, flow control, HTTP/2 streams, SSE framing, connection pooling.
  • Observability: Prometheus metrics, OpenTelemetry tracing, structured logging.

2. Why Build This

2.1 Historical + Architectural Significance

Era Advance
2022 Orca (Yu et al.) — continuous batching shows iteration-level scheduling > static batching; request multiplexing becomes the bottleneck
2023 vLLM/PagedAttention (Kwon et al.) — KV cache fragmentation solved; now routing must be KV-cache aware to avoid re-computation
2024 SGLang RadixAttention (Zheng et al.) — prefix-cache reuse across requests; router must track per-backend prefix trees
2024 DistServe/Splitwise — disaggregated prefill/decode changes the fleet topology; router must coordinate two-phase dispatch
2024 Mooncake — KV-cache migration between nodes; router must expose hints and observe transfer completion
  • The router is the new bottleneck: naive round-robin wastes 60–80% of prefix-cache hits (SGLang paper).
  • Production stacks (vLLM production-stack, AIBrix, Ray Serve) all converged on a dedicated routing layer — this project builds that layer from scratch.

2.2 Unique Systems Knowledge Taught

  • Prefix/radix cache routing: building a PrefixTree (radix tree) from token hashes; longest-prefix match across a replica set; cache eviction tracking.
  • KV-utilization-aware scheduling: EWMA of kv_util as a signal; admission gating on utilization thresholds; interaction with continuous batching.
  • Disaggregated prefill/decode: two-phase dispatch — route prefill request to a prefill replica, then route decode continuation to a decode replica; coordination via Lease.
  • Streaming relay correctness: byte-order preservation, backpressure propagation across HTTP/2 flow control windows, half-close semantics.
  • Admission control under SLO: priority queues with deadline-aware shedding; Little's Law applied to queue sizing; token-bucket vs leaky-bucket tradeoffs.
  • Circuit breaker state machine: closed → open → half-open transitions; per-backend failure tracking; slow-start after recovery.
  • Hedging under idempotency constraint: hedge only when Request.idempotent == true; cancel trailing hedge on first complete response.
  • EWMA load estimation: configurable α; convergence time vs noise tradeoff; reset on ejection.

2.3 Engineering Muscles Developed

  • Designing async data pipelines with explicit backpressure (tokio channels + H2 flow control).
  • Writing lock-free or fine-grained-locking concurrent data structures (registry, PrefixTree updates).
  • Instrumenting a multi-stage pipeline for p99 latency attribution (where is the tail?).
  • Config hot-reload without downtime: SIGHUP → atomic Arc<Config> swap.
  • Failure-injection testing: chaos harness simulates backend latency spikes, OOM, CUDA errors.
  • Capacity planning: model queue depth vs TTFT SLO vs fleet size.

2.4 Production Engineering Concepts Exercised

Concept Where exercised
Outlier ejection infr-health: consecutive failure counting → Ejected state
Retry budget infr-dispatch: max_retries per request; global retry-budget counter
Backpressure propagation infr-ingress ← infr-queue ← infr-dispatch ← backend H2 flow control
Work-conserving scheduling infr-sched: never idle a backend while queue is non-empty
SLO budgets + error budgets infr-queue: deadline-aware shed; infr-telemetry: SLO breach rate
Graceful drain infr-ingress: SIGTERM → stop accepting → drain inflight → exit
Autoscale signal emission infr-sched: publishes scale_up/scale_down events for HPA/KEDA
Zero-downtime config reload SIGHUP → registry hot-swap; no request drop

2.5 Cross-Links to Portfolio Projects

Related project Shared concept
Compiler / IR optimizers PrefixTree data structure; SSA-style IR shares radix-tree intuitions
Distributed KV store / consensus Replica failure detection; lease semantics; at-most-once delivery
Scheduler (OS/job) Priority queues; SLO deadlines; work-conserving fairness
Networking stack / HTTP proxy HTTP/2 framing; connection pool; backpressure; circuit breakers
Vector DB / ANN index Approximate prefix lookup; cache-hit estimation
GPU/tensor runtime KV cache topology; continuous batching interaction; prefill/decode split
Storage engine Bounded queue invariant; write-ahead of admission decisions
Service mesh (Envoy/linkerd) Outlier detection; retry policy; health-check probing

2.6 Calibration Value

  • Demonstrates mastery of every layer of a production serving stack: protocol → admission → routing → dispatch → observability.
  • Forces principled tradeoffs with citations: prefix-cache hit rate vs load balance vs tail latency.
  • Produces a system reviewers can interrogate end-to-end: given a request trace, explain every routing decision and its RouteReason.
  • Distinguishes senior engineers (correct async streaming relay, bounded memory, EWMA correctness) from junior (stateless round-robin, unbounded queues).

2.7 What Mastery Looks Like

  • Protocol-to-policy fluency (Phase 13 / §25): Given a captured request trace, name the routing policy applied, the RouteReason enum variant, which P# governed the fallback, and whether the SLO deadline was met before dispatch — without consulting code.
  • Invariant-driven debugging (Phase 3 / §7): When inflight_gauge drifts above actual dispatched count, immediately identify it as Lease invariant (I5) violation, isolate the panic path that bypassed Lease::drop, and write the RAII fix — not after reading the code but from the invariant table alone.
  • Capacity arithmetic on demand (Phase 0 / §4): Estimate router RSS at 10k inflight and 500 backends from first principles — Lease size + registry size + PrefixTree entries — and verify against System Constraints numbers within 20%.
  • Resilience design tradeoffs (Phase 8 / §20.6): Explain why hedging is gated on idempotent: bool (at-most-once invariant I3), under what SLO regime hedging becomes net negative (high baseline load → 2× backend load worsens p99), and how circuit-breaker trip thresholds should differ between prefill and decode pools.
  • Streaming relay correctness (Phase 7 / §3.7): Write the relay invariant assertion, identify the H2 flow-control release-capacity bug pattern (pitfall §19.6 item 1) from a symptom description alone, and propose the fix without looking at the file.
  • Scalability reasoning (Scalability Analysis / Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model): Given a fleet of 1,000 backends and a target of 50k req/s, derive the PrefixTree read budget, admission-queue lock contention ceiling, and the breakeven point at which model sharding is required — arithmetic shown.
  • Architecture comparison (Comparative Architecture Table): Compare this design's approach to prefix-cache routing against vLLM production-stack and Ray Serve on three load-bearing dimensions (state model, routing unit, scale-out mechanism) and articulate the exact regime where each alternative would outperform.
  • Failure-mode synthesis (Failure Modes & Recovery / §8.5): Trace a KV-cache eviction storm through the full system: which metric fires first (kv_util_gauge), what infr-route does next (fallback from PrefixHit to LeastLoad), why TTFT spikes anyway (cold-fill cost), what autoscale signal is emitted, and the minimum time to recovery given cold-start probe window.

3. Architectural Overview

3.1 Canonical Architecture Diagram

    clients (HTTP/1.1, HTTP/2, gRPC, streaming)
            |
      [infr-ingress] parse request; resolve model/adapter; compute prefix_hash
            |  Request
      [infr-queue] admission: SLO deadline, priority, bounded queue -> admit / shed / backpressure   <->   [infr-sched] fairness, autoscale signals
            |  admitted Request
      [infr-route] policy: least-load + prefix-cache-aware (PrefixTree longest match) + adapter-aware   <->   [infr-registry]+[infr-health] (model->replicas, LoadState, PrefixTree)
            |  RoutingDecision (BackendId + reason)
      [infr-dispatch] conn pool -> backend; stream tokens back; retry / hedge / timeout / circuit-break
            |  token stream
        back to client          [infr-telemetry] taps every stage

3.2 infr-ingress

  • Responsibilities: accept TCP connections; negotiate HTTP/1.1 upgrade or HTTP/2 ALPN; parse OpenAI-compatible /v1/chat/completions and /v1/completions JSON bodies; resolve model → ModelId, optional adapter → AdapterId; compute prefix_hash (SHA-256 of canonicalized prompt tokens); emit a Request struct; relay SSE (data: <token>\n\n) or chunked transfer back to client byte-for-byte preserving order; execute graceful drain on SIGTERM.
  • Concurrency boundary: one tokio::task per client stream; H2 multiplexes N streams over one TCP connection; backpressure via H2 flow-control windows propagates to upstream client.
  • Protocol boundary: external (client-facing) vs internal (infr-queue channel); no backend protocol here.
  • Failure modes: malformed JSON → 400; unknown model → 404; upstream queue full → 429 with Retry-After; graceful drain timeout → 503.

3.3 infr-queue

  • Responsibilities: per-ModelId bounded VecDeque<Lease>; admit requests by priority (Priority enum: high/normal/low/batch); enforce SLO deadline (Slo.deadline) — shed if wall-clock would exceed deadline before dispatch; emit backpressure signal to infr-ingress when queue at high-water mark; interact with infr-sched for fairness quotas.
  • Async queue/channel: tokio mpsc channel from ingress → queue; semaphore-based bounded admission; tokio::time::sleep_until(deadline) for SLO expiry.
  • Bounded-queue invariant: queue capacity = max_queue_depth per model (config); on saturation, shed lowest-priority first (oldest-deadline-first within same priority); never allocate unbounded.
  • Concurrency boundary: queue actor runs in its own tokio task; registry/health read is read-only shared Arc<RwLock<RegistryState>>.
  • Failure modes: queue full + high-priority request → shed lowest priority to make room; all priorities full → 503; SLO deadline already expired on dequeue → drop + increment requests_expired_total.

3.4 infr-sched

  • Responsibilities: observe queue depths across all models; emit autoscale signals (ScaleSignal { model, direction: Up|Down, reason }) to a broadcast channel consumed by an external HPA/KEDA bridge; enforce per-model and per-tenant quota (token/minute, request/minute); coordinate disaggregated prefill/decode: on prefill completion, emit a decode-continuation Request to the decode-replica queue with affinity to the correct decode backend.
  • Disaggregated prefill/decode: prefill replicas handle prompt evaluation; decode replicas handle token generation; sched tracks PrefillLease → DecodeLease handoff; invariant: decode continuation is only dispatched after prefill KV-cache is confirmed transferred.
  • Async queue/channel: sched subscribes to infr-health's LoadEvent broadcast; publishes to autoscale_tx: broadcast::Sender<ScaleSignal>.
  • Failure modes: prefill backend fails mid-transfer → abort decode lease; emit InferError::PrefillFailed; retry policy configurable.

3.5 infr-registry + infr-health

  • infr-registry responsibilities: authoritative map ModelId → Vec<Replica>; adapter inventory AdapterId → Vec<BackendId>; capacity descriptors (GPU memory, max_batch); config reload (SIGHUP → atomic Arc swap of RegistryState); exposes read-only handle to infr-route.
  • infr-health responsibilities: periodic heartbeat probes (HTTP GET /health); scrape queue-depth + kv-util from backend metrics endpoints; compute EWMA of ttft_ms per replica; run ejection state machine (Healthy → Degraded → Ejected); publish LoadEvent to broadcast channel; update LoadState in RegistryState via write lock.
  • Ejection state machine: consecutive_failures >= threshold → Ejected; periodic re-probe in Ejected state; re-admit to Degraded on first success; Degraded → Healthy after recovery_probes successes.
  • Health invariant: infr-route reads only replicas with Health::Healthy | Health::Degraded (Degraded used only when no Healthy alternative); Health::Ejected replicas are never returned by route().
  • Persistence boundary: no persistent storage; state is in-memory; re-bootstraps from config on restart.

3.6 infr-route

  • Responsibilities: given admitted Request + current RegistryState, select BackendId and record RouteReason; policy chain (in order): 1) filter by ModelId + Health; 2) filter by AdapterId if request.adapter.is_some(); 3) PrefixTree longest-prefix match on prefix_hash (prefix-cache-aware); 4) least-load on kv_util * w_kv + ewma_ttft * w_ttft + queue_depth * w_q; 5) consistent hashing for session affinity; 6) fallback: lowest queue_depth.
  • PrefixTree: radix tree keyed by token-prefix byte sequences; leaf = BTreeSet<BackendId> with recency timestamp; updated by infr-health when backends report cache hits; eviction: LRU per prefix entry; thread-safe via RwLock<PrefixTree> (write only on health events, read on every route call — low contention).
  • Decision auditability: every call returns RoutingDecision { backend: BackendId, reason: RouteReason } — RouteReason ∈ {PrefixHit, LeastLoad, AdapterResident, Affinity, Fallback}; logged and counted in infr-telemetry.
  • Failure modes: no healthy replica for model → InferError::NoHealthyReplica; prefix match found but replica now Ejected → fall through to least-load; all replicas at max kv_util → InferError::Overloaded → shed at queue.

3.7 infr-dispatch

  • Responsibilities: maintain per-BackendId HTTP/2 connection pool (hyper client, max pool_size streams); forward Request to chosen BackendId as HTTP POST; stream response tokens back through a tokio::sync::mpsc::Sender<Bytes>; implement retry (on retriable errors + request.idempotent), hedging (second replica after hedge_delay_ms if idempotent), timeout (Slo.ttft_ms for first token, Slo.tpot_ms per token), circuit breaker per backend.
  • Circuit breaker: per-backend AtomicU32 failure counter; >= cb_threshold → Open; Open → reject immediately with InferError::CircuitOpen; half-open probe after cb_reset_ms.
  • At-most-once invariant: non-idempotent requests sent to exactly one backend; hedging disabled; retry only on connection errors before first byte sent.
  • Order invariant: SSE/chunked bytes forwarded in arrival order; no reordering in relay buffer.
  • Lease invariant: Lease acquired before dispatch, released (and backend accounting decremented) on completion, error, or timeout.
  • Failure modes: backend 5xx → retry if budget; backend timeout → retry or hedge; all retries exhausted → InferError::BackendExhausted; circuit open → InferError::CircuitOpen → queue may re-route to alternate backend.

3.8 infr-telemetry

  • Responsibilities: tap every stage via tracing::Span and metrics crate (Prometheus-compatible); emit per-route histograms for TTFT, TPOT, queue wait, dispatch latency; counters for cache-hit/miss, shed requests, retry count, circuit-open events; gauges for queue depth, kv_util per backend, inflight requests; export via /metrics HTTP endpoint (Prometheus scrape); structured JSON logs via tracing-subscriber; OpenTelemetry trace export (OTLP/gRPC).
  • Instrumentation points: ingress parse, queue admit/shed, route decision (+ RouteReason), dispatch start/first-byte/last-byte, retry, hedge, circuit-breaker transition.
  • No side effects on hot path: all metric updates are atomic increments or histogram observations; tracing spans are sampled; never blocks the routing hot path.

3.9 Data Flow Summary

Request path (per request):
  1. TCP accept → HTTP parse → Request{id, model, adapter, prefix_hash, slo, priority} (infr-ingress)
  2. mpsc send → infr-queue: priority enqueue; SLO deadline check
  3. sched signal: quota gate; disaggregated-prefill routing hint
  4. dequeue → infr-route: PrefixTree match + load score → RoutingDecision
  5. Lease acquire (infr-core) → infr-dispatch: H2 forward → backend
  6. Token stream: backend → dispatch relay buffer → ingress SSE writer → client
  7. Lease release; telemetry record TTFT/TPOT/route-reason

Control path (continuous background tasks):
  - infr-health: probe loop (every health_interval_ms) → update LoadState + PrefixTree → broadcast LoadEvent
  - infr-sched: observe LoadEvents → emit ScaleSignal → external HPA
  - infr-registry: SIGHUP → reload config → Arc::swap(RegistryState)

3.10 Trust + Persistence Boundaries

Boundary Description
External trust Clients are untrusted; request size limits enforced at infr-ingress
Internal trust Backend replicas are trusted (same cluster); no mTLS required by default (hook exists)
Persistence None: all state in-memory; restart = re-bootstrap from config + health probes
Cross-router No shared state between router pods; each pod has independent registry view
Auth hook AuthzFilter trait in infr-ingress; no-op default; pluggable for API-key checks

4. System Constraints

4.1 Constraints Table

Dimension Target Notes
Router process RSS ≤ 512 MB steady-state 10K inflight + 1K backends + PrefixTree; OOM kills are SLA breaches
Max concurrent requests (inflight) 10,000 per router pod tokio task per stream; fd budget 65,535 (ulimit -n); H2 stream multiplexing
Ingress throughput ≥ 50,000 req/s for short prompts (non-streaming) Benchmarked via infr-bench wrk2 harness
Routing hot-path latency (p50/p95/p99) p50 ≤ 0.2 ms / p95 ≤ 0.5 ms / p99 ≤ 1.0 ms Time from dequeue to dispatch (route + conn-pool lookup); excludes backend latency
TTFT added overhead (router contribution) ≤ 2 ms at p99 Router must not be TTFT bottleneck for typical 1–10 s backend TTFT
Queue wait budget ≤ 5% of Slo.ttft_ms Shedding triggers before queue wait exceeds this fraction
Backend connection pool size configurable; default 32 H2 streams/backend Tune per backend GPU concurrency limit
Health probe interval configurable; default 200 ms; min 50 ms Lower bound: avoid probe storms on large fleets
PrefixTree update latency ≤ 5 ms per update (RwLock write) Hash prefix → radix insert; amortized O(prefix_len)
Config reload (SIGHUP) ≤ 100 ms to new config active Arc::swap; no request drops
Startup time ≤ 5 s cold start (config load + initial health probes) Readiness probe must not fail during initial probe window
Graceful shutdown (SIGTERM) drain in ≤ 30 s; hard kill after Stop accepting; drain inflight; close backend connections
CPU cores assumed ≥ 4 physical cores; NUMA-unaware by default NUMA pinning deferred to Phase 12
Network RTT (router ↔ backend) ≤ 1 ms intra-DC; ≤ 5 ms cross-AZ Cross-AZ routing penalized in load score
File descriptor budget 65,535 (soft); 524,288 (hard) Each backend conn + each client conn; monitor fd_usage gauge
Availability target 99.9% (three nines) monthly ≤ 43.8 min downtime/month; single router pod; multi-pod deployment → 99.99%
RTO ≤ 60 s (pod restart + re-probe) K8s liveness probe + readiness gates fleet re-admission
Failure budget (error rate) ≤ 0.1% of requests return 5xx attributable to router Excludes backend errors transparently relayed
Max backend fleet size 1,000 replicas per router pod Registry Vec<Replica> fits in L3 cache; PrefixTree bounded by max_prefix_entries
Queue depth per model configurable; default 1,000 requests Bounded-queue invariant; overflow → shed lowest priority
Retry budget ≤ 3 retries per request; global retry-budget 10% of inflight Prevents retry amplification storms
Hedging budget ≤ 5% of inflight requests hedged simultaneously Only idempotent; configurable hedge_fraction
Memory per request (Lease + metadata) ≤ 2 KB RequestId(16B ULID) + Request(~128B) + Lease(~64B) + routing overhead
IO amplification 1× read, 0× write (no storage path) Router is a pure network relay; no disk I/O on request path

4.2 Scaling Assumptions

  • Horizontal: linear scale-out; each router pod is independent; no coordination protocol.
  • Vertical: memory-bound at ~50K inflight (512 MB); CPU-bound at ~200K req/s on 16-core (profiled async task overhead).
  • Backend fleet growth: O(N) registry scan on route is acceptable up to N=1,000; PrefixTree match is O(prefix_len), independent of fleet size.
  • Model count: ≤ 100 distinct ModelId per router; per-model queue and PrefixTree namespace.
  • Adapter count: ≤ 1,000 AdapterId per model; adapter inventory fits in HashMap in L3 cache.

4.3 Failure Budget Allocation

Failure category Allowed rate
Router-induced 5xx (routing bug, panic) ≤ 0.01% of requests
Shed (429) due to queue saturation ≤ 1% of requests at target load
Retry-induced duplicate tokens (idempotent) 0 (at-most-once for non-idempotent)
SLO breach (TTFT > slo.ttft_ms) ≤ 5% of requests (p95 SLO target)
Circuit-breaker false-positive ejection ≤ 1 event per 24 h per backend

4.4 Observability Baseline (must be present before Phase 6)

Metric Type Labels
router_request_total Counter model, status, route_reason
router_ttft_seconds Histogram model, backend
router_tpot_seconds Histogram model, backend
router_queue_depth Gauge model, priority
router_kv_util Gauge backend
router_cache_hit_total Counter model
router_shed_total Counter model, reason
router_retry_total Counter backend, error_type
router_circuit_breaker_state Gauge (0/1/2) backend
router_inflight_requests Gauge —
