6. Design Principles

6.1 Backpressure Is Propagated, Never Swallowed

  • Statement: every queue, pool, and channel is bounded; when full, upstream callers block or receive explicit rejection — never silent discard.
  • Tradeoff: bounded channels can cause cascading pauses up to the client TCP write buffer; this is correct — client experiences slowdown rather than silent data loss.
  • Operational implication: infr-queue saturation → 429 with Retry-After; infr-dispatch pool exhaustion → 503; both emit queue_depth_max_hit counter.
  • Violation consequence: unbounded channels + async tasks → runaway heap allocation → OOM kill of router process; all in-flight requests lost; cascading failure to clients.
  • Enforcement: all tokio::sync::mpsc channels created with explicit capacity parameter; Semaphore guards dispatch slots.

6.2 At-Most-Once Dispatch for Non-Idempotent Requests

  • Statement: a Request with idempotent=false is forwarded to exactly one backend; no retry, no hedge.
  • Tradeoff: increases P99 latency on backend timeout (must wait for full timeout before erroring); correctness beats latency here.
  • Operational implication: operators must set idempotent=true only for side-effect-free routes; infr-dispatch checks flag before retry path.
  • Violation consequence: double-charging / double-generation; user sees duplicate streamed tokens; non-idempotent writes executed twice.
  • Enforcement: Lease struct carries idempotent bit; retry logic hard-gates on it; hedge path returns error if idempotent=false.

6.3 Observability Is a First-Class Invariant, Not an Add-On

  • Statement: every stage emits structured span + counter + histogram before code ships; no "add metrics later."
  • Tradeoff: ~2–5 µs/request overhead from tracing spans; acceptable given TTFT targets in tens of ms.
  • Operational implication: on-call can reconstruct routing decision, queue wait, backend latency, and token stream timing from a single request_id; RouteReason logged on every decision.
  • Violation consequence: production incident requires log archaeology; cache-hit / miss routing bugs invisible; SLO breach root cause unknown.
  • Enforcement: infr-telemetry provides a macro observe_stage!(stage, req_id, ...) — stages call it unconditionally; linting rule rejects infr-* functions lacking span instrument.

6.4 Health State Drives All Routing; No Stale State

  • Statement: infr-route reads replica Health and LoadState only from a consistent view published by infr-health; never from stale in-process snapshots older than probe_interval × 2.
  • Tradeoff: frequent probing (default 1 s heartbeat) increases backend HTTP overhead; tolerable vs. routing to a broken backend.
  • Operational implication: infr-health EWMA decay constant tunable; fast spike detection vs. false ejection tradeoff exposed as config.
  • Violation consequence: routing to Ejected backend; request fails at dispatch layer; latency spike + retry storm risk.
  • Enforcement: Replica.health field is Arc<AtomicU8> written only by infr-health probe loop; infr-route reads atomically with Acquire ordering.

6.5 Decision Auditability — Every Route Has a Reason

  • Statement: RoutingDecision always carries a RouteReason variant; no silent default routing.
  • Tradeoff: small enum serialization cost; worth it for debuggability.
  • Operational implication: reason=fallback rate > threshold triggers alert ("policy misconfiguration?"); reason=prefix_hit rate feeds cache-hit dashboard.
  • Violation consequence: impossible to distinguish cache-hit routing from fallback load-balance routing; cache warm-up effectiveness invisible.
  • Enforcement: RouteReason is non-optional in RoutingDecision; infr-route has no code path that produces a RoutingDecision without setting reason.

6.6 Bounded Resource Usage Under All Load Conditions

  • Statement: router memory, fd count, and goroutine/task count are bounded by config, not by request arrival rate.
  • Tradeoff: requests are shed (429/503) rather than queued indefinitely; correct for an LLM router where stale queued requests violate TTFT SLO anyway.
  • Operational implication: max_queue_depth, max_connections_per_backend, max_inflight all tunable; defaults chosen conservatively.
  • Violation consequence: queue depth unbounded → heap growth → OOM → process restart → all in-flight lost → client retry storm → full outage.
  • Enforcement: infr-queue uses ArrayDeque<Lease, N> (fixed N); infr-dispatch pool uses Semaphore(max_connections_per_backend).

6.7 Prefix-Cache Affinity Is a Hint, Not a Hard Constraint

  • Statement: infr-route prefers the backend with longest PrefixTree match, but falls back to least-load if that backend is Degraded / overloaded (kv_util > threshold).
  • Tradeoff: sacrifices cache hit → compute re-prefill cost; buys latency predictability and avoids routing pile-up on one hot backend.
  • Operational implication: prefix_cache_override_threshold config knob; when crossed, reason=fallback overrides reason=prefix_hit.
  • Violation consequence: hard affinity → single hot backend under skewed prefix distribution → cascading overload; other backends idle.
  • Enforcement: PrefixTree::match_with_load_check(prefix_hash, load_states) -> (BackendId, RouteReason) always returns a valid backend.

6.8 SLO Deadlines Govern Admission, Queueing, and Shedding

  • Statement: every admitted Request carries a Slo { ttft_ms, tpot_ms, deadline }; if the request cannot be dispatched before deadline - safety_margin_ms, it is shed with 503.
  • Tradeoff: early shedding wastes no backend compute; client can retry immediately with exponential backoff.
  • Operational implication: slo_shed_rate counter; if high, autoscaling signal should fire; infr-sched reads this counter.
  • Violation consequence: requests dispatched past SLO deadline → backend still processes → compute wasted → GPU quota consumed but client already timed out.
  • Enforcement: infr-queue timer loop checks Instant::now() > lease.deadline - margin on dequeue; sheds immediately.

6.9 Crash-Only Design — No Graceful Degradation via Complex State Repair

  • Statement: on unrecoverable state corruption (PrefixTree invariant violated, Lease double-release), the affected subsystem panics and lets the supervisor restart it; no in-place repair.
  • Tradeoff: brief restart disruption; preferred over subtle correctness bugs from partial state repair.
  • Operational implication: infr-health detect-and-eject covers transient errors; panic is for invariant violations only (programming errors).
  • Violation consequence: attempted in-place repair of corrupt routing table → silent mis-routing → wrong backend for model/adapter → runtime error at backend.
  • Enforcement: debug_assert! for invariants in hot paths; assert! (always-on) for safety invariants; no unwrap() on external data.

6.10 Streaming Relay Is Transparent — No Re-Buffering

  • Statement: infr-dispatch relays backend token stream to client byte-for-byte, in order, without accumulating the full response.
  • Tradeoff: cannot rewrite/enrich streamed tokens mid-stream; response-level metadata (usage counts) must come via trailer or non-streaming path.
  • Operational implication: TTFT measured at first byte forwarded from backend; TPOT measured per-chunk interval; relay must not introduce buffering latency.
  • Violation consequence: full-response buffering → client sees TTFT = total generation time → defeats streaming UX entirely.
  • Enforcement: infr-ingress SSE/chunked relay uses Stream::forward() with Bytes chunks; no collect() on token stream.

6.11 Adapter and Model Co-Location Is a Hard Routing Constraint

  • Statement: a request for (ModelId, AdapterId) is dispatched only to a Replica whose adapters set contains that AdapterId.
  • Tradeoff: reduces eligible backend set; may require adapter pre-loading signal to autoscaler.
  • Operational implication: infr-registry maintains adapter -> set<BackendId> index; infr-route intersects with load-ranked candidates.
  • Violation consequence: dispatch to backend lacking adapter → backend returns 404/400 → unretryable error surfaced to client.
  • Enforcement: infr-route policy returns InferError::NoEligibleBackend if intersection is empty; never routes to wrong adapter set.

6.12 Config Reload Is Non-Disruptive

  • Statement: infr-registry and infr-queue accept SIGHUP / config-file watch → hot-reload without dropping in-flight requests.
  • Tradeoff: reload must be atomic from routing's perspective; use ArcSwap<Config> not Mutex<Config> to avoid lock contention.
  • Operational implication: reload emits config_reload_ok / config_reload_err counter; old replicas drain before removal.
  • Violation consequence: non-atomic reload → half-old/half-new routing table → requests routed to removed backends → dispatch errors.
  • Enforcement: ArcSwap for all hot-path-read config objects; writer side publishes atomically; old Arc kept alive until all readers drop.

6.13 Autoscaling Signals Are Emitted, Not Acted Upon

  • Statement: infr-sched computes scale-out/scale-in recommendations and emits them as events (metric + optional webhook); it does NOT call K8s API or manage replicas.
  • Tradeoff: decouples router from orchestrator; HPA or custom controller interprets signals; slower reaction time.
  • Operational implication: scale_out_recommended{model=X} gauge; external controller polls or subscribes; documented latency between signal and new replica ready.
  • Violation consequence: router managing replicas directly → tight coupling → router bug kills production fleet.
  • Enforcement: infr-sched has no K8s client dependency; signals are tokio::broadcast::Sender<AutoscaleEvent>.

6.14 Circuit Breakers Trip Per Backend, Not Per Model

  • Statement: circuit breaker state (Closed/Open/HalfOpen) is scoped to BackendId, not ModelId; a broken backend does not mark the model as unhealthy.
  • Tradeoff: finer-grained; correct for fleet deployments where one node fails; model remains routable via other replicas.
  • Operational implication: circuit_breaker_open{backend_id=X} gauge; infr-health ejection state machine is the upstream of circuit state.
  • Violation consequence: per-model circuit trip → all replicas of healthy model shed → full model outage from single backend failure.
  • Enforcement: infr-dispatch CircuitBreaker keyed by BackendId; infr-health ejection drives the Open transition.

6.15 Fairness Is Enforced at the Queue, Not at the Dispatch Layer

  • Statement: per-model/per-tenant quota enforcement happens in infr-queue before dispatch; infr-dispatch sees only admitted requests.
  • Tradeoff: quota bypass via direct dispatch path is structurally impossible; simplifies dispatch code.
  • Operational implication: quota counters in infr-queue; infr-sched reads them for fairness signals; operators tune tokens_per_second per model.
  • Violation consequence: quota enforcement at dispatch → quota bypass race possible; tenant starvation; unpredictable backend load.
  • Enforcement: single admission checkpoint in infr-queue::admit(); all other stages receive already-admitted Lease.

7. System Invariants

7.1 Health Invariant — No Dispatch to Ejected Backend

Attribute Detail
Statement A request is never dispatched to a Replica whose Health == Ejected or whose circuit breaker is Open.
Why it matters Ejected backends are mid-failure; dispatching wastes latency budget and may cause duplicate side effects on retry.
Where enforced infr-route filters candidates: `replicas.iter().filter(
Violation detection dispatch_to_ejected_backend counter > 0 fires alert; detected in infr-dispatch pre-check.
Telemetry backend_health_state{backend_id, state} gauge; dispatch_ejected_attempt_total counter.
Recovery infr-dispatch returns InferError::BackendUnavailable; infr-route re-runs selection excluding that backend.
Debug methodology Check backend_health_state timeline; correlate with circuit_breaker_open events; inspect infr-health probe logs for ejection trigger.

7.2 Order Invariant — Token Stream Byte Order Preserved

Attribute Detail
Statement Bytes relayed from backend to client for a single request appear in exactly the order produced by the backend; no reordering, no insertion.
Why it matters SSE token stream is parsed by client as sequential tokens; reorder → garbled text; insertion → corrupted JSON.
Where enforced infr-dispatch relay loop: single AsyncRead → single AsyncWrite; no concurrent chunk writers per request.
Violation detection Integration test: inject sequence-tagged chunks; assert client-side arrival order matches.
Telemetry Chunk sequence numbers in debug tracing span (off by default, enabled via INFR_DEBUG_STREAM=1).
Recovery Not recoverable mid-stream; stream error surfaced to client; client must retry from scratch.
Debug methodology Enable INFR_DEBUG_STREAM; capture span with chunk offsets; diff against backend-side log.

7.3 At-Most-Once Invariant — Non-Idempotent Requests Reach One Backend

Attribute Detail
Statement For Request.idempotent == false, the request body is forwarded to at most one backend, regardless of timeout, retry, or hedging configuration.
Why it matters LLM generation may have side effects (billing events, stateful sessions); double-dispatch = double charge or duplicate output.
Where enforced infr-dispatch retry gate: if !lease.request.idempotent { return Err(InferError::NonIdempotentTimeout) }. Hedge path: same gate.
Violation detection duplicate_dispatch_attempt_total counter; set in infr-dispatch before the gate; should always be zero.
Telemetry idempotent_retry_total, nonidempotent_shed_on_timeout_total.
Recovery Return error to client; client decides to retry (with new RequestId).
Debug methodology Search traces for same RequestId appearing in two different backend_id dispatch spans.

7.4 Bounded-Queue Invariant — No Unbounded Memory Growth

Attribute Detail
Statement The total number of Lease objects simultaneously alive is bounded by max_queue_depth + max_inflight_per_backend × num_backends; heap usage remains stable under sustained overload.
Why it matters Unbounded queue → OOM → process crash → all in-flight requests lost.
Where enforced infr-queue uses fixed-capacity queue; admit() returns Err(QueueFull) when at capacity. infr-dispatch semaphore bounds inflight.
Violation detection Heap profiler (heaptrack / jemalloc stats); queue_depth{model} gauge; alert at 90% of max_queue_depth.
Telemetry queue_depth{model}, queue_shed_total{model, reason}, inflight_requests{backend}.
Recovery Shed new requests with 429; existing queue drains naturally; no action needed beyond shedding.
Debug methodology queue_depth plateau at max → shedding; check queue_shed_total reason breakdown; verify max_queue_depth config matches capacity intent.

7.5 Lease Invariant — One Lease Per Admitted Request, Released Exactly Once

Attribute Detail
Statement Every admitted Request holds exactly one Lease; the Lease is released exactly once on completion, timeout, or cancellation; double-release and non-release are both bugs.
Why it matters Lease tracks backend accounting (inflight count, SLO deadline); double-release → negative inflight count → over-admission; non-release → ghost inflight → under-admission.
Where enforced Lease implements Drop which calls infr-queue::release(); release() is idempotent via AtomicBool::compare_exchange.
Violation detection lease_double_release_total counter > 0 → programming error alert. inflight_requests negative → impossible with u32, panics in debug build.
Telemetry lease_active_total, lease_release_total, lease_timeout_total.
Recovery Double-release: log + ignore (idempotent); non-release: lease TTL in infr-queue timer loop forcibly releases after slo.deadline + grace_ms.
Debug methodology Match lease_active_total vs lease_release_total + lease_timeout_total; gap = leaked leases; trace by request_id.

7.6 PrefixTree Consistency — Routing Reads Snapshot, Writers Serialized

Attribute Detail
Statement Routing reads of PrefixTree always see a consistent snapshot; partial-write states are never visible to the route selection path.
Why it matters Partial write → wrong backend set for a prefix → request routed to backend without KV cache → cache miss despite reason=prefix_hit in log → correctness confusion.
Where enforced PrefixTree internal lock: RwLock<Tree>; routing uses read() guard; updates (insert/evict) use write() guard.
Violation detection prefix_tree_stale_hit_total counter: dispatch returns cache miss but RouteReason==prefix_hit; incremented in infr-dispatch response inspection.
Telemetry prefix_cache_hit_total, prefix_cache_miss_total, prefix_tree_stale_hit_total.
Recovery Evict stale entry; infr-health notifies infr-route when backend evicts a cached prefix (via load probe response).
Debug methodology Compare prefix_cache_hit_total with actual backend cache-hit metric; large divergence → stale tree entries.

7.7 Adapter Eligibility Invariant — Request Dispatched Only to Adapter-Resident Backend

Attribute Detail
Statement For Request.adapter = Some(id), dispatch target's Replica.adapters.contains(id) is always true at time of dispatch.
Why it matters Backend returns 400/404 if adapter not loaded; request fails unretryably (non-idempotent path has no retry).
Where enforced infr-route eligibility filter: replica.adapters.contains(adapter_id); infr-registry marks adapter loading state.
Violation detection dispatch_wrong_adapter_total counter; detected via backend 4xx response with adapter error code.
Telemetry adapter_eligible_backend_count{adapter}, dispatch_wrong_adapter_total.
Recovery infr-registry receives adapter-loaded webhook from backend; updates Replica.adapters; future requests route correctly.
Debug methodology adapter_eligible_backend_count{adapter=X} == 0 while requests queued → adapter not loaded on any replica → autoscale signal needed.

7.8 Graceful Drain Invariant — In-Flight Requests Complete Before Shutdown

Attribute Detail
Statement On SIGTERM, infr-ingress stops accepting new connections; all in-flight requests are allowed to complete or timeout before process exits.
Why it matters Abrupt shutdown mid-stream → client receives truncated SSE → garbled partial response.
Where enforced infr-ingress drain future: drain_signal.notified() → set accepting = false → inflight_semaphore.acquire_all() → exit.
Violation detection graceful_drain_timeout_total counter; set when drain exceeds drain_timeout_ms.
Telemetry drain_inflight_remaining, drain_duration_ms.
Recovery After drain_timeout_ms, force-close remaining connections; log request_id list for operator.
Debug methodology Slow drain → check drain_inflight_remaining gauge; identify stuck backends via inflight_requests{backend}.

7.9 Config Atomic-Reload Invariant — No Partial-Config Routing State

Attribute Detail
Statement Between old and new config versions, routing table is never in a half-applied state; readers see either old-complete or new-complete config.
Why it matters Partial reload → some requests route to old backends, some to new → inconsistent adapter/model mapping → failures.
Where enforced ArcSwap<RegistrySnapshot> swap is a single atomic pointer store; readers see old or new, never intermediate.
Violation detection config_version{loaded} counter; compare across replicated router instances — should agree within probe_interval.
Telemetry config_reload_total{result}, config_version.
Recovery On reload parse error: keep old config; emit config_reload_err; alert on-call.
Debug methodology config_version mismatch across router instances → load balancer routing to routers with different configs; check reload logs.

7.10 Circuit Breaker State Machine Invariant — Transitions Are Monotone Within Window

Attribute Detail
Statement Circuit breaker for a backend transitions Closed → Open only when error threshold exceeded; Open → HalfOpen only after base_ejection_time; HalfOpen → Closed only on probe success; no backward skip.
Why it matters Out-of-order transition → premature re-admission → retry storm on still-broken backend.
Where enforced infr-dispatch CircuitBreaker state machine with tokio::time::sleep for ejection timer; state field is AtomicU8 with CAS transitions.
Violation detection circuit_breaker_invalid_transition_total counter; CAS failure = concurrent transition attempt, logged.
Telemetry circuit_breaker_state{backend, state}, circuit_breaker_open_duration_ms.
Recovery CAS failure: loser retries transition; no state corruption possible (CAS atomicity).
Debug methodology circuit_breaker_open_duration_ms histogram; bimodal distribution suggests flapping; check backend error rate timeline.

7.11 SLO Deadline Monotonicity Invariant — Deadline Never Extended

Attribute Detail
Statement Once a Lease.deadline is set at admission, it is never extended; queue wait + dispatch time + generation time must all fit within original slo.deadline.
Why it matters Extension → backend processes request → client already timed out → wasted GPU compute.
Where enforced infr-queue sets Lease.deadline = Instant::now() + slo.deadline; immutable thereafter; no code path calls a deadline setter post-admission.
Violation detection Code review / static analysis: Lease.deadline field has no setter; only constructor sets it.
Telemetry slo_deadline_exceeded_total{stage} (queue, dispatch, generation).
Recovery Expired deadline at any stage → shed with 503 + X-Infr-Shed-Reason: slo_exceeded.
Debug methodology slo_deadline_exceeded_total{stage=queue} high → queue wait too long → increase backends or reduce max_queue_depth; {stage=generation} high → backend too slow for SLO target.

7.12 Disaggregated Prefill/Decode Coordination Invariant

Attribute Detail
Statement When infr-sched enables disaggregated mode for a request, the KV state produced by the prefill backend is transferred to and confirmed by the decode backend before decode starts; no decode dispatch without KV-ready signal.
Why it matters Decode without KV state → backend generates garbage or crashes; KV-transfer failure → silent wrong output.
Where enforced infr-sched disaggregated path: prefill_complete_rx.await before issuing decode dispatch to infr-dispatch.
Violation detection kv_transfer_timeout_total counter; kv_transfer_duration_ms histogram; decode started without prefill confirmation → decode_without_kv_total alert.
Telemetry prefill_duration_ms, kv_transfer_duration_ms, decode_start_delay_ms.
Recovery KV-transfer timeout → abort request; return 503; emit kv_transfer_timeout_total.
Debug methodology kv_transfer_duration_ms p99 > TTFT budget → disaggregated mode too costly for this SLO; consider co-located mode.

8. Domain Model — Distributed Inference Routing & Scheduling

8.1 Entity Taxonomy

Entity Crate Cardinality Lifecycle
RequestId(Ulid) infr-core 1 per client request birth at ingress parse; death at response flush or shed
Request infr-core 1:1 with RequestId immutable after ingress; passed by value through pipeline
BackendId(u32) infr-core 1 per replica process assigned at registry registration; stable until explicit deregister
Replica infr-registry 1 per backend process registered via config/service-discovery; refreshed on health probe
LoadState infr-health 1 per Replica mutable; updated each probe cycle (default 500 ms EWMA window)
RoutingDecision infr-route 1 per admitted Request produced by policy engine; consumed by dispatch
Lease infr-core 1 per in-flight Request held from route assignment until completion/timeout
PrefixTree infr-route 1 per ModelId shared mutable; write-on-completion, read-on-route
Slo infr-core 1 per Request sourced from client header or default per-model config
ModelId infr-core M across registry opaque string; validated against registry on ingress
AdapterId infr-core M per Replica LoRA adapter identifier; registry tracks per-replica inventory

8.2 Core Type Normative Definitions

RequestId  = Ulid  -- monotonic, sortable, URL-safe 26-char

Request {
  id           : RequestId
  model        : ModelId        -- validated against registry
  adapter      : Option<AdapterId>
  prompt_tokens: u32            -- tokenized count; used for admission scoring
  max_tokens   : u32
  prefix_hash  : PrefixHash     -- sha256[..16] of first min(512,N) prompt tokens
  slo          : Slo
  priority     : Priority       -- {Critical, High, Normal, Low, BestEffort}
  idempotent   : bool           -- gates hedging eligibility
  stream       : bool           -- SSE/chunked relay vs buffered response
}

LoadState {
  queue_depth  : u32   -- backend pending request count
  kv_util      : f32   -- [0.0,1.0] KV cache occupancy
  running_batch: u32   -- currently executing sequences
  ewma_ttft_ms : f32   -- EWMA of TTFT over last window
  inflight     : u32   -- dispatched, awaiting first token
}

Slo { ttft_ms: u32, tpot_ms: u32, deadline: Instant }

Replica {
  id      : BackendId
  model   : ModelId
  adapters: HashSet<AdapterId>
  load    : LoadState
  health  : Health   -- {Healthy, Degraded, Ejected}
  addr    : SocketAddr
}

RoutingDecision { backend: BackendId, reason: RouteReason }

RouteReason = least_load | prefix_hit | adapter_resident | affinity | fallback

Lease { request: RequestId, backend: BackendId, deadline: Instant }

PrefixHash = [u8; 16]   -- first 16 bytes of SHA-256 over canonical token sequence

8.3 Request Lifecycle State Machine

States and transitions for a single RequestId:

  [RECEIVED] ──parse ok──► [ADMITTED_PENDING]
       │                          │
  parse fail                 queue full / SLO expired
       │                          │
       ▼                          ▼
   [REJECTED]             [SHED / 503]
                                  
  [ADMITTED_PENDING] ──dequeued──► [ROUTING]
                                      │
                              no healthy replica
                                      │
                                      ▼
                               [ROUTE_FAILED / 503]
                                      
  [ROUTING] ──decision──► [DISPATCHING]
                                │
                           connect fail / pool exhausted
                                │
                                ▼
                          [RETRY] ──retries exhausted──► [FAILED]
                                      
  [DISPATCHING] ──first_token──► [STREAMING]
  [DISPATCHING] ──response_complete──► [COMPLETED]
  [STREAMING]   ──last_token──► [COMPLETED]
  [DISPATCHING/STREAMING] ──backend_error──► [RETRY] or [FAILED]
  [DISPATCHING/STREAMING] ──deadline_exceeded──► [TIMED_OUT]
  [COMPLETED/FAILED/TIMED_OUT/SHED/REJECTED] ──lease released──► [TERMINAL]

State-transition table:

From Event To Side Effects
RECEIVED parse_ok ADMITTED_PENDING enqueue; emit request.received counter
RECEIVED parse_fail REJECTED emit 400; trace span closed
ADMITTED_PENDING dequeue ROUTING acquire Lease; record queue_wait_ms
ADMITTED_PENDING queue_full SHED emit 429 or 503; update shed_total counter
ADMITTED_PENDING slo_deadline_expired SHED emit 504; record slo_miss
ROUTING decision_ok DISPATCHING bind Lease.backend; emit route_reason label
ROUTING no_healthy_replica ROUTE_FAILED emit 503; release Lease
DISPATCHING first_token STREAMING record TTFT; emit ttft_ms histogram
DISPATCHING complete COMPLETED record e2e latency; release Lease; update PrefixTree
DISPATCHING backend_err RETRY circuit-breaker check; if idempotent → hedge
DISPATCHING timeout TIMED_OUT emit 504; release Lease; update ewma on backend
STREAMING last_token COMPLETED record TPOT; flush SSE [DONE]; release Lease
STREAMING backend_disconnect RETRY/FAILED non-idempotent → FAILED; idempotent → RETRY
RETRY retry_ok DISPATCHING select new backend; record retry_count++
RETRY retries_exhausted FAILED emit 502; release Lease

8.4 Replica Health State Machine

  [Healthy] ──consecutive_failures(≥3)──► [Degraded]
  [Healthy] ──kv_util > 0.95──► [Degraded]
  [Degraded] ──probe_success + load_ok──► [Healthy]
  [Degraded] ──consecutive_failures(≥5)──► [Ejected]
  [Ejected] ──slow_start_probe_success──► [Degraded]  (not directly Healthy)
  [Ejected] ──manual_force_healthy──► [Healthy]
Health Routing Eligibility Probe Interval Recovery Path
Healthy Full weight 500 ms —
Degraded Reduced weight (50%) 200 ms 2 consecutive successes → Healthy
Ejected None (invariant: never dispatched) 5 s slow-start probe success → Degraded
  • Health invariant: RoutingDecision.backend is NEVER a BackendId whose Replica.health = Ejected.
  • EWMA decay: ewma_new = α * sample + (1-α) * ewma_old; α=0.2 default; configurable per model.

8.5 PrefixTree (Radix Cache) Model

Purpose: route requests whose prompt shares a long prefix with tokens already materialized in a backend's KV cache → reduces TTFT and compute cost.

  • PrefixTree structure:
  • root
    • ├── [hash_A] → {BackendId(1), BackendId(3)} recency: T-10s
    • │ ├── [hash_A+B] → {BackendId(1)} recency: T-2s
    • │ └── [hash_A+C] → {BackendId(3)} recency: T-5s
    • └── [hash_D] → {BackendId(2)} recency: T-1s

Key operations:

  • longest_match(prefix_hash) -> Option<(depth, Set<BackendId>)> — walk radix tree; return deepest node whose BackendId set intersects Healthy replicas.
  • insert(prefix_hash, backend_id, timestamp) — called on COMPLETED; adds leaf; evicts LRU nodes beyond capacity.
  • evict(backend_id) — called on Ejected; removes all tree nodes referencing that backend.
  • invalidate_stale(ttl: Duration) — background task; removes nodes older than TTL.

PrefixHash computation:

prefix_hash = SHA-256( canonical_token_bytes )[0..16]
canonical_token_bytes = little-endian u32 array of first min(512, N) token ids

Routing with prefix cache:

  1. longest_match(request.prefix_hash) → candidates.
  2. Intersect candidates with Healthy replicas for request.model + request.adapter.
  3. If intersect non-empty: pick lowest LoadState.kv_util among matches → RouteReason::prefix_hit.
  4. If empty (cold start or all matched backends degraded/ejected): fall through to least_load.

Edge cases:

  • Hash collision: two distinct prompts produce same prefix_hash → harmless; backend may not have the exact prefix but will re-compute; routed with RouteReason::prefix_hit but actual cache miss is acceptable.
  • All matching backends ejected: invariant forces fallback; prefix_hit reason NOT emitted; fallback emitted.
  • Tree race: concurrent insert + evict on same node → protected by per-tree RwLock; writers hold write lock; readers share read lock; stale reads tolerated (eventual consistency acceptable for cache hints).

8.6 Admission Control & Queue Model

Queue per (ModelId, Priority) pair:

Queue key: (ModelId, Priority)
Queue capacity: config.queue_max[model] * priority_weight[p]
  e.g., model="llama3-70b", capacity=1000 total
        Critical=200, High=300, Normal=400, Low=80, BestEffort=20

Admission algorithm (sequential checks, first failure → shed):

  1. Deadline check: now + estimated_queue_wait > slo.deadline → shed 504.
  2. Capacity check: queue.len() >= queue_max → shed 429 (backpressure).
  3. Token budget check: request.prompt_tokens + request.max_tokens > model.max_context → reject 400.
  4. Replica availability check: at least one Healthy replica for model+adapter → admit; else hold for replica_wait_timeout then shed.

Queue drain:

  • Dequeue order: priority-descending, then FIFO within priority tier.
  • Dequeue rate governed by infr-sched fairness policy (token budgets per tenant/model).
  • SLO scan: background task every 50 ms evicts expired entries (deadline < now) → shed 504 without dispatching.

Backpressure propagation:

  • HTTP/1.1: return 429 with Retry-After header.
  • HTTP/2: send RST_STREAM or 429 response.
  • gRPC: RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED status.
  • Internal: infr-ingress checks queue fullness before accepting TCP read; applies TCP backpressure by pausing reads on saturated connections.

Bounded-queue invariant: queue.len() is bounded by queue_max; no dynamic growth; admission is rejected before exceeding bound.

8.7 Routing Policy Engine — Decision Procedure

Candidate set (computed once per request): replicas serving req.model, filtered to Health == Healthy, further filtered to replicas holding req.adapter when an adapter is requested. An empty candidate set short-circuits to RouteReason::Fallback with an error — no tier below is consulted.

Decision tiers, evaluated in strict priority order against the candidate set — the first tier that yields a non-empty eligible subset wins and the search stops:

Tier Name Eligible subset (within candidates) Winner selection RouteReason on match
1 Session affinity Candidates equal to the backend bound in affinity_store for req.session_id, if that backend is not overloaded The bound backend itself Affinity
2 Prefix-cache aware Candidates ∩ tree.longest_match(req.prefix_hash) backend set min by score_load (table below) over the intersection PrefixHit
3 Adapter-resident Candidates already holding req.adapter loaded (skips adapter swap-in cost) min by score_load over that subset AdapterResident
4 Least-load (default) Full candidate set min by score_load over all candidates LeastLoad

score_load weighted-sum components (default weights, per-model configurable via infr-cli config reload, no restart required):

Component Default weight Contribution
load.queue_depth w_q = 0.4 pending requests at replica
load.kv_util w_kv = 0.3 KV-cache occupancy fraction
load.ewma_ttft_ms w_t = 0.2 smoothed time-to-first-token
load.inflight w_i = 0.1 dispatched, awaiting first token

score_load(r) = w_q·queue_depth + w_kv·kv_util + w_t·ewma_ttft_ms + w_i·inflight; lower score wins each tier's min.

8.8 Dispatch & Streaming Protocol Model

Connection pool per BackendId:

  • Multiplexed HTTP/2 streams; max concurrent streams bounded by backend's max_concurrent_streams advertisement.
  • Pool size: min_idle=2, max_size=config.backend_max_conns (default 32).
  • Acquire timeout: 50 ms; on timeout → circuit-breaker check → fallback to least-loaded alternative.

Request forwarding wire format:

POST /v1/completions  (OpenAI-compatible) or /generate (vLLM native)
Headers: X-Request-Id, X-Upstream-Deadline, X-Prefix-Hash, X-Router-Lease
Body: JSON { model, prompt, max_tokens, stream, ... }

Streaming relay invariants:

  • Order invariant: bytes forwarded to client in exact order received from backend; no reordering, no buffering beyond flow control.
  • SSE relay: each data: ... chunk forwarded immediately on receipt; [DONE] forwarded then connection closed.
  • Chunked-transfer relay: chunk boundaries preserved; no re-chunking.
  • On backend mid-stream disconnect: if request.idempotent → attempt hedge/retry on new backend from token 0 (no partial replay); else → send SSE data: {"error":"upstream_disconnect"} then close.

Retry policy:

Attempt Delay Condition
1 (initial) 0 —
2 50 ms jitter 5xx, connect_err, timeout; only if idempotent=true or retryable_error
3 200 ms jitter same; new backend selected
4+ rejected retries_exhausted → FAILED

Hedging (speculative execution):

  • Enabled only if request.idempotent=true AND p99_ttft > slo.ttft_ms * 0.8 (configurable threshold).
  • Hedge fires after hedge_delay = ewma_ttft_ms * 1.5 ms on a second backend.
  • First response wins; second request cancelled via HTTP/2 RST_STREAM.
  • At-most-once invariant: hedging is ONLY permitted for idempotent requests.

Circuit breaker per BackendId:

States: Closed → Open → HalfOpen
Closed  : normal dispatch; trip on consecutive_failures >= threshold (default 5)
Open    : all requests to backend fail-fast; timeout = 10s
HalfOpen: allow 1 probe request; success → Closed; failure → Open (reset timer)

8.9 Disaggregated Prefill/Decode Coordination

Applies when backend fleet supports disaggregation (e.g., DistServe-style split prefill/decode nodes).

Model:

  • PrefillReplica: optimized for high-throughput prompt ingestion (large batch, high MFU).
  • DecodeReplica: optimized for low-latency token streaming (smaller batch, KV cache resident).
  • KV cache transfer: prefill node sends KV blocks to decode node over RDMA/NVLink or TCP fallback.

Routing for disaggregated requests — applies only when model.disaggregated_mode is set; unified (non-disaggregated) models skip this table entirely and use the standard §8.7 decision procedure:

Step Action On failure
1 Select prefill_backend from PrefillReplicas via the §8.7 decision procedure (reason: LeastLoad) No prefill candidate → route_failed
2 Select decode_backend from DecodeReplicas via the §8.7 decision procedure (reason: KvAffinity) No decode candidate → route_failed
3 Dispatch prompt to prefill_backend; receive opaque KV-transfer-token Prefill fails → retry on a new prefill_backend (idempotent step)
4 Forward (prompt_ref, kv_token) to decode_backend; stream tokens to client Decode fails post-transfer → route_failed (KV blocks are not re-transferable without cost)

Coordination invariants:

  • Lease covers BOTH prefill and decode phases; released only after decode completes.
  • KV transfer token is opaque to infr-route; treated as bytes forwarded to decode backend.
  • infr-sched emits autoscale signals independently for prefill pool and decode pool.

8.10 Autoscaling Signal Model

infr-sched emits AutoscaleSignal events consumed by external orchestrators (Kubernetes HPA, Ray autoscaler):

AutoscaleSignal {
  model          : ModelId
  replica_class  : ReplicaClass   -- {Prefill, Decode, Combined}
  current_count  : u32
  desired_count  : u32
  reason         : ScaleReason    -- {queue_depth, slo_miss_rate, kv_util, idle}
  timestamp      : Instant
}

Scale-out triggers:

Metric Threshold Action
avg_queue_depth > 10 for 30 s scale_out by ceil(queue_depth / target_queue_depth)
slo_miss_rate (TTFT) > 5% over 60 s scale_out by 1
avg_kv_util > 0.85 for 60 s scale_out by 1
p99_ttft_ms > slo.ttft_ms * 1.2 scale_out by 1

Scale-in triggers:

Metric Threshold Action
avg_queue_depth < 2 for 300 s scale_in by 1 (keep min_replicas)
avg_kv_util < 0.3 for 300 s scale_in by 1
No traffic 600 s scale_in to min_replicas

Cooldown: 120 s between consecutive scale actions per model (prevents thrash).

8.11 Observability Data Model

Metrics emitted per stage (label cardinality: model, adapter, backend_id, route_reason, priority):

Metric Name Type Labels Description
infr_request_total Counter model, priority, outcome requests by final state
infr_queue_depth Gauge model, priority instantaneous queue length
infr_queue_wait_ms Histogram model, priority time from enqueue to dequeue
infr_shed_total Counter model, priority, reason admission-rejected requests
infr_route_decision_total Counter model, route_reason routing decisions by reason
infr_ttft_ms Histogram model, backend_id, route_reason time-to-first-token
infr_tpot_ms Histogram model, backend_id time-per-output-token
infr_e2e_latency_ms Histogram model, priority full request latency
infr_backend_kv_util Gauge backend_id, model KV cache utilization
infr_backend_queue_depth Gauge backend_id backend queue depth (probed)
infr_prefix_cache_hit_total Counter model requests routed via prefix_hit
infr_prefix_cache_miss_total Counter model requests with no prefix match
infr_retry_total Counter model, backend_id, reason dispatch retries
infr_circuit_open_total Counter backend_id circuit-breaker trips
infr_autoscale_signal_total Counter model, replica_class, direction scale signals emitted
infr_slo_miss_total Counter model, slo_dimension SLO violations (ttft/tpot/deadline)
infr_lease_active Gauge model inflight leases

Trace span hierarchy:

  • infr_request (root span: RequestId, model, priority)
    • ├── queue_wait
    • ├── routing_decision (route_reason, backend_id)
    • ├── dispatch_connect (pool_acquire_ms)
    • │ ├── prefill (disaggregated mode only)
    • │ └── decode / generate
    • └── stream_relay (ttft_ms, token_count, tpot_ms)

Structured log fields (every log line): request_id, model, backend_id, phase, route_reason, priority, duration_ms, outcome.

8.12 Configuration & Reload Model

Config is hot-reloaded (no restart) via SIGHUP or API endpoint POST /admin/reload:

RouterConfig {
  models: Map<ModelId, ModelConfig>
  backends: Map<BackendId, BackendConfig>
  routing: RoutingConfig
  queue: QueueConfig
  dispatch: DispatchConfig
  sched: SchedConfig
}

ModelConfig { max_context: u32, adapters: Vec<AdapterId>, disaggregated: bool, queue_max: u32 }
BackendConfig { addr: SocketAddr, model: ModelId, adapters: Vec<AdapterId>, max_conns: u32 }
RoutingConfig { weights: LoadWeights, prefix_ttl_s: u64, affinity_ttl_s: u64 }
QueueConfig { priorities: PriorityLimits, slo_scan_interval_ms: u64 }
DispatchConfig { retry_max: u8, hedge_enabled: bool, circuit_breaker: CbConfig }
SchedConfig { fairness: FairnessPolicy, autoscale: AutoscaleConfig }

Reload invariants:

  • Config is validated before applying; invalid config → reload rejected, old config retained.
  • New backends added: registered in infr-registry, health probed before routing eligible.
  • Removed backends: drained (no new requests), existing Leases allowed to complete, then removed.
  • Weight changes: applied immediately to next routing decision; in-flight requests unaffected.
  • Queue-max reduction: new arrivals bounded by new max; existing queue not truncated.

8.13 Worked Examples

Example A — Normal streaming request (cold prefix)

T=0ms  client POST /v1/completions {model="llama3-70b", stream=true}
T=1ms  infr-ingress: parse → Request{id=01HX.., prefix_hash=ab12..}
T=1ms  infr-queue: admit (queue_depth=3/1000, deadline ok) → Lease created
T=2ms  infr-route: longest_match(ab12..) → miss; least_load → Backend(2)
T=2ms  RoutingDecision{backend=2, reason=least_load} emitted
T=3ms  infr-dispatch: acquire conn from pool(Backend 2); forward POST
T=45ms infr-dispatch: first byte received → TTFT=42ms; emit ttft_ms=42
T=45ms infr-ingress: begin SSE relay to client
T=800ms last token; SSE [DONE]; Lease released; PrefixTree.insert(ab12.., Backend 2)

Example B — Prefix-cache hit

Request{prefix_hash=ab12..} arrives 10s after Example A
PrefixTree.longest_match(ab12..) → {Backend 2}, depth=1, recency=10s
Backend 2 health=Healthy, kv_util=0.6 (acceptable)
RoutingDecision{backend=2, reason=prefix_hit}
TTFT reduced (KV cache warm): 12ms vs 42ms baseline

Example C — Admission shed (queue full)

Model="llama3-70b" queue at capacity (1000/1000)
New request arrives: infr-queue capacity check fails
Response: 429 Too Many Requests, Retry-After: 2
Metric: infr_shed_total{model="llama3-70b", reason="queue_full"}++

Example D — Backend ejection mid-stream

Backend 3 returns 500 on 5 consecutive requests → Health: Healthy→Degraded
After 5 more failures → Health: Degraded→Ejected
infr-route: Backend 3 removed from candidate set (Health invariant enforced)
PrefixTree.evict(Backend 3) called (removes all prefix nodes referencing Backend 3)
infr-dispatch: any in-flight request on Backend 3 → retry if idempotent; fail if not
Circuit breaker: Open for Backend 3, 10s timeout

Example E — SLO deadline eviction from queue

Request{slo.deadline=T+500ms, priority=Normal} enqueued at T=0
Queue stall: all backends at capacity
At T=500ms: background SLO scan fires; deadline < now → evict from queue
Response: 504 Gateway Timeout
Metric: infr_slo_miss_total{model=.., slo_dimension="deadline"}++

Example F — Disaggregated prefill/decode

Request dispatched to PrefillReplica(id=10)
Prefill completes in 200ms; returns kv_token="kv://block/abc123"
infr-dispatch sends (prompt_ref + kv_token) to DecodeReplica(id=20)
DecodeReplica streams tokens; relay to client
If DecodeReplica fails: FAILED (KV transfer not replayable without cost)
If PrefillReplica fails before KV token: retry PrefillReplica (idempotent)

Example G — Hedge firing

Request idempotent=true, slo.ttft_ms=100
ewma_ttft_ms for Backend 4 = 80ms; hedge_delay = 80*1.5 = 120ms
Primary dispatch → Backend 4 at T=0
At T=120ms: no first token yet; hedge fires → Backend 5
Backend 5 returns first token at T=135ms (Backend 4 still pending)
Backend 5 wins; Backend 4 RST_STREAM sent
Client receives tokens from Backend 5

8.14 Failure Modes & Domain-Level Invariant Enforcement

Failure Detection Response Invariant Preserved
Backend returns 5xx infr-dispatch status code retry (idempotent) or fail at-most-once
Backend silent hang (no bytes) dispatch.timeout timeout → retry/fail lease released
KV cache full on backend kv_util probe ≥ 0.95 Health→Degraded; reduce weight Health invariant
All backends ejected route candidates empty 503 + autoscale signal Health invariant
Queue overflow queue.len() == queue_max shed 429 Bounded-queue invariant
PrefixTree eviction race backend ejected while routing fallback via least_load Health invariant
Mid-stream byte reorder (implementation guard) panic in debug; alert in prod Order invariant
Hedge on non-idempotent idempotent flag check hedge blocked at-most-once
Config reload with invalid schema validation before apply reject reload, keep old config consistency
Lease leak (no release) Lease TTL watchdog (deadline + grace) force-release + alert Lease invariant

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-01 Implementation language Rust (edition 2021, MSRV 1.78) Go, C++ No GC tail latency; memory safety; mature async networking; linkerd2-proxy precedent Go wins when team Go expertise dominates, throughput SLO is loose (>100ms P99 acceptable), and rapid feature iteration outweighs tail latency — e.g., an internal batch-only inference proxy with no streaming
ADR-02 Async runtime tokio (1.x, multi-thread scheduler) async-std, smol, glommio Largest ecosystem; well-tested under high connection counts; tokio-compat for hyper/tonic glommio wins on bare-metal single-NUMA-node deployment where io_uring and thread-per-core isolation eliminate cross-thread wakeup overhead at >500k req/s
ADR-03 HTTP/1.1 + HTTP/2 server hyper (1.x) actix-web, axum (wraps hyper), h2 direct Hyper is the foundational layer; minimal overhead; full HTTP/2 server push & trailers axum wins when route-dispatch ergonomics matter more than raw throughput (e.g., internal admin API gateway with dozens of typed handlers and no streaming)
ADR-04 gRPC tonic (0.11+) grpc-sys (C bindings), tower-grpc Pure Rust; built on hyper + prost; streaming first-class; interop with vLLM gRPC API grpc-sys wins when the existing backend ecosystem is entirely C++ and zero-copy interop with a shared protobuf memory arena is required — e.g., a Triton-only deployment with custom C++ filters
ADR-05 Build system Cargo (workspace) Bazel, Buck2 Native Rust toolchain; workspace for 11 crates; reproducible via Cargo.lock; no Bazel overhead Bazel wins when the router must compile with a multi-language monorepo (C++/Python/Rust) sharing Protobuf definitions and requiring hermetic cross-compilation for multiple hardware targets
ADR-06 Protobuf/gRPC code generation prost + tonic-build protobuf (official), prost-build alone prost generates idiomatic Rust structs; tonic-build integrates build.rs; no runtime reflection protobuf-official (with grpc-sys) wins when reflection-based gRPC server (grpc.reflection.v1alpha) is required for runtime schema discovery by a polyglot ops toolchain
ADR-07 JSON serialization serde_json + serde (derive) simd-json, rmp-serde Standard; serde derive zero-cost; simd-json not stable API for production simd-json wins at ≥1 GB/s JSON ingress throughput (e.g., high-fanout event logging pipelines) where SIMD acceleration is measurably dominant; rmp-serde wins on internal binary-only paths where wire format is not human-readable
ADR-08 Request ID ulid crate (Ulid) uuid v4, snowflake, nanoid ULID: monotonic, sortable, URL-safe; no coordination; 128-bit entropy; good for distributed tracing correlation Snowflake wins in a single data-center deployment with a centralized ID service where millisecond-precision sortability and 64-bit compact IDs are required for high-throughput log join
ADR-09 Metrics metrics crate facade + metrics-exporter-prometheus opentelemetry-metrics, statsd, miette metrics facade is zero-cost; swap exporter without changing instrumentation sites; Prometheus pull model standard OpenTelemetry-metrics wins when the organization mandates a single telemetry SDK across traces + metrics + logs with push-based OTLP to a vendor-managed backend (e.g., Grafana Cloud, Honeycomb)
ADR-10 Distributed tracing opentelemetry + tracing crate + tracing-opentelemetry jaeger-client (deprecated), zipkin native tracing integrates with tokio spans; opentelemetry standard; OTLP exporter; GRPC trace propagation Zipkin wins in brownfield environments where the entire observability stack is Zipkin-based and the cost of migrating trace storage is prohibitive
ADR-11 Structured logging tracing + tracing-subscriber (JSON formatter) log crate + env_logger, slog tracing spans carry context (request_id, model, backend_id) automatically; JSON for log aggregators slog wins in high-frequency embedded or edge scenarios where tracing's span machinery overhead is measurable and structured async context is not needed (e.g., a bare-metal proxy with sub-microsecond logging budgets)
ADR-12 Configuration format TOML (config crate + toml) YAML, JSON, HCL TOML: unambiguous; config crate supports layered overrides (file + env vars + CLI flags) HCL wins when the deployment is Terraform/Nomad-native and operators already manage infrastructure in HCL, making config format unification reduce cognitive load and tooling surface
ADR-13 Async channel primitives tokio::sync::{mpsc, broadcast, watch} crossbeam, flume, async-channel tokio-native; watch for config broadcast; mpsc for pipeline stages; broadcast for health updates crossbeam wins in mixed sync+async code where bounded channels must be used from both thread-pool workers and async tasks without spawning a runtime, e.g., a CPU-bound pipeline stage in a Rayon thread pool
ADR-14 Concurrency for shared state Arc<RwLock<T>> (tokio::sync::RwLock) Mutex everywhere, lock-free structures, dashmap RwLock: read-heavy workloads (routing, registry); async-aware; dashmap considered but RwLock simpler invariants dashmap wins when the shared map is write-heavy under high concurrency (e.g., per-request mutable state with thousands of concurrent writers) and lock contention on RwLock becomes a measured bottleneck
ADR-15 HTTP connection pooling (outbound) hyper-util PoolClient + custom pool wrapper reqwest (too high-level), hyper direct only hyper-util provides HTTP/2 multiplexed pooling; custom wrapper adds circuit-breaker + per-backend limits reqwest wins for low-concurrency admin/sidecar clients where convenience (redirect, cookie, form encoding) outweighs the overhead of hyper-util and the number of concurrent connections is <10
ADR-16 Hashing for PrefixHash SHA-256 (sha2 crate), truncated to 16 bytes xxHash, FNV, blake3 SHA-256: collision resistance adequate; sha2 pure Rust; 16-byte truncation sufficient for radix tree key blake3 wins when PrefixHash computation is on the critical path for requests with >4 KB prompt prefixes and the SIMD throughput advantage (>3× SHA-256) is a measured bottleneck
ADR-17 Testing framework cargo test (unit) + nextest (CI) criterion-only, proptest-only nextest: parallel, faster CI; unit tests per crate; integration tests in tests/ proptest-only wins for pure algorithmic crates (e.g., PrefixTree eviction logic) where the entire correctness argument is property-based and no integration harness is needed
ADR-18 Property-based / fuzz testing proptest (property) + cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) quickcheck, AFL proptest: deterministic shrinking; cargo-fuzz: LLVM coverage-guided; fuzz targets in infr-bench/fuzz/ AFL wins on embedded targets or non-Linux environments where LLVM sanitizer support is unavailable but persistent mode fuzzing via AFL++ forkserver is feasible
ADR-19 Benchmark harness criterion (micro) + infr-bench crate (load/chaos) hyperfine (CLI-only), divan criterion: statistical micro-benchmarks for routing/queue hot paths; infr-bench: full end-to-end load generation divan wins for simple before/after regressions in a crate with many small pure-function benchmarks where criterion's statistical overhead and warm-up time inflate CI benchmark suite duration past an acceptable gate
ADR-20 CI/CD pipeline GitHub Actions (matrix: stable + MSRV 1.78) CircleCI, Buildkite, Jenkins GitHub Actions native to repo; matrix tests MSRV; clippy + rustfmt + deny + audit in CI Buildkite wins when CI must run on self-hosted GPU nodes for model-serving integration tests, and GitHub Actions hosted runners lack GPU access or network access to on-premise model registries
ADR-21 Dependency audit cargo-audit + cargo-deny (licenses + advisories) snyk, dependabot alone cargo-deny: license policy + advisory DB; cargo-audit: RustSec; CI-blocking on high-severity CVEs Snyk wins in polyglot organizations where a single security platform must scan Rust, Python, and container layers with a unified dashboard and policy engine, making per-ecosystem tools operationally fragmented
ADR-22 Container image distroless/cc-debian12 base; multi-stage Dockerfile Alpine (musl compat issues), full Ubuntu distroless: minimal attack surface; multi-stage: build in rust:1.78 official, copy binary only Alpine wins when image size is the primary constraint (e.g., edge deployments with <100 MB image budget) and musl compatibility has been verified with the full dependency tree including ring and rustls
ADR-23 Orchestration interface Kubernetes-compatible (emit metrics for HPA; /healthz, /readyz endpoints) Nomad, raw VMs K8s dominant for LLM serving infra; HPA consumes custom metrics via Prometheus adapter; /readyz gates traffic Nomad wins in regulated environments (financial, air-gapped) where Kubernetes control-plane attack surface is a compliance blocker and existing Nomad infrastructure is already mature
ADR-24 Security: TLS termination rustls (0.23+) via tokio-rustls OpenSSL (via openssl-sys), native-tls rustls: memory-safe; no C deps; FIPS-certified ring backend available; mTLS for backend auth OpenSSL wins when FIPS 140-2 Level 2 certification is mandated by a government or financial-sector contract and rustls + ring's FIPS module has not yet received the required validation for the target environment
ADR-25 Release versioning SemVer; cargo workspace version sync CalVer, manual per-crate SemVer: standard for Rust crates; workspace version in root Cargo.toml propagated to all crates CalVer wins for an operational release train (e.g., 2024.Q3.1) where version numbers must communicate release cadence and time-based support windows to downstream SRE consumers, not API compatibility
ADR-26 Reproducible builds Cargo.lock committed; rust-toolchain.toml pinned; Dockerfile ARG RUST_VERSION Nix flakes, hermetic Bazel rust-toolchain.toml: pins toolchain per repo; Cargo.lock: exact dep versions; sufficient for binary reproducibility Nix flakes win when byte-for-byte binary reproducibility is required for supply-chain attestation (e.g., SLSA Level 3 provenance), where Cargo.lock alone does not guarantee identical build environments across OS versions
ADR-27 Documentation rustdoc (all public APIs; #![deny(missing_docs)]); architecture docs in docs/ Markdown mdBook, Notion rustdoc: inline with code; deny(missing_docs) enforces coverage; architecture ADRs as Markdown for PR review mdBook wins when the documentation audience is primarily non-Rust engineers (e.g., ML platform teams consuming the gRPC API) and a navigable multi-chapter book with cross-references outweighs inline rustdoc
ADR-28 Inter-crate API stability infr-core types are #[non_exhaustive] enums where future variants expected sealed traits, pub(crate) only #[non_exhaustive]: allows adding variants without semver break; downstream crates must handle _ match arms pub(crate) wins when the crate boundary is internal-only and no downstream consumers outside the workspace exist, making the #[non_exhaustive] constraint an unnecessary friction on exhaustive pattern matching in tests
