11. Core Schema and Core Types

11.1 Identity Types

Type Definition Ownership Mutation Concurrency Serialization Compat
RequestId struct RequestId(Ulid) value; Copy+Clone immutable after creation lock-free (value type) JSON string (26-char Crockford base32); bincode u128 stable; never reuse
BackendId struct BackendId(u32) value; Copy immutable trivially Send+Sync JSON u32; bincode u32 stable; retire-not-reuse
ModelId struct ModelId(Arc<str>) cheaply cloneable immutable Send+Sync JSON string semver-tagged string; no spaces
AdapterId struct AdapterId(Arc<str>) cheaply cloneable immutable Send+Sync JSON string LoRA adapter name; registry-validated
PrefixHash struct PrefixHash([u8; 32]) value; Copy immutable trivially safe hex string (JSON); raw bytes (bincode) deterministic: SHA-256 of token prefix byte repr
  • Ulid sourced from ulid crate; monotonic within same millisecond; sortable; no coordination needed.
  • PrefixHash computation: SHA-256 over the byte concatenation of token ids (little-endian u32 each) up to prefix_len tokens. Deterministic across router instances.
  • ModelId and AdapterId intern via Arc<str>; equality is byte-equality; no normalization.

11.2 Request

struct Request {
    id:            RequestId,           // immutable; assigned at ingress
    model:         ModelId,             // required; routing key
    adapter:       Option<AdapterId>,   // LoRA adapter; None = base model
    prompt_tokens: u32,                 // parsed token count; 0 if unknown pre-tokenize
    max_tokens:    u32,                 // upper bound; used for capacity estimation
    prefix_hash:   PrefixHash,          // SHA-256 of prompt token prefix; routing key
    slo:           Slo,                 // TTFT + TPOT + wall-clock deadline
    priority:      Priority,            // P0..P3; drives queue ordering
    idempotent:    bool,                // gates hedging; caller declares
    stream:        bool,                // SSE/chunked relay vs. batch response
}
  • Ownership: Arc<Request> shared across queue, route, dispatch stages; never mutated after construction.
  • Concurrency: Send+Sync; interior immutability; no locks needed.
  • Persistence: not persisted; in-memory only; reconstructed from wire on retry.
  • Serialization: serde JSON for admin/debug endpoints; bincode for internal IPC if needed; proto for gRPC path (InferRequest).
  • Compat: additive fields only; #[non_exhaustive] on public constructors; prompt_tokens=0 is valid (router estimates from byte length).
  • Failure mode: prompt_tokens overflow → cap at u32::MAX; log warning; do not panic.

11.3 Slo

struct Slo {
    ttft_ms:     u32,          // max time-to-first-token milliseconds; 0 = unconstrained
    tpot_ms:     u32,          // max time-per-output-token milliseconds; 0 = unconstrained
    deadline:    Option<Instant>, // absolute wall-clock deadline; None = no hard deadline
}
  • Value type; Copy; computed at ingress from request headers or tenant config.
  • deadline set to Instant::now() + slo_window; drives queue expiry scan in infr-queue.
  • Invariant: ttft_ms > 0 || tpot_ms > 0 || deadline.is_some() for SLO-tracked requests; all-zero = best-effort.
  • Serialization: ttft_ms/tpot_ms as JSON u32; deadline as relative offset-ms from request receipt (transport serialization only).

11.4 Priority

enum Priority { P0 = 0, P1 = 1, P2 = 2, P3 = 3 }
  • P0 = highest (interactive/latency-sensitive); P3 = lowest (batch/background).
  • Drives min-heap ordering in infr-queue; P0 pre-empts P3 on saturation shedding.
  • Copy; stable repr; serialized as u8.
  • Invariant: P0 requests are never shed before P1–P3 at the same deadline; P3 shed first.

11.5 LoadState

struct LoadState {
    queue_depth:   u32,     // backend-reported pending request count
    kv_util:       f32,     // KV cache utilization [0.0, 1.0]
    running_batch: u32,     // current continuous-batch size
    ewma_ttft_ms:  f32,     // EWMA of recent TTFT observations (α=0.1)
    inflight:      u32,     // requests currently being processed (router-tracked)
}
  • Owned by infr-health; published via Arc<ArcSwap<LoadState>> per replica.
  • Mutation: only infr-health writes; all others read via ArcSwap::load() — wait-free reads.
  • kv_util ∈ [0.0, 1.0]; values outside clamped at scrape time; NaN treated as 1.0 (worst case).
  • ewma_ttft_ms computed: new = α * sample + (1-α) * old; α = 0.1 configurable per deployment.
  • Persistence: none; re-populated from next scrape cycle on restart.
  • Serialization: JSON for /admin/backends endpoint; not sent over wire to clients.
  • Failure mode: scrape timeout → retain last LoadState; mark replica Degraded after N consecutive failures.

11.6 Health

enum Health { Healthy, Degraded, Ejected }
  • FSM transitions: Healthy → Degraded (error-rate threshold or scrape miss); Degraded → Ejected (consecutive failures); Ejected → Healthy (successful probe + hysteresis window).
  • Ejected replicas are filtered out before any routing decision — health invariant enforced in infr-route::policy.
  • Copy; stored in Replica.health: AtomicU8 (0/1/2) for lock-free reads.
  • Serialization: JSON string "Healthy"/"Degraded"/"Ejected" in admin API.

11.7 Replica

struct Replica {
    id:       BackendId,
    model:    ModelId,
    adapters: HashSet<AdapterId>,    // LoRA adapters loaded; empty = base-model only
    load:     Arc<ArcSwap<LoadState>>,
    health:   Arc<AtomicU8>,          // Health as u8; see 11.6
    addr:     SocketAddr,
}
  • Owned by infr-registry; shared read-only via Arc<Replica> to routing and dispatch stages.
  • adapters: immutable after replica registration; update = deregister + re-register.
  • load and health are the only mutable fields; updated by infr-health without acquiring registry lock.
  • Concurrency: Arc<Replica> is Send+Sync; ArcSwap load is wait-free; AtomicU8 is lock-free.
  • Serialization: JSON for admin; addr as "ip:port" string.
  • Failure mode: stale Replica ref after deregister → dispatch sees Ejected health → request re-routed.

11.8 RoutingDecision

struct RoutingDecision {
    backend: BackendId,
    reason:  RouteReason,
}

enum RouteReason {
    LeastLoad,
    PrefixHit { match_len: u32 },   // tokens matched in PrefixTree
    AdapterResident,
    Affinity { session_key: u64 },
    Fallback,                        // no preferred backend; random among healthy
}
  • Value; Copy; produced by infr-route; consumed by infr-dispatch and infr-telemetry.
  • Every RoutingDecision logged with RequestId → decision auditability invariant.
  • PrefixHit.match_len recorded in telemetry → prefix-cache-hit-rate metric.
  • Serialization: JSON for trace span attributes.

11.9 Lease

struct Lease {
    request:  Arc<Request>,
    backend:  BackendId,
    deadline: Instant,              // copied from request.slo.deadline or now + max_ttl
}
  • Created by infr-queue on admit; held through dispatch; dropped on completion or timeout.
  • Lease invariant: exactly one Lease per admitted request; drop releases backend inflight counter.
  • Implemented via RAII Drop: impl Drop for Lease { fn drop → decrement inflight }.
  • Not serialized; in-memory only; not Clone (single ownership enforces invariant).
  • Failure mode: Lease drop on panic → inflight still decremented (RAII); no counter leak.

11.10 PrefixTree

struct PrefixTree {
    // radix trie: PrefixHash prefix → BTreeSet<(Instant, BackendId)>
    // recency tracked per (backend, prefix) entry
    // eviction: LRU by last-access Instant when node_count > capacity
}

trait PrefixIndex: Send + Sync {
    fn insert(&self, hash: PrefixHash, backend: BackendId);
    fn longest_match(&self, hash: PrefixHash) -> Option<(BackendId, u32)>;
    fn evict_backend(&self, backend: BackendId);
    fn node_count(&self) -> usize;
}
  • Concurrency: RwLock<RadixTree> internally; write on insert/evict; read on longest_match.
  • longest_match returns (BackendId, match_len_tokens) for the backend with the longest cached prefix for hash; None if no match.
  • Eviction policy: LRU; triggered when node_count > capacity; removes oldest (Instant, BackendId) entries first.
  • Failure mode: evict_backend called on replica deregister → removes all prefix entries for that backend; must not block routing.
  • Not persisted; rebuilt from backend-reported cache state on startup/reload.
  • Serialization: not serialized; admin dump endpoint returns node count + top-K prefixes as JSON.

11.11 AdapterInventory

struct AdapterInventory {
    // adapter_id → Vec<BackendId> index
    // maintained in sync with Replica.adapters
}

trait AdapterIndex: Send + Sync {
    fn backends_for(&self, adapter: &AdapterId) -> SmallVec<[BackendId; 4]>;
    fn register(&self, backend: BackendId, adapters: &HashSet<AdapterId>);
    fn deregister(&self, backend: BackendId);
}
  • Owned by infr-registry; Arc<AdapterInventory> shared to infr-route.
  • Mutation: protected by RwLock; writes on register/deregister; reads lock-free via snapshot pattern if hot-path demands it.
  • SmallVec<[BackendId; 4]> avoids heap alloc for common case (≤4 replicas per adapter).
  • Failure mode: adapter requested but no backend has it → InferError::AdapterNotFound.

11.12 InferError

enum InferError {
    BackendUnavailable { backend: BackendId, reason: String },
    AdapterNotFound    { adapter: AdapterId },
    ModelNotFound      { model: ModelId },
    QueueFull          { model: ModelId, queue_len: u32 },
    SloDeadlineExpired { request: RequestId },
    UpstreamTimeout    { phase: TimeoutPhase, elapsed_ms: u64 },
    UpstreamError      { status: u16, body: Bytes },
    CircuitOpen        { backend: BackendId },
    HedgeCancelled     { winner: BackendId },
    ParseError         { detail: String },
    Internal           { detail: String },
}

enum TimeoutPhase { Connect, FirstToken, TokenInterval }
  • Non-exhaustive (#[non_exhaustive]); new variants are additive.
  • All variants carry enough context for structured log emission without additional lookups.
  • QueueFull maps to HTTP 429; SloDeadlineExpired maps to HTTP 504; UpstreamTimeout maps to HTTP 504; UpstreamError proxies upstream status; CircuitOpen maps to HTTP 503.
  • Serialization: JSON for error response body; detail field for human-readable message.
  • InferError implements std::error::Error + Send + Sync + 'static.
  • Failure mode: Internal variant is the catch-all for unexpected states; always logs at ERROR level with full context.

11.13 CapacityDescriptor

struct CapacityDescriptor {
    backend:       BackendId,
    max_batch:     u32,          // backend-declared max continuous-batch size
    kv_capacity:   u64,          // total KV cache slots (tokens)
    gpu_mem_bytes: u64,          // reported GPU memory (informational)
    adapter_slots: u32,          // max simultaneous loaded adapters
}
  • Owned by infr-registry; read-only after backend registration.
  • Used by infr-sched for autoscale signal computation and batching hints.
  • Serialization: JSON in registry API.

11.14 RouterConfig

struct RouterConfig {
    listen:            ListenConfig,      // bind addr, TLS cert/key paths, H2 settings
    backends:          Vec<BackendSpec>,  // static backend list; overridden by dynamic registry
    routing:           RoutingConfig,     // policy name, prefix_cache_capacity, affinity TTL
    queue:             QueueConfig,       // per-model capacity, shedding policy
    dispatch:          DispatchConfig,    // pool size, timeouts, retry/hedge params
    sched:             SchedConfig,       // fairness quotas, autoscale thresholds
    telemetry:         TelemetryConfig,   // prometheus addr, OTLP endpoint, log level
    health:            HealthConfig,      // probe interval, scrape interval, EWMA α
}
  • Loaded at startup from TOML file + env overrides via figment; validated before use.
  • Hot-reload on SIGHUP: diff applied atomically; reload errors logged, old config retained.
  • Serialization: TOML (config file); JSON (admin GET /config endpoint).
  • Failure mode: invalid config at startup → process exits with structured error; hot-reload error → retain old config + emit metric config_reload_error_total.

11.15 SloMetrics and Telemetry Value Types

struct SloMetrics {
    ttft_hist:        Histogram,   // TTFT observation buckets (ms)
    tpot_hist:        Histogram,   // TPOT observation buckets (ms)
    queue_depth:      Gauge,       // per-model queue depth
    cache_hit_rate:   Counter,     // prefix cache hits
    cache_miss_rate:  Counter,     // prefix cache misses
    slo_breach_total: Counter,     // requests that missed TTFT or TPOT SLO
    shed_total:       Counter,     // requests shed at admission
}
  • All metrics labeled with {model, adapter, backend, route_reason} where applicable.
  • Histogram bucket boundaries: 10, 50, 100, 250, 500, 1000, 2500, 5000 ms for TTFT; 1, 5, 10, 25, 50, 100 ms for TPOT.
  • Metrics registered in global Prometheus registry at infr-telemetry init; no per-request allocation.
  • Failure mode: metric label cardinality explosion if adapter is unbounded → label adapter capped at top-100; overflow bucketed as "other".

11.16 Concurrency and Ownership Summary

Type Ownership pattern Read path Write path
Request Arc<Request> (immutable) clone Arc; no lock ingress only; never mutated
LoadState Arc<ArcSwap<LoadState>> load() wait-free infr-health store()
Health (Replica) Arc<AtomicU8> load(Relaxed) infr-health store(SeqCst)
PrefixTree Arc<RwLock<…>> shared read insert/evict write
AdapterInventory Arc<RwLock<…>> shared read register/deregister write
RegistryState Arc<RwLock<RegistryState>> shared read config reload write
Lease Box<Lease> (unique) owner only RAII drop
RouterConfig Arc<ArcSwap<RouterConfig>> load() SIGHUP reload
  • Hot-path rule: routing and dispatch read paths must be wait-free or lock-free; no Mutex in the P99 critical path.
  • ArcSwap used for LoadState and RouterConfig; provides wait-free reads and atomic pointer swap on update.
  • RwLock allowed for PrefixTree and AdapterInventory (updated infrequently; read-heavy workload).
  • All public types that cross async await points are Send + Sync + 'static.

12. Phase 0 — Skeleton — crates, core types, reverse proxy that forwards one request to one static backend and relays the response (no routing logic)

12.1 Objectives

  • Establish full Cargo workspace with all 11 spine crates (infr-core, infr-ingress, infr-registry, infr-health, infr-route, infr-queue, infr-dispatch, infr-sched, infr-telemetry, infr-cli, infr-bench); each crate compiles.
  • Define every canonical core type in infr-core exactly as per spine: RequestId(Ulid), Request, BackendId(u32), Replica, LoadState, RoutingDecision, Slo, Priority, Lease, InferError; all with #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)] and serde.
  • Implement minimal working path: single static backend in config → infr-ingress accepts one HTTP request → infr-dispatch forwards it → streams response back to client → exits cleanly.
  • Constraint: no routing policy logic, no health checks, no queuing, no telemetry aggregation — just plumbing.
  • MSRV 1.78, edition 2021, #![forbid(unsafe_code)] in all crates except where FFI is explicitly noted.

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: ADR-01 (Rust language), ADR-02 (tokio runtime), ADR-05 (Cargo workspace), ADR-08 (ULID for RequestId), ADR-07 (serde_json), B4 (Programming Rust crate design), P1 (I4 bounded-queue early type definition)

Workspace scaffold (Cargo.toml root):

  • [workspace] members = ["infr-core", "infr-ingress", ...]; resolver = "2".
  • Shared [workspace.dependencies]: tokio = { version="1", features=["full"] }, hyper = { version="1", features=["full"] }, hyper-util, http-body-util, bytes, ulid, serde, serde_json, thiserror, tracing, tracing-subscriber, anyhow, arc-swap, dashmap, tokio-stream, futures-util, toml, clap.

infr-core/src/lib.rs:

  • mod types → RequestId, Request, BackendId, ModelId, AdapterId, PrefixHash(u64), Replica, LoadState, RoutingDecision, RouteReason, Slo, Priority, Lease, Health, InferError.
  • RequestId wraps ulid::Ulid; new() calls Ulid::new().
  • InferError: variants Upstream(String), Timeout, NoBackend, Overload, Cancelled, Internal(String).
  • Priority: Critical=0, High=1, Normal=2, Bulk=3; implements Ord.
  • Slo { ttft_ms: u32, tpot_ms: u32, deadline: Option<std::time::Instant> } — Instant excluded from serde; serialize as offset millis.
  • LoadState { queue_depth: u32, kv_util: f32, running_batch: u32, ewma_ttft_ms: f32, inflight: u32 }.
  • Health: Healthy, Degraded, Ejected; is_routable() -> bool { matches!(self, Healthy | Degraded) }.
  • Lease { request_id: RequestId, backend: BackendId, admitted_at: std::time::Instant, deadline: std::time::Instant }.
  • All types: #[cfg_attr(feature="serde", derive(serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize))]; feature serde default on.

infr-dispatch/src/pool.rs (Phase 0 stub):

  • StaticBackend { addr: hyper::Uri } read from env INFR_BACKEND_ADDR.
  • forward(req: http::Request<Incoming>) -> Result<http::Response<Incoming>, InferError> — builds hyper_util::client::legacy::Client (HTTP/1.1), sends, returns response.
  • Streaming: relay body bytes using http_body_util::BodyExt::frame() loop into axum::body::Body or hyper response passthrough.

infr-ingress/src/server.rs (Phase 0 stub):

  • axum::Router with single catch-all POST /*path handler.
  • Handler: extract raw body + headers → construct Request with stub RequestId::new(), zero LoadState, prompt_tokens=0, prefix_hash=PrefixHash(0) → call infr-dispatch::pool::forward → relay status + headers + body stream.
  • Bind on 0.0.0.0:8080 (configurable via INFR_LISTEN_ADDR); axum::serve with graceful shutdown on SIGTERM/SIGINT via tokio::signal.

infr-cli/src/main.rs:

  • clap::Parser; subcommand serve → calls infr_ingress::server::run().
  • Config: struct Config { listen_addr, backend_addr } loaded from env in Phase 0.

infr-bench/src/lib.rs (stub):

  • mod harness — empty struct BenchHarness; no logic yet.

Test harness (Phase 0):

  • infr-core/tests/types.rs: roundtrip serde for every type; RequestId uniqueness (1000 IDs, no collision).
  • infr-dispatch/tests/forward.rs: mock backend with axum echo server in test thread → assert forwarded body matches → assert response status 200.
  • infr-ingress/tests/smoke.rs: spawn ingress + echo backend; reqwest::Client POST → assert echoed body.

12.3 Deliverable

  • cargo build --workspace compiles cleanly with zero warnings (-D warnings).
  • cargo run -p infr-cli -- serve starts, listens on :8080, forwards all requests to $INFR_BACKEND_ADDR.
  • Any HTTP/1.1 POST reaches the backend verbatim; response (including chunked streaming) relayed byte-for-byte.
  • cargo test --workspace passes; 3 integration tests green.
  • Operators can confirm behavior via INFR_LISTEN_ADDR, INFR_BACKEND_ADDR env vars + server log line at startup.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Threshold
cargo build --workspace Zero errors, zero warnings
Roundtrip serde for all infr-core types 100% pass
RequestId collision in 10 000 samples 0 collisions
Echo body integrity SHA-256 of sent == SHA-256 of received
p99 forward latency overhead (loopback backend, 1 KB body) < 2 ms added vs direct
RSS at idle < 20 MB
Graceful shutdown (SIGTERM) In-flight request completes; no ECONNRESET to client
clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings Clean

12.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected output
tests/fixtures/request_roundtrip.json Serde round-trip for Request with all fields populated Deserializes to identical struct
tests/fixtures/request_minimal.json Request with only required fields, optionals absent Defaults applied; no panic
tests/fixtures/loadstate_boundary.json kv_util=1.0, queue_depth=u32::MAX No overflow, serde clean
tests/fixtures/inferr_variants.json All InferError variants Each deserializes correctly
tests/fixtures/priority_ord.txt Ordering assertion: Critical < High < Normal < Bulk Passes assert!(Priority::Critical < Priority::Bulk)
tests/fixtures/echo_body_4kb.bin 4 KB binary blob Forwarded body SHA-256 matches

12.6 Pitfalls

  • Hyper body consumption double-read — reading body for logging then forwarding: body stream is consumed on first read. Root cause: hyper::body::Incoming is single-pass. Debug: response body empty at client. Fix: axum::body::to_bytes then re-wrap in http_body_util::Full; signal via ingress_body_buffer_bytes counter.
  • MSRV breakage from transitive deps — ulid or hyper-util may require newer Rust. Root cause: Cargo.lock resolves latest patch. Debug: cargo +1.78.0 check. Fix: pin transitive deps to MSRV-compatible versions in workspace Cargo.toml.
  • Chunked transfer encoding double-wrap — axum re-encodes already-chunked backend response. Root cause: setting Transfer-Encoding: chunked header while also using axum::body::Body stream. Debug: garbled client response. Fix: strip TE header before relay; let hyper handle framing.
  • SIGTERM vs SIGINT race on shutdown — axum::serve::with_graceful_shutdown waits for all connections; if backend is slow the router hangs. Debug: process stuck at shutdown. Fix: add shutdown deadline (5 s default, INFR_SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_MS); force-close after.
  • Ulid monotonicity skew under test parallelism — concurrent test threads calling Ulid::new() at the same millisecond can produce same Ulid. Root cause: monotonic counter resets per thread. Fix: use ulid::Generator with mutex per test thread; assert uniqueness in test.
  • Response header forwarding stripping content-length — axum strips content-length when body is stream. Root cause: mismatched framing. Debug: client sees content-length: 0. Fix: preserve original header only when body is fully buffered; else omit and let chunked flow.

12.7 Performance Concerns

  • Allocation per request — Bytes::copy_from_slice on each body chunk; for 1 KB chunks at 10k RPS = 10 GB/s memcpy. Mitigate: use bytes::Bytes with zero-copy slice from hyper's internal buffer; avoid extra Vec::new.
  • Tokio task-per-connection — Phase 0 spawns one task per connection; at 10k concurrent connections that's 10k tasks × ~2 KB stack = 20 MB. Acceptable at this scale; note for Phase 7 connection pooling.
  • syscall overhead — tokio::io::copy under hyper issues one write(2) per frame. Batch small frames with BufWriter; measure with strace -c.
  • HTTP/1.1 keep-alive — Phase 0 uses hyper_util::client::legacy::Client which does keep-alive by default; pool size 1. Ensure pool is reused across requests (static OnceLock<Client>).

12.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty body POST — Content-Length: 0; http_body_util::Empty → forward as-is; backend must handle.
  • Backend unreachable at startup — INFR_BACKEND_ADDR DNS fails; ingress starts anyway, returns 502 on first request; log at ERROR.
  • Malformed INFR_BACKEND_ADDR — non-URI string; Uri::from_str panics. Fix: validate at startup, exit 1 with message.
  • Client disconnects mid-stream — relay task receives BrokenPipe; cancel backend request if possible (Phase 7 cancellation); in Phase 0 let backend complete, discard bytes.
  • Concurrent requests to static backend — hyper client serializes if connection count=1; Phase 0 is single-backend single-connection; document as known limitation.
  • Response > 2 GB — http_body_util::BodyExt::collect() would OOM; Phase 0 must use streaming path exclusively, never buffer full response body.

12.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Backend TCP refused hyper::Error on connect All requests fail 502 Ingress still accepts; returns error Operator fixes INFR_BACKEND_ADDR; hot config reload Phase 2
Ingress bind fails (port in use) tokio::net::TcpListener::bind error at startup Process exits 1 No traffic served Change INFR_LISTEN_ADDR
OOM from buffered body Process killed by OOM killer All in-flight requests drop None in Phase 0 Streaming path prevents; set GOMEMLIMIT analog via ulimit
Panic in handler tokio::task panic; connection closed Single request axum catch-unwind per task Add tower::catch_panic middleware in Phase 1
Graceful shutdown timeout Log line shutdown_forced=true In-flight requests aborted Force-close after deadline Tune INFR_SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_MS

12.10 Operational Notes

  • Startup log: JSON line {"event":"infr_start","listen":$addr,"backend":$addr,"phase":0} at INFO.
  • Metrics stub: infr-telemetry exports requests_total{status} counter via Prometheus text at /metrics; Phase 0 only increments on response completion.
  • Tracing: tracing_subscriber::fmt with RUST_LOG env filter; request_id injected into every span via tracing::Span::current().record("request_id", ...).
  • Deployment: single binary infr-cli; no external state; config purely via env. Docker: FROM scratch + musl target (x86_64-unknown-linux-musl).
  • Rollback: stateless; revert binary, restart.
  • Compatibility: Phase 0 has no config file schema; forward-compat guaranteed since Phase 2 introduces TOML config with versioned schema_version field.

13. Phase 1 — Ingress — HTTP/1.1 + HTTP/2 + streaming (SSE/chunked) request parsing, Request model, graceful drain/shutdown

13.1 Objectives

  • Full production ingress in infr-ingress: HTTP/1.1 keep-alive, HTTP/2 multiplexed, gRPC framing (h2 transport), SSE (text/event-stream) and chunked streaming relay.
  • Parse incoming request into canonical Request struct: extract model, adapter, prompt_tokens, max_tokens, slo, priority, idempotent, stream from JSON body / gRPC message / query params.
  • Compute prefix_hash: PrefixHash from tokenized prompt prefix (via configurable hashing, Phase 1 uses FNV-64 over raw prompt bytes as placeholder).
  • Graceful drain: stop accepting new connections on shutdown signal; finish in-flight requests up to configurable deadline; drain counter exposed as metric.
  • Constraint: routing policy not yet wired (stub RoutingDecision { backend: BackendId(0), reason: RouteReason::Fallback }).
  • infr-ingress must compile and pass tests independent of infr-route/infr-queue.

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: ADR-03 (hyper server), ADR-24 (rustls TLS), P1 (backpressure propagated — I4), P2 (at-most-once — I3 idempotent field parsed here), ADR-10 (tracing spans), B7 (HTTP/2 flow-control)

File Responsibility
src/server.rs Dual-stack axum::serve (HTTP/1.1+2) via hyper_util::server::conn::auto::Builder; rustls TLS optional (INFR_TLS_CERT/KEY, plaintext default); routes /v1/completions, /v1/chat/completions, /v1/embeddings → handlers, /metrics → telemetry, /healthz
src/parse.rs parse_openai_request(): serde_json::from_slice into OpenAIRequestEnvelope; model→ModelId, adapter from x-infr-adapter header or base:adapter suffix; prompt_tokens word-approx (real tokenizer Phase 4); prefix_hash = FNV-64 over first min(512,N) prompt bytes; slo from slo_* fields/headers (defaults ttft_ms=5000, tpot_ms=200); priority from header/field (default Normal); idempotent from x-infr-idempotent header; stream from JSON field
src/grpc.rs parse_grpc_request(): application/grpc length-prefixed frame → InferRequest proto (prost stub); same field extraction as parse.rs
src/relay.rs relay_stream(): SSE via tokio_stream::StreamExt::map; chunked via http_body_util::BodyExt::frame() passthrough; byte-for-byte order, no buffering/reorder (Order invariant); ttft_ms recorded on first token frame
src/drain.rs DrainSignal (watch::Sender<bool>); SIGTERM/SIGINT → stop accept(), wait for in_flight_count: AtomicU32 → 0 or INFR_SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_MS; count incremented on handler entry, decremented by Drop guard InFlightGuard
src/middleware.rs tower layer order: CatchPanic → RequestId injection (x-request-id if absent) → Timeout (INFR_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS, default 300s) → TracingLayer (OTel span per request)

Tests: tests/parse_openai.rs (12 fixtures → exact field assert), tests/parse_grpc.rs (raw frame → fields), tests/relay_sse.rs (20-event mock stream, order + data: prefix), tests/graceful_drain.rs (10 concurrent, SIGTERM after 5 admitted, all complete), tests/timeout.rs (10s hang, 1s timeout → 504 within 1.1s).

13.3 Deliverable

  • infr-ingress binary (via infr-cli serve) accepts HTTP/1.1, HTTP/2, gRPC on same port.
  • Every inbound request parsed into Request with all fields populated; logged at TRACE.
  • SSE/chunked streams relayed without buffering; TTFT metric recorded.
  • Graceful drain: operator sends SIGTERM; no new connections accepted; in-flight complete; process exits 0.
  • /healthz returns {"status":"ok","phase":1}.

Check Threshold

13.6 Pitfalls

  • gRPC trailer frames dropped — gRPC uses HTTP/2 HEADERS frame with END_STREAM as trailers; hyper/axum may not relay trailers. Root cause: http::Response body does not expose trailers. Debug: grpc client sees unknown error. Fix: use hyper::body::Body::trailers() explicitly; wrap in axum::body::Body::new.
  • SSE flush starvation — TCP Nagle delays small SSE frames. Root cause: write() coalesces < 512-byte frames. Debug: high perceived TTFT despite fast backend. Fix: set TCP_NODELAY on accepted sockets; axum::body::Body writer flushes on each frame.
  • Prefix hash collision across models — same prompt bytes with different model → same PrefixHash; Phase 1 FNV-64 does not include ModelId. Root cause: hash input omits model. Fix in Phase 1: hash = fnv64(model_id_bytes ++ prompt_prefix_bytes).
  • Content-Length mismatch on stream — backend sends Content-Length: N but streams tokens; relay forwards header; client may cut off early. Root cause: content-length inaccurate for streaming. Fix: strip Content-Length header when stream=true.
  • H2 WINDOW_UPDATE deadlock — large request body + slow backend; client's h2 flow control window exhausted, blocks. Root cause: h2 default window 65 535 bytes. Fix: increase via hyper_util::server::conn::auto::Builder → http2_initial_connection_window_size(1<<20).
  • Timeout tower layer position — if Timeout wraps only handler (not connection), a slow client sending body can consume connection indefinitely. Root cause: tower::timeout::Timeout starts after body fully received. Fix: set axum::serve tcp_keepalive + http1_header_read_timeout on hyper builder.
  • In-flight counter leak on panic — InFlightGuard drop not called if handler panics without catch-unwind. Root cause: tokio task panic unwinds before guard drop in some configurations. Fix: CatchPanic layer must be outermost tower layer; verify guard drop in panic test.

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • Per-request allocation: serde_json::from_slice allocates Value tree; use serde_json::from_slice::<OpenAIRequestEnvelope> directly to avoid Value intermediate.
  • Header map clone: http::HeaderMap clone on each request to inject x-request-id; use insert in-place on the forwarded request, not on received.
  • String interning for ModelId: repeated ModelId(String::from("llama3")) per request; intern via Arc<str> stored in ModelId(Arc<str>) — single heap alloc per unique model name.
  • SSE frame copy: relay copies each SSE chunk into new Bytes; use Bytes::copy_from_slice only for prefix injection; otherwise pass through backend Bytes directly.
  • Tokio task spawn per request — unavoidable but minimize by not spawning sub-tasks within handler; keep single-task per connection model.
  • Tail latency from JSON parsing: p999 parse latency spikes if large system prompt in messages; cap prompt_tokens count at 128K tokens; reject above with 400 early.

13.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty messages array — prompt_tokens = 0; prefix_hash = PrefixHash(0); valid; backend decides.
  • max_tokens = 0 — valid for embedding requests; pass through.
  • Unicode in model name — ModelId must accept arbitrary UTF-8; reject non-printable control characters.
  • Duplicate x-request-id header — multiple values; take first; log warning.
  • HTTP/1.0 client — no keep-alive; handle Connection: close; drain after single request.
  • Concurrent SSE + non-stream on same h2 connection — h2 multiplexing handles independent streams; verify no cross-stream body leakage.
  • Extremely large max_tokens — e.g., 4_000_000; store as u32 (max ~4B); validate against model max_output_tokens in Phase 3; Phase 1: store verbatim.
  • Client sends Expect: 100-continue — hyper handles automatically; verify Phase 1 does not break by stripping the header before forward.
  • SIGTERM during h2 GOAWAY — concurrent shutdown + new h2 stream initiation; GOAWAY frame must be sent before closing listener; verify with h2spec.

13.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Parse error (malformed JSON) InferError::Internal; 400 to client Single request Ingress continues Client fixes payload
Backend connection refused during relay InferError::Upstream; 502 All requests to that backend Phase 0: all fail; Phase 2+: re-route Fix backend
Timeout exceeded tower::timeout fires; 504 Single request Others unaffected Tune INFR_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS
In-flight counter overflow (u32::MAX) Monotonic metric exceeds 4B Impossible in practice Saturating add + alert Restart; investigate leak
Drain timeout exceeded Log drain_forced=true; exit 1 Remaining in-flight aborted SIGKILL after deadline Increase INFR_SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_MS or fix slow backend
Panic in handler CatchPanic → 500; task restarted Single request Layer absorbs Check logs for panic trace
H2 stream reset by client h2::Error::Reason(CANCEL) Single stream Relay task cancelled Normal client behavior; no action

13.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics (all in infr_telemetry):
    • infr_requests_total{model, status, stream} — counter.
    • infr_request_duration_ms{model, quantile} — histogram (p50/p95/p99/p999).
    • infr_ttft_ms{model, quantile} — histogram.
    • infr_in_flight{model} — gauge from in_flight_count.
    • infr_drain_in_progress — gauge 0/1.
  • Tracing: tracing::span! per request with fields request_id, model, stream, priority; propagate via traceparent header (W3C trace context).
  • Structured logs: tracing_subscriber::fmt::json(); fields: timestamp, level, request_id, model, event.
  • Deployment: zero-downtime via SO_REUSEPORT; new process binds same port; old drains. Documented procedure in ops runbook.
  • Config reload: Phase 1 uses env-only config; no SIGHUP handling yet (Phase 2 introduces TOML + SIGHUP).
  • Compatibility: x-infr-* header namespace reserved; no conflict with OpenAI headers. gRPC service name infr.v1.InferService — stable, versioned.

14. Phase 2 — Backend registry + static config — replica set, model->replica map, adapter inventory, hot config reload

14.1 Objectives

  • Implement infr-registry crate: in-memory ReplicaSet keyed by BackendId; ModelRegistry (model → Vec<BackendId>); AdapterInventory (adapter → Vec<BackendId>); CapacityDescriptor per replica.
  • Load initial state from TOML config file (infr.toml); parse into typed structs; validate.
  • Hot reload: SIGHUP triggers atomic config swap via arc_swap::ArcSwap<Config>; no downtime; in-flight requests see old config until completion.
  • Expose read-only registry API for infr-route, infr-health, infr-dispatch via Arc<Registry>.
  • Phase 2 constraint: no health probing (static Health::Healthy for all), no LoadState updates (zeros).
  • infr-registry must compile independently; tested via unit + integration fixture tests.

14.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: ADR-14 (Arc<RwLock<T>> / ArcSwap for registry reads), I1 (single-writer registry invariant), ADR-12 (TOML config format), B3 (concurrent data structure discipline), P2 (at-most-once — adapter inventory correctness for routing)

File Responsibility
src/lib.rs RegistryHandle = Arc<ArcSwap<Registry>>; Registry { replicas: HashMap<BackendId,Replica>, model_index, adapter_index, capacity: HashMap<BackendId,CapacityDescriptor> }; CapacityDescriptor { max_batch, max_context_len, gpu_count, backend_type }; BackendType = VLlm|SGLang|Tgi|Custom
src/config.rs Config { schema_version, replicas: Vec<ReplicaConfig>, models: Vec<ModelConfig> }; load() reads + toml::from_str + checks schema_version==1; validate(): addrs parseable as Uri, no duplicate BackendId, every model-ref resolves, max_batch>=1, max_context_len>=512
src/registry.rs from_config() builds indices; replicas_for_model(), replicas_for_adapter(), replica(id), all_replicas(), capacity(id) accessors
src/reload.rs watch_sighup(): SignalKind::hangup() stream → Config::load → Registry::from_config → handle.store(Arc::new(..)); error → log + keep old registry, never partial-update; registry_reload_total{result} counter; ArcSwap::store is one atomic pointer swap (never torn)
src/watcher.rs Optional notify-crate file-watch (feature file-watch); 500ms debounce; same reload path as SIGHUP (for K8s ConfigMap mounts with no SIGHUP)
src/snapshot.rs snapshot() → RegistrySnapshot for /debug/registry: replica/model/adapter counts + per-replica {id, addr, models, health}
infr-cli serve --config <path> (default ./infr.toml) loads registry at startup; passes RegistryHandle to ingress + dispatch stubs

Tests: tests/load_config.rs (5 fixtures → parsed structs), tests/registry_index.rs (index correctness), tests/hot_reload.rs (tmpfile + SIGHUP → handle updated <200ms), tests/reload_error.rs (invalid TOML → old registry unchanged), tests/snapshot.rs (JSON fields present).

14.3 Deliverable

  • infr-cli serve --config infr.toml starts, loads registry, logs N replicas, M models, K adapters at INFO.
  • GET /debug/registry returns JSON snapshot of current registry state.
  • kill -HUP <pid> triggers hot reload; updated replica count logged; requests in-flight unaffected.
  • Phase 2 smoke test: two replicas in config; GET /debug/registry shows both; SIGHUP with one removed shows one.

14.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Threshold
Config load (5 fixtures) 100% parse correctly
Registry index correctness All replicas_for_model / replicas_for_adapter assertions pass
Hot reload latency (SIGHUP → RegistryHandle updated) < 200 ms
Hot reload under concurrent readers 0 torn reads (assert in 10 000-iteration test)
Invalid config reload Old registry preserved; error logged
File-watch reload (ConfigMap scenario) Triggers within debounce + 100 ms
/debug/registry snapshot Valid JSON; all replicas present
Config parse p99 latency (100-replica config) < 10 ms

14.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected
tests/fixtures/config_minimal.toml 1 replica, 1 model Parse OK; 1 entry in each index
tests/fixtures/config_full.toml 10 replicas, 5 models, 3 adapters All indices populated correctly
tests/fixtures/config_multi_adapter.toml Replica serving 3 adapters adapter_index has 3 entries
tests/fixtures/config_bad_schema_version.toml schema_version = 99 ConfigError::UnsupportedVersion
tests/fixtures/config_bad_addr.toml Replica addr = "not-a-uri" ConfigError::InvalidAddr
tests/fixtures/config_missing_model_ref.toml Replica references undefined model ConfigError::UnknownModel
tests/fixtures/config_duplicate_id.toml Two replicas same id ConfigError::DuplicateBackendId
tests/fixtures/config_v2_reload.toml Valid replacement config (10→8 replicas) After reload, 8 replicas in handle
tests/fixtures/config_concurrent_reload.toml Used in 10 000-iteration torn-read test Zero torn reads

14.6 Pitfalls

  • Torn read during reload with non-atomic data structure — using RwLock<Registry> with nested mutable fields; reader holds lock while writer swaps partial state. Root cause: multi-field update not atomic. Fix: ArcSwap<Registry> — build new Registry fully off-line, then single-pointer swap. Signal: torn read test detects impossible intermediate states.
  • Model reference not validated at load — replica references model = "gpt-4" not in models list; downstream routing panics. Root cause: missing cross-reference validation in validate(). Debug: panic in route crate at models_index[model_id]. Fix: validate() checks all refs; ConfigError::UnknownModel.
  • Adapter ID collision across models — two models use same AdapterId string for different LoRA weights; adapter_index maps both to same entry. Root cause: AdapterId is global, not scoped to model. Fix: scope adapter index as HashMap<(ModelId, AdapterId), Vec<BackendId>>; document in ADR.
  • SIGHUP lost during reload — rapid successive SIGHUPs; second signal arrives while first reload still in progress. Root cause: tokio signal stream buffers at most one pending signal. Fix: reload task drains signal stream, coalesces, runs latest config only; AtomicBool::compare_exchange guards re-entrant reload.
  • File-watch bounce loop — ConfigMap write triggers multiple Modify events in < 500 ms; without debounce, registry reloads N times. Root cause: editor/atomic rename triggers 2-3 events. Fix: 500 ms debounce via tokio::time::sleep; cancel-and-restart on new event.
  • Config file not UTF-8 — toml::from_str expects &str; std::fs::read_to_string returns Err(InvalidUtf8). Fix: catch and return ConfigError::InvalidEncoding; log path + error.
  • Registry snapshot serialization includes Instant — Replica.load.ewma_ttft_ms is fine, but snapshot includes last-seen timestamp as std::time::Instant. Root cause: Instant not Serialize. Fix: convert to UNIX millis (SystemTime::now() offset) in RegistrySnapshot.

14.7 Performance Concerns

  • HashMap lookup on hot path — replicas_for_model called per-request; HashMap<ModelId, Vec<BackendId>> with Arc<str> key; FxHashMap (rustc-hash) 2× faster for string keys. Use rustc-hash::FxHashMap in registry.
  • ArcSwap read cost — ArcSwap::load() is one atomic load + refcount bump; ~5 ns; negligible. Prefer load_full() only when holding across await points.
  • Config parse on large fleet — 500-replica config; toml::from_str allocates many String; parse in background task, not on signal handler thread.
  • Index rebuild on reload — for 500 replicas, rebuild is O(N); ~1 ms; acceptable; no incremental diff needed at this scale.
  • Clone of Vec<BackendId> per lookup — replicas_for_model returns &[BackendId]; callers must not hold reference across reload; document lifetime requirement.

14.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty replica list for a model — valid config: model exists, no replicas assigned; replicas_for_model returns &[]; router returns InferError::NoBackend.
  • Zero replicas total — config with replicas = []; valid parse; all requests fail NoBackend; log WARN at startup.
  • Config file deleted between loads — std::fs::read_to_string returns NotFound; treat as reload error, keep old config.
  • Replica address change on reload — existing in-flight request holds BackendId; dispatch uses BackendId to look up addr in registry; if old registry is released but dispatch still holds old Replica via Arc<Replica>, address remains valid. Invariant: Arc<Replica> in Lease pins old address.
  • max_context_len set below running request's prompt_tokens — Phase 2: no enforcement; Phase 5 admission control will enforce. Document as known gap.
  • 100 adapters per replica — HashSet<AdapterId> in Replica; lookup O(1); insertion O(100) at load; acceptable.
  • Schema version upgrade — schema_version = 2 in future; validate() returns ConfigError::UnsupportedVersion; router keeps serving on old config; operator must upgrade binary first.
  • TOML syntax error in reload — toml::de::Error; log with line/column; old config preserved.

14.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Config file not found at startup ConfigError::Io(NotFound); process exits 1 Service does not start None Fix path; restart
Validation error at startup ConfigError::*; process exits 1 with message Service does not start None Fix config
Hot reload error (bad TOML) Log ERROR registry_reload_failed; counter incremented Zero — old config active ArcSwap not updated Fix config; re-send SIGHUP
Reload tears reader state Torn-read test detects None in expected Some Data corruption ArcSwap prevents this N/A (architectural invariant)
Signal handler panics tokio task panics; no more reloads possible Cannot reload until restart Signal task respawnable Wrap watcher task in tokio::spawn retry loop
File-watch debounce drops valid update Registry stale Possibly stale routing Operator can SIGHUP manually Tune debounce; send manual SIGHUP
OOM during index rebuild Allocator returns None / process killed Service restart Registry rebuild is bounded Reduce replica count; increase node memory

14.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics:
    • infr_registry_replicas_total{model} — gauge per model.
    • infr_registry_adapters_total — gauge.
    • infr_registry_reload_total{result} — counter (ok / error).
    • infr_registry_last_reload_timestamp_seconds — gauge (Unix epoch float).
  • Debug endpoint: GET /debug/registry returns RegistrySnapshot JSON; gated by INFR_DEBUG_ENABLED=true; no auth in Phase 2 (add in Phase 9).
  • Config schema versioning: schema_version field in TOML; current 1; bump on breaking field rename; migration path: support N and N-1 in same binary.
  • Kubernetes deployment: ConfigMap volume mount → file-watch triggers reload; no SIGHUP needed. notify feature must be enabled in release build.
  • Structured log on reload: {"event":"registry_reload","replicas":N,"models":M,"adapters":K,"elapsed_ms":X} at INFO.
  • Rollback: TOML config is declarative; roll back by reverting ConfigMap or file; SIGHUP applies immediately.
  • Compatibility: BackendId(u32) is stable across reloads; operators must not reuse IDs for different replicas without full restart (ID reuse may confuse infr-health state machine in Phase 3).

15. Phase 3 — Health checking + load probing — heartbeats, queue-depth/KV-utilization scraping, EWMA load state, outlier ejection

15.1 Objectives

Goal Constraint
Heartbeat every replica on configurable interval (default 5 s) Must not add >1 ms to p99 routing decision
Scrape per-replica LoadState (queue_depth, kv_util, running_batch, inflight, ewma_ttft_ms) Scrape jitter ≤ ±200 ms to avoid thundering-herd
Maintain Health FSM: Healthy → Degraded → Ejected and recovery path Ejected replicas invisible to infr-route
Compute EWMA load score per replica; α configurable (default 0.2) New-sample weight applied at scrape time, not read time
Outlier ejection: eject replicas whose load score exceeds fleet mean by configurable σ-multiple (default 2.0) Ejection holds for minimum eject_hold_ms (default 30 s)
Expose HealthStore (Arc<RwLock<HashMap<BackendId, Replica>>>) to infr-route and infr-queue Readers never block writers beyond one RwLock cycle
Invariant: no Ejected replica appears in any RoutingDecision Enforced in infr-route, gated by Health check

Scope: infr-health crate exclusively. infr-registry provides initial Replica set; infr-health enriches LoadState and drives Health transitions. No routing logic here.

15.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: I2 (health probe liveness — no dispatch to Ejected), I5 (Lease released before Health update), ADR-09 (metrics facade for probe counters), ADR-13 (tokio::sync::watch for health broadcast), R4 (Envoy outlier detection FSM reference), B1 (Systems Performance — EWMA methodology)

File Responsibility
lib.rs Re-exports; HealthStore type alias Arc<RwLock<HashMap<BackendId,Replica>>>
prober.rs BackendProber: one tokio::task/BackendId (tracked in JoinSet); scrapes GET /metrics or /v1/load; parses queue_depth, kv_util_fraction, running_batch_size, inflight_requests; scrape_timeout_ms (default 2000) → consecutive_scrape_failures; ≥fail_threshold (default 3) → Degraded; per-task jitter via rand::gen_range(0..jitter_ms)
heartbeat.rs HeartbeatTask: GET /healthz or gRPC Check; stores RTT as ewma_rtt_ms (observability only); consecutive failures → Health::Ejected immediately (bypasses Degraded)
ewma.rs EwmaTracker — pure formula, no lock: updated = alpha·sample + (1−alpha)·prev (f32); applied to queue_depth, kv_util, ewma_ttft_ms (running_batch/inflight are instantaneous)
ejection.rs EjectionFsm, runs after each batch write: computes fleet μ,σ of ewma_ttft_ms over Healthy+Degraded; marks Ejected where ewma_ttft_ms > μ + σ_multiple·σ; holds eject_hold_ms (via ejected_at: Instant) before recovery eligible; recovers at ewma_ttft_ms < μ + recovery_fraction·σ (default 1.0) AND heartbeat passing; emits EjectionEvent{backend, reason, score, threshold}
store.rs HealthStore update cycle: write() lock, update LoadState+Health, release (target <50µs hold); infr-route takes read() per decision, snapshots into SmallVec<[Replica;32]>
config.rs HealthConfig: intervals, thresholds, α, σ-multiple

Observability hooks: health_state_change{backend,from,to,reason} event; scrape_duration_ms{backend} histogram; load_state_snapshot{backend,queue_depth,kv_util,ewma_ttft_ms} gauge batch.

Test harness: MockBackend (axum test server, configurable JSON LoadState, injectable failure modes: timeout/500/bad-JSON); HealthConfig::test_defaults() (intervals=100ms, fail_threshold=2, eject_hold_ms=500).

15.3 Deliverable

  • infr-health compiles; BackendProber + HeartbeatTask run against MockBackend fleet.
  • HealthStore reflects live LoadState within one scrape interval.
  • EjectionFsm promotes/demotes replicas; infr-route sees no Ejected entries.
  • Operators: GET /admin/health on infr-ingress returns HashMap<BackendId, Health + LoadState> JSON.
  • cargo test -p infr-health passes all unit + integration tests.

15.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
HealthStore read latency (p99) < 5 µs
Scrape-to-store propagation ≤ scrape_interval + 10 ms
EWMA correctness Unit test: 10-sample sequence matches analytic value ±1e-4
Ejection triggers Within 2 scrape cycles of replica load spike > μ+2σ
Recovery holds eject_hold_ms FSM stays Ejected for full hold duration
Heartbeat failure → Ejected Within fail_threshold missed heartbeats
Zero Ejected replicas in routing infr-route integration test: 10k routing decisions, 0 dispatches to ejected
Memory: HealthStore per-replica overhead < 256 bytes
cargo test -p infr-health 100% pass, no flaky tests
cargo clippy -p infr-health -- -D warnings Clean

15.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture file Purpose Expected output
fixtures/health/load_state_normal.json Valid scrape response; all fields present LoadState parsed; EWMA updated
fixtures/health/load_state_missing_kv.json kv_util field absent Field defaults to 0.0; no parse error
fixtures/health/load_state_malformed.json Invalid JSON (truncated) ScrapeError::ParseFailure; failure counter incremented
fixtures/health/heartbeat_timeout.toml Configures 0 ms scrape timeout consecutive_scrape_failures increments; Degraded after threshold

Replay: fixture runner re-drives EjectionFsm with timestamped LoadState snapshots; compares final Health state to expected column.

15.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug signal Recovery
EWMA warm-up bias Cold-start replica looks artificially fast (EWMA = 0) No initial sample → zero EWMA beats real replicas ewma_ttft_ms = 0 in metrics on new replica Seed EWMA with fleet mean at registration
Thundering-herd scrape All probers fire simultaneously → backend CPU spike Fixed interval without jitter Scrape histogram shows burst at interval boundary Add per-task startup jitter (rand offset)
Write-lock contention on large fleet Routing p99 spikes during scrape batch write All probers finish ≈ same time; all write health_store_write_wait_us histogram Stagger writes; batch into one writer task per interval
Ejection with no survivors All replicas ejected → 503 storm Correlated overload; σ-multiple too low ejected_count == total_count metric Floor: never eject last healthy replica per model
Stale HealthStore after probers crash Routes to overloaded backends Prober JoinSet task panicked silently prober_task_restart_total counter = 0 when expected > 0 Prober supervisor: restart on task exit
RTT measurement includes network jitter ewma_rtt_ms misleads load ranking RTT ≠ processing time Compare ewma_rtt_ms vs ewma_ttft_ms Use ewma_ttft_ms (from backend stats) for load decisions, not RTT
Recovery oscillation Replica flaps Ejected↔Healthy rapidly σ_multiple close to recovery_fraction health_state_change event rate spike Set recovery_fraction < σ_multiple with hysteresis gap ≥ 0.5

15.7 Performance Concerns

  • RwLock read path: Arc<RwLock<HashMap>> — read() is uncontended in steady state; contention only during write (1 writer per scrape cycle). Mitigation: dashmap::DashMap if p99 read > 1 µs under high parallelism.
  • Allocation per scrape: JSON parse allocates; reuse serde_json::from_slice on a pre-allocated Vec<u8> scratch buffer per prober task.
  • Task count: 1 prober + 1 heartbeat task per replica. 1000-replica fleet = 2000 tasks; tokio task overhead ~1 KB stack each. Acceptable; do not use OS threads.
  • EWMA computation: 3 float multiplies per metric per scrape; negligible.
  • EjectionFsm scan: O(N) over replica set per scrape cycle. N ≤ 10k; ~10 µs at 10k replicas. Acceptable. Avoid allocating sorted vec; compute running mean/variance in single pass.
  • Histogram cardinality: scrape_duration_ms has BackendId label; cap at 10k series or aggregate by model.
  • Clock source: tokio::time::Instant (monotonic); no SystemTime in hot path.

15.8 Edge Cases

Case Handling
Backend returns HTTP 200 but body is empty ScrapeError::EmptyBody; treat as parse failure
kv_util > 1.0 (backend bug) Clamp to [0.0, 1.0] before EWMA; emit kv_util_clamped counter
queue_depth wraps u32 (overflow) Saturate at u32::MAX; log warning
Replica removed from registry mid-probe Prober task receives cancellation token; drops gracefully
Clock skew between router and backend Scrape timestamps are router-local; backend timestamps used only for ordering within stream relay
Two probers race to write same BackendId Impossible by design: one prober task per BackendId; enforced by JoinSet key uniqueness
Backend metric endpoint changes URL backend_kind config drives endpoint; reload via infr-registry config reload signal
Fleet size = 1 Ejection disabled (σ undefined for N=1); replica always Healthy unless heartbeat fails

15.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
All probers hang (backend firewall) scrape_duration_ms p99 → timeout value; consecutive_scrape_failures across all backends HealthStore stale; routing uses last-known LoadState Stale TTL: if no update for stale_threshold_ms, mark Degraded Fix firewall; probers auto-recover on next scrape cycle
EjectionFsm panics Panic logged; FSM task exits; no further ejections No new ejections; degraded replicas not evicted Supervisor restarts FSM task Auto-restart within 1 s; emit ejection_fsm_restart_total
HealthStore write lock deadlock Extremely unlikely (no re-entrant lock usage); detected by tokio::time::timeout around lock acquire Full routing stall if lock never released Timeout on lock acquire → skip update cycle; alert Code review: verify no lock held across await points
Metric endpoint returns wrong model's stats LoadState for wrong model applied to replica Misrouting if wrong queue_depth Cross-validate model_id field in scrape response vs registry Add model_id assertion in prober; reject mismatch

Postmortem signal: health_state_change event stream replay gives full FSM history per replica.

15.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: infr_health_scrape_duration_ms (histogram), infr_health_kv_util (gauge), infr_health_queue_depth (gauge), infr_health_ejected_total (counter), infr_health_state{backend,state} (gauge 0/1).
  • Tracing: tracing::debug! per scrape (sampled 1/100); tracing::warn! on Degraded transition; tracing::error! on Ejected.
  • Structured log fields: backend_id, model_id, health_state, scrape_latency_ms, kv_util, ewma_ttft_ms.
  • Config reload: HealthConfig reloads via SIGHUP → infr-registry broadcasts new replica set; BackendProber adds/removes tasks for new/removed backends without restart.
  • Deployment: infr-health is an in-process library (no separate binary); no migration needed. New fields in LoadState are backward-compatible (serde default).
  • Rollback: Disabling ejection (σ_multiple = 999.0) is a safe config-only fallback.
  • Alerting thresholds: Page if ejected_fraction > 0.3; warn if stale_loadstate_count > 0 for > 60 s.

16. Phase 4 — Basic routing policies — round-robin, weighted, least-load (queue depth / EWMA TTFT), session affinity

16.1 Objectives

Goal Constraint
Implement PolicyEngine in infr-route with 4 strategies: RoundRobin, Weighted, LeastLoad, SessionAffinity Strategies are runtime-selectable per model via RoutingPolicy config
LeastLoad scores = f(queue_depth, kv_util, ewma_ttft_ms) with configurable weights Must filter Ejected replicas before scoring (Health invariant)
SessionAffinity maps session_id: Option<String> → BackendId via consistent hashing Sticky mapping has TTL; expiry falls back to LeastLoad
Every decision emits RoutingDecision { backend, reason } with RouteReason discriminant reason must be auditable: `least_load
infr-route reads HealthStore snapshot per decision; no mutable state in policy fn Policy fn is fn select(candidates: &[Replica], req: &Request, state: &RouteState) -> RoutingDecision
Fallback chain: primary policy → secondary (LeastLoad) → any Healthy → error Never returns Ejected; errors only if 0 Healthy replicas

Scope: infr-route crate (policy.rs, affinity.rs, score.rs). Reads HealthStore from infr-health. Writes RoutingDecision consumed by infr-dispatch.

16.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: P7 (Consistent Hashing paper — AffinityTable ring design), ADR-08 (ULID session key), ADR-16 (PrefixHash SHA-256), I2 (Health invariant — never route to Ejected), I9 (policy chain terminates), P1 (backpressure — fallback to LeastLoad under failure)

File Responsibility
lib.rs PolicyEngine, select() entry point
policy.rs enum RoutingPolicy { RoundRobin, Weighted, LeastLoad, SessionAffinity{fallback: Box<RoutingPolicy>} }; strategy dispatch
score.rs LeastLoadScorer: score = w_q·norm(queue_depth) + w_k·kv_util + w_t·norm(ewma_ttft_ms), lower wins; norm(x)=x/fleet_max(x) (0 if fleet_max=0); default weights (0.4,0.3,0.3); ties broken by BackendId
round_robin.rs AtomicUsize counter per (ModelId, instance); fetch_add(1, Relaxed) % len(candidates) over Healthy-filtered slice; Relaxed acceptable — fairness over strict order
weighted.rs WeightedSampler: alias table from Replica.capacity_weight: f32 (new field); O(N) build / O(1) sample; rebuilt on HealthStore generation-counter change
affinity.rs AffinityTable: DashMap<SessionId, (BackendId, Instant)> (SessionId=Arc<str>); Request.session_id: Option<Arc<str>> (new field); hit → check Instant+ttl, evict+fallback if expired/Ejected; miss → fallback policy + insert; TTL default 300s; lazy + 60s background sweep eviction
config.rs RouteConfig: per-model policy, score weights, affinity TTL

PolicyEngine::select(): (1) snapshot health_store → SmallVec<[Replica;32]> candidates where model==req.model && health==Healthy; (2) empty → Err(NoHealthyReplica); (3) dispatch to configured policy for one pick; (4) wrap as RoutingDecision{backend,reason}; (5) emit routing_decision event.

Observability: routing_decision{backend,model,reason,score,candidate_count} event; affinity_hit_total/affinity_miss_total/affinity_eviction_total counters; least_load_score_histogram{backend}.

Test harness: MockHealthStore (static Vec<Replica>, configurable Health/LoadState); property test (n replicas, random LoadState, verify min-score selection); affinity test (1000 same-session requests → same backend while healthy).

16.3 Deliverable

  • infr-route compiles; PolicyEngine::select() dispatches all 4 strategies.
  • Integration test: 10k requests through PolicyEngine with MockHealthStore; no Ejected backend selected.
  • RouteReason populated on every RoutingDecision.
  • Operators: GET /admin/routing-policy returns current per-model RoutingPolicy config.
  • cargo test -p infr-route passes.

16.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
PolicyEngine::select() p99 latency < 10 µs (1000-replica fleet, LeastLoad)
Zero Ejected replicas selected Property test: 100k decisions, 0 violations
RoundRobin distribution Chi-squared test: uniform within 5% over 10k requests, 10 replicas
Weighted distribution Chi-squared test: matches weights within 5% over 10k requests
LeastLoad selects min-score 100% of decisions in unit test select minimum-score replica
SessionAffinity stickiness 1000 same-session requests → same backend (healthy) 100%
Affinity TTL expiry Expired mapping falls back within 1 decision
RouteReason populated 100% of RoutingDecision have non-Fallback reason on healthy fleet
cargo test -p infr-route 100% pass
cargo clippy -p infr-route -- -D warnings Clean

16.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/route/replicas_10_equal.json 10 replicas, equal load RoundRobin: uniform; LeastLoad: tie → BackendId lowest
fixtures/route/replicas_skewed_load.json Replica 3 has queue_depth=100, others=5 LeastLoad never selects replica 3

fixtures/route/single_replica.json 1 Healthy replica All strategies select it; reason = fallback for RoundRobin

Regression: fixture replicas_skewed_load.json re-run on every CI push; output RoutingDecision.backend compared against golden file.

16.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug signal Recovery
Stale candidate snapshot Route to backend ejected post-snapshot HealthStore write races with select() read routing_decision{reason=ejected_post_select} counter (add in dispatch check) Re-validate Health in infr-dispatch before send; re-route if Ejected
Alias table not rebuilt on replica set change WeightedSampler samples removed backend index capacity_weight vector length mismatch Index out of bounds panic or wrong backend Rebuild alias table on HealthStore generation counter change
Affinity table memory growth OOM on long-lived sessions No eviction + unbounded DashMap affinity_table_size gauge Background sweep + max-entry cap (default 100k)
norm(x) division by zero NaN score → undefined selection All replicas have queue_depth=0 NaN in least_load_score_histogram Guard: if fleet_max == 0, score all replicas equally
AtomicUsize wraps at usize::MAX RoundRobin index computation wraps; skips index 0 for one cycle Overflow None visible Modulo arithmetic is correct post-wrap; cosmetic only
Session ID collision in hash Two distinct sessions route to same backend Hash collision in DashMap Unexpected co-location of unrelated sessions Use full Arc<str> key (exact equality), not hash-only routing
LeastLoad herding All routers select same "least loaded" backend simultaneously Fleet-level coordination failure One backend spikes; others idle Add small random jitter ±5% to score; or use power-of-two-choices

16.7 Performance Concerns

  • Candidate allocation: SmallVec<[Replica; 32]> avoids heap alloc for ≤32 replicas (common case).
  • HealthStore read lock: held only for snapshot copy; O(N) clone of Replica structs (~200 bytes each). For N=1000: ~200 KB copy per decision. Consider Arc<Replica> to reduce clone cost.
  • LeastLoad scoring: O(N) scan; N=1000 → ~1000 float ops; < 1 µs on modern hardware.
  • AffinityTable DashMap: shard-based concurrent map; hot session IDs may contend same shard. Monitor affinity_lock_wait_us.
  • Alias table rebuild: O(N) per rebuild; hold rebuild behind OnceCell + generation counter. Rebuild is rare (replica topology change).
  • RouteReason enum: repr(u8); no allocation.
  • Telemetry emission: non-blocking channel send (try_send); drop event if channel full (bounded); never block routing hot path.

16.8 Edge Cases

Case Handling
req.model has no registered replicas candidates.is_empty() → Err(NoHealthyReplica) immediately
req.session_id = None with SessionAffinity policy Skip affinity lookup; invoke fallback policy
capacity_weight = 0.0 for all replicas WeightedSampler treats as uniform
capacity_weight < 0.0 (config error) Reject at config parse; RouteConfig validation returns Err
Concurrent policy reload during routing Arc<PolicyConfig> swap; in-flight decisions use old config; no partial state
Request with priority = Emergency and LeastLoad Priority does not affect backend selection in this phase (priority used in infr-queue); document gap
All replicas Degraded (none Ejected) filter(Healthy) returns empty → Err(NoHealthyReplica); need to include Degraded as fallback

16.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
PolicyEngine panics tokio::task panics; upstream gets InferError Single request fails; task recovers on next call (stateless fn) No persistent state in select(); self-healing None needed; log + alert on panic rate
AffinityTable DashMap deadlock Extremely unlikely (no nested locks); detected by timeout on lookup Requests with session affinity stall Timeout on affinity lookup → fallback to LeastLoad Code review: no blocking ops inside shard lock
Wrong policy applied (config mismatch) Chi-squared distribution test fails in production canary Suboptimal routing; no correctness violation Policy config is per-model; wrong policy → poor latency, not errors GET /admin/routing-policy exposes live config; operator can reload
HealthStore unavailable (lock poisoned) Rust's PoisonError on read(); ? propagates All routing fails Treat poisoned lock as InferError::Internal; reset store Prober restarts store; operator alert

16.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: infr_route_decision_total{model,reason}, infr_route_candidate_count{model} (histogram), infr_route_select_duration_us (histogram), infr_route_affinity_table_size{model} (gauge).
  • Tracing: tracing::debug! per decision (sampled); span includes request_id, backend_id, reason, score.
  • Config reload: RouteConfig reloads on SIGHUP via arc-swap; PolicyEngine holds ArcSwap<RouteConfig>.
  • A/B policy testing: per-model policy allows canary: model_id = "llama3-canary" → LeastLoad; "llama3-prod" → Weighted.
  • Rollback: switch policy to RoundRobin (stateless, no table state) for safe fallback.
  • Deployment: no migration; AffinityTable is in-memory, ephemeral. Session affinity breaks on router restart — document as known limitation.
  • Alerting: page if routing_decision{reason=fallback} / total > 0.05 (5% fallback rate = replica shortage).

17. Phase 5 — Prefix / KV-cache-aware routing — per-replica prefix radix tree, longest-prefix match, cache-hit routing, prefix eviction tracking

17.1 Objectives

Goal Constraint
Implement PrefixTree (radix tree) in infr-route: maps PrefixHash → Set<BackendId> with recency Lookup must be O(L) where L = prefix length in tokens (not bytes)
Compute prefix_hash: PrefixHash in infr-ingress from tokenized prompt prefix (first K tokens or until cache boundary) K configurable (default 256 tokens); hash is Blake3 of token-id bytes
PolicyEngine::select() augmented: longest-prefix match → select backend with cache hit; fall back to LeastLoad RouteReason::PrefixHit on cache-hit selection
Track prefix evictions: backend signals eviction via DELETE /prefix/{hash} or scrape metric; PrefixTree removes that backend from set Stale entries (backend evicted but not yet notified) → routed to backend that re-computes prefix; no correctness violation
PrefixTree is shared across all routing decisions; Arc<RwLock<PrefixTree>> Write lock held < 100 µs; updates batched if possible
On completed request, infr-dispatch reports (request_id, prefix_hash, backend_id) back to PrefixTree to register new cache entries Post-request registration, not pre-routing
Recency: each (PrefixHash, BackendId) entry stores last_seen: Instant; expired after prefix_ttl (default 600 s) TTL eviction runs in background; does not block routing

Scope: infr-route/src/prefix_tree.rs (new module); infr-ingress adds prefix_hash computation; infr-dispatch adds post-completion callback. infr-health scrape extended to pull cached_prefix_hashes list (optional; push model preferred).

17.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: P3 (SGLang RadixAttention — PrefixTree design inspiration), P2 (PagedAttention — KV-cache block vocabulary), ADR-16 (SHA-256/Blake3 for PrefixHash), I6 (PrefixTree consistency — soft state only), P9 (FIFO Queues — eviction policy comparison), R2 (SGLang radix_cache.py reference)

Types: PrefixHash([u8;32]) (Blake3 of infr-core) computed in infr-ingress as Blake3::hash(token_ids[..min(K,len)]). PrefixTree { inner: HashMap<PrefixHash,PrefixEntry>, token_index: RadixNode, prefix_ttl: Duration }; PrefixEntry { backends: SmallVec<[(BackendId,Instant);4]> }. RadixNode { children: HashMap<u32,(Vec<u32>,Box<RadixNode>)>, hash: Option<PrefixHash> } operates over token-id sequences (key = Vec<TokenId>), not byte strings; insert does standard edge-splitting, lookup returns the deepest node with hash.is_some(). API: register(tokens,hash,backend), longest_match(tokens) -> Option<(PrefixHash,Vec<BackendId>)>, evict_backend(hash,backend), evict_expired(now) -> usize.

Step Detail
PolicyEngine::select() integration If req.prefix_hash set and longest_match returns Some((hash,backends)): intersect with Healthy candidates; non-empty → min-LeastLoad-score pick, reason=PrefixHit; empty (all ejected) → fall through to LeastLoad
infr-ingress/parse.rs Tokenize first K tokens (model tokenizer or byte-level fallback); compute+attach PrefixHash; store prompt_tokens: Arc<[u32]> (new Request field); tokenizer default tiktoken-style BPE via tokenizers crate
infr-dispatch/relay.rs post-completion On final token: send PrefixRegistration{prefix_hash,backend_id} via mpsc::Sender; writer task drains channel, batch-inserts, one write-lock per batch (≤10ms coalesce window)
infr-health/prober.rs eviction Scrape response evicted_prefix_hashes: Vec<String> → EvictBatch{backend,hashes} to eviction channel (backend-push POST /prefix/evict variant rejected, ADR note)
Background TTL sweep tokio::spawn, every ttl_sweep_interval (default 60s): write-lock, evict_expired(now), release; emits prefix_eviction_count

Observability: prefix_hit_total{model}/prefix_miss_total{model} counters; prefix_tree_size{model} gauge; prefix_match_depth{model} histogram; prefix_registration_latency_us histogram.

Test harness: unit (1000-entry insert, longest_match correctness), integration (2-backend mock, 500 shared-prefix requests → ≥90% same-backend), eviction (register→evict→no-longer-returned).

17.3 Deliverable

  • PrefixTree compiles; unit tests pass.
  • PolicyEngine::select() uses PrefixHit path when prefix is cached.
  • infr-ingress computes and attaches prefix_hash to every request.
  • infr-dispatch registers prefix on completion.
  • GET /admin/prefix-tree?model=X returns node count, top-10 hot prefixes (by backend count).
  • cargo test -p infr-route passes including prefix tests.

17.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
longest_match latency (p99) < 5 µs (1000 registered prefixes, L=256 tokens)
Prefix routing correctness 100% of requests with registered prefix route to a backend in the hit-set (if Healthy)
Cache-hit rate under shared-prefix workload ≥ 80% hit rate (10 replicas, 500 requests sharing 128-token prefix)
Eviction propagation Backend eviction removes backend from prefix set within 1 scrape cycle
TTL sweep Expired entries removed within ttl + sweep_interval
Zero routing to Ejected via prefix Property test: 100k decisions, 0 violations
PrefixTree memory < 1 MB for 10k registered prefixes
cargo test -p infr-route prefix 100% pass
prefix_hash always set in Request infr-ingress integration test: 0 requests with prefix_hash = None

fixtures/prefix/longest_match_sequence.jsonl 20 token sequences of increasing length longest_match returns deepest registered ancestor hash per row
fixtures/prefix/evict_backend.json Register prefix on 2 backends; evict 1 Lookup returns only remaining backend
fixtures/prefix/ttl_expiry.json Register with prefix_ttl = 100 ms; lookup after 200 ms Empty result (entry expired)
fixtures/prefix/empty_prefix.json prompt_tokens = [] longest_match returns None; no routing via prefix
