23.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
WFQ virtual finish time overflow starvation of tenants with large vft values vft accumulates without normalization when queues drain tenant_vft gauge shows diverging values normalize vft: set tenant_vft = global_vft when tenant queue empties
Token bucket refill drift under clock skew inaccurate rate limiting AtomicU64 timestamp using Instant; platform clock jump rate_limit_total count anomaly use monotonic clock exclusively; add jitter tolerance
Scale-up signal storm external autoscaler receives hundreds of Up signals missing cooldown on evaluation loop scale_signal_emitted_total spike enforce cooldown state per model; de-duplicate signals
Quota misconfiguration zeros out tenant tenant gets 0 tokens_per_s → all requests 429 operator typo in quota config rate_limit_total{tenant}=100% validate quota in config parser; require tokens_per_s > 0
WFQ underweight tenant starvation low-weight tenant TTFT → ∞ under saturation no minimum service guarantee tenant_queue_depth{low_weight} grows unbounded add minimum_weight floor (0.1% of total) in WFQ config
Rate limit bypass via default tenant tenants without quota mapping bypass limits missing TenantId → default has unlimited quota requests_total{tenant=default} anomaly set default quota to conservative limit; require explicit tenant provisioning
Autoscale signal to webhook fails scale never happens; cluster unresponsive network partition to webhook endpoint scale_signal_emitted_total increments but no actual scaling circuit-breaker on WebhookSink; log failure; fall back to local log sink

23.7 Performance Concerns

  • Token bucket try_admit is on the hot path (every request); must be lock-free: AtomicF32 CAS loop; ≤ 1 µs per call.
  • DashMap<(TenantId, ModelId), TokenBucket> shard count = 32; key interning via TenantId(Arc<str>) avoids per-call string alloc.
  • WFQ min-heap: BinaryHeap<Reverse<(VirtualTime, RequestId)>>; protected by Mutex; lock held only during enqueue/dequeue, not during dispatch.
  • Autoscale evaluation loop: 10s interval; reads AtomicU64 counters snapshot; ≤ 10 µs per evaluation; no blocking I/O on hot path (webhook emit is spawned).
  • Per-tenant telemetry: one AtomicU64 per counter per tenant; avoid per-request HashMap lookup by pre-indexing tenant counters at admission.
  • TenantId interning: global DashSet<Arc<str>>; first-seen tenants allocated once; subsequent requests share Arc.
  • Scale signal JSON serialization: serde_json on a small struct (< 200 bytes); < 5 µs; spawned task avoids blocking scheduler.

23.8 Edge Cases

  • Tenant quota reduced to 0 mid-flight: in-flight requests complete; new requests immediately 429; no disruption to other tenants.
  • All tenants simultaneously at quota: WFQ still operates on admitted queue; total throughput = sum of quotas; no request starvation within quota.
  • max_inflight reached but token bucket has capacity: admission blocked by inflight check; quota_exceeded_total increments; not a rate-limit 429 (different error code: 503 with Retry-After).
  • Config hot-reload adds new tenant: new TokenBucket created lazily on first request; initial tokens = burst (generous start).
  • Config hot-reload removes tenant: existing inflight complete; new requests from that tenant map to default; no panic.
  • Scale-down signal during active scale-up: scale-down blocked by cooldown; correct by design; log scale_down_suppressed_by_cooldown.
  • Weight sum = 0 (all tenants weight 0): fallback to equal weights; log warning.
  • WfqScheduler empty under zero load: next_request() returns None; scheduler sleeps until notified; no spin.

23.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery
WFQ scheduler deadlock all requests queued; no dispatch; tenant_queue_depth grows all tenants watchdog: if wfq_dispatch_idle_s > 5 and queue non-empty → alert force-drain queue; restart scheduler task
TokenBucketMap memory leak (tenants never evicted) memory growth; DashMap size unbounded router memory LRU eviction for tenants inactive > 1h add TTL eviction to TokenBucketMap
Webhook sink unavailable long-term no scaling response; cluster under/over-provisioned cluster capacity emit to local log; alert scale_sink_failure_total restore webhook; replay buffered signals (ring buffer, last 100)
Quota config parse failure on reload old quotas remain active; operator change ignored quota enforcement parse error logged; old config retained; config_reload_error_total fix config; re-issue SIGHUP
AtomicF32 CAS spin under extreme contention CPU saturation on admission thread all tenant admission profile: rate_limiter_cas_retry_total histogram shard TokenBucket by tenant hash; reduce contention

23.10 Operational Notes

  • Prometheus metrics per tenant: requests_total{tenant}, tokens_generated_total{tenant}, rate_limit_total{tenant}, tenant_inflight{tenant}, tenant_queue_depth{tenant}.
  • Alert rules: rate_limit_total{tenant} / requests_total{tenant} > 0.5 → quota too tight; tenant_queue_depth > 100 → under-capacity for tenant; scale_signal_emitted_total{direction=Up} → scaling event.
  • GET /admin/quotas → JSON of all tenant quotas + current token bucket state.
  • GET /admin/scale-log → last 100 ScaleSignal events (ring buffer).
  • Tracing: span admission_check with tenant_id, admit_result, tokens_remaining attributes.
  • Config: autoscale thresholds in infr-core::SchedConfig { up_queue_thresh: u32, down_queue_thresh: u32, up_load_thresh: f32, down_load_thresh: f32, up_slo_miss_thresh: f32, down_slo_miss_thresh: f32, scale_up_cooldown_s: u64, scale_down_cooldown_s: u64, eval_interval_s: u64 }.
  • Deployment: quotas pre-loaded from quotas.toml at startup; default tenant always present with conservative defaults.
  • Rollback: set all quotas to high values; scale signals switch to Hold; no restart required.
  • Migration: adding TenantId to Request is a breaking API change; provide migration shim that injects TenantId from HTTP header X-Tenant-ID or JWT claim; default to "anonymous".

24. Phase 12 — Observability — per-route/per-model metrics, cache-hit rate, queue depth, TTFT/TPOT histograms, distributed tracing

24.1 Objectives

Deploy the full production telemetry pipeline in infr-telemetry, instrumenting every stage of the canonical data path: Prometheus-compatible /metrics, OTLP spans per request, structured JSON logs at every decision boundary, per-model/adapter/backend/route granularity for latency/throughput/error/resource metrics, and zero observable P99 regression from telemetry overhead (budget ≤200µs added per request). Scope: infr-telemetry shared crate + instrumentation hooks in infr-ingress, infr-queue, infr-route, infr-dispatch, infr-sched, infr-health, infr-registry. Constraints: all recording non-blocking (lock-free counters / thread-local aggregation, async flush); span context propagated across await points via tokio::task_local! or explicit Context threading; zero heap allocation on the hot path (pre-allocated label sets, atomic counters); cardinality cap of 10k distinct label-set values per metric, enforced at registration.

24.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: ADR-09 (metrics crate facade + Prometheus exporter), ADR-10 (opentelemetry + tracing-opentelemetry), ADR-11 (structured logging via tracing-subscriber JSON), B1 (Systems Performance — metric methodology, histograms), P11 (Network-Load Imbalance — cardinality explosion warning applied here)

File Responsibility
metrics.rs Registry wraps prometheus::Registry + opentelemetry_sdk::metrics::MeterProvider; pre-registers all descriptors at startup (panics on duplicate name); MetricHandles gives typed handles (no repeated label-lookup on hot path); LabelSetGuard (Arc<DashMap<SmallVec<[Label;8]>,()>>) enforces the cardinality cap
tracing.rs TracingConfig{otlp_endpoint,sampling_ratio,service_name}; init via opentelemetry_otlp::new_pipeline().tracing().install_batch(Tokio); SpanGuard RAII records RouteReason/BackendId/RequestId/status on drop; span hierarchy infer.request → infer.queue → infer.route → infer.dispatch → infer.stream; injects x-request-id/traceparent into backend requests
log.rs tracing_subscriber JSON formatter (request_id,model,backend_id,route_reason,latency_ms,error); levels TRACE=token relay, DEBUG=routing decisions, INFO=shed/eject, WARN=circuit-open/degraded, ERROR=dispatch failure; macros log_route_decision!(), log_shed!()
scrape.rs MetricsScrapeHandler (axum, GET /metrics, Prometheus text encoding); HealthHandler (GET /healthz → 200/503 on readiness)

Instrumentation hooks: infr-ingress records infr_request_total + starts root span on arrival; infr-queue records infr_queue_depth/infr_queue_shed_total/infr_queue_admit_latency_us; infr-route records prefix hit/miss + route.reason span attribute + infr_route_cache_hit_rate; infr-dispatch records TTFT (Instant at dispatch send → elapsed at first chunk), TPOT (rolling inter-chunk delta), retry/hedge, E2E on stream close; infr-health updates infr_backend_kv_util/queue_depth/ewma_ttft_ms/health per probe; infr-sched emits autoscale-signal counter + fairness quota gauge per cycle.

Metric inventory (all pre-registered):

Metric name Type Labels Unit
infr_request_total Counter model, adapter, route_reason, status requests
infr_request_duration_ttft_ms Histogram model, adapter, backend_id ms

Histogram buckets (non-default): TTFT [1,2,5,10,20,50,100,200,500,1000,2000,5000]ms (12 buckets); TPOT [0.1,0.5,1,2,5,10,20,50,100]ms; E2E [10,50,100,500,1000,5000,30000,120000]ms; admit latency [1,5,10,50,100,500]µs.

TTFT is timestamped at dispatch send (Instant::now() in Lease; elapsed at first chunk); TPOT is a rolling inter-chunk delta (prev_token_ts per-stream state).

24.3 Deliverable

  • infr-telemetry crate compiles; all 20+ metrics pre-registered; /metrics serves Prometheus text format; OTLP trace export verified against a local Jaeger/Tempo instance.
  • Every crate instruments its data path (cargo test --workspace passes with telemetry enabled); log_route_decision! emits a JSON log line per routing decision in DEBUG mode.
  • Grafana dashboard JSON (infr-bench/dashboards/) covers request rate, TTFT P50/P95/P99 per model, queue depth per model, cache hit rate per model, backend health heatmap, retry/shed rate.

24.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Fix
Span context lost across tokio::spawn Child spans have no parent; traces disconnected in Jaeger opentelemetry::Context is thread-local; not auto-propagated into spawned tasks Missing parent_span_id in OTLP export Capture Span::current() before spawn; pass into task; span.in_scope(|| ...)
Histogram bucket mismatch (router vs backend) Router TTFT P99 disagrees with backend-reported P99 Different bucket boundaries and measurement points (router = full RTT, backend = GPU compute) Divergent P99 dashboards Document measurement points; keep bucket boundaries identical; expose route_overhead_ms = router_ttft - backend_ttft
Lock contention on prometheus::Registry P99 metric-increment latency spikes to 5+ms at 10k RPS Registry::gather() mutex contends with hot-path recording Blocking-thread count rises during scrapes Atomic counters on hot path; gather only at scrape time
TPOT skew on buffered streaming TPOT appears artificially high BufReader/hyper body buffers multiple tokens before yield; delta inflated Bimodal TPOT histogram Measure at TCP recv layer, not post-buffer; label as "client-observed TPOT"
Cardinality explosion from user-supplied model names /metrics scrape returns 100s of MB; scraper OOM Untrusted model label used unnormalized infr_metric_cardinality_exceeded_total Validate ModelId against registry at ingress; reject unknown with 400 before recording
OTLP exporter blocks on shutdown Router shutdown hangs 30+s draining span buffer shutdown_tracer_provider() blocks until flush completes Shutdown latency alert BatchConfig::max_export_timeout=2s; shutdown in spawn_blocking with hard timeout
Gauge staleness for ejected backends infr_backend_health still Healthy minutes after ejection Probe task panicked silently, gauge updates stopped infr_health_probe_task_restarts_total static Watchdog restarts probe tasks; emit restart counter

24.7 Performance Concerns

Counters use AtomicU64::fetch_add(1, Relaxed) (no mutex); prometheus::Histogram::observe takes a per-core shard lock, verified <1µs under contention; LabelSet structs pre-allocated per (model,backend) at replica registration (never String-alloc per request); /metrics encoding runs on spawn_blocking (never blocks the async runtime); OTLP batch size 512 spans (2048 above 5k RPS to cut export syscalls); DEBUG routing-decision logs are token-bucket rate-limited (1000 events/s per label) to prevent amplification under load; cardinality-guard DashMap uses 64 shards and is checked only at label registration, never per-increment; span-guard overhead capped at 3 stack frames (verified via cargo flamegraph).

24.8 Edge Cases

Case Handling
Request completes before TTFT recorded (empty response) Record TTFT = 0; increment infr_dispatch_empty_response_total
Backend streams tokens out of order (chunked encoding bug) Order invariant violation; log ERROR; record infr_dispatch_order_violation_total; do not reorder
OTLP endpoint unreachable at startup Log WARN; continue without tracing; retry connection every 30 s
Clock skew: Instant overflow (uptime > 584 years) Rust Instant is monotonic and wraps safely; no action needed
Metric scrape during rolling restart Scrape may see partial state; document as expected; use stale marker in Prometheus staleness protocol
RequestId collision (ULID monotonic overflow) Probability ~0 in practice; assert uniqueness in tests; log WARN if collision detected
Zero-token response (model returns empty) TPOT histogram: no observations; TTFT recorded; infr_request_total{status="empty"} incremented
Label value contains \n or " Sanitize to _ before recording; log DEBUG
Trace context header malformed (invalid traceparent) Ignore; start new root span; log DEBUG
Concurrent scrape + registry mutation (new model added) prometheus::Registry internal mutex handles; no race

24.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery
Metrics endpoint down Scrape returns non-200/times out; alert within 2 scrape intervals (30s default) Observability loss only; serving unaffected /metrics on separate axum route; scrape-handler panic caught by axum middleware Automatic — health probe restarts handler; no counter state lost (in-memory); infr_scrape_errors_total
OTLP exporter crash infr_otlp_export_errors_total; spans drop silently Tracing blind; metrics unaffected Ring-buffered spans (default 8192 slots), oldest dropped on overflow Reconnect with exponential backoff (max 60s); alert on infr_otlp_dropped_spans_total > 0
Gauge stuck (stale value) infr_backend_health{backend_id} unchanged for >3× probe interval Stale routing signal for one backend infr_health_probe_task_restarts_total incremented on crash Watchdog restarts task within 5s; gauge updates; check restart-counter trend postmortem
Cardinality explosion /metrics body > 10MB; scraper memory spikes Prometheus scrape degradation Cardinality guard blocks new label sets; existing series unaffected Operator identifies offending label via infr_metric_cardinality_exceeded_total{metric}; fixes upstream normalization

24.10 Operational Notes

Prometheus: 15s scrape interval, 15d histogram retention, recording_rules for P50/P95/P99 TTFT/TPOT per model. Grafana: ship infr-bench/dashboards/infr-overview.json (request rate, error rate, TTFT heatmap, queue depth, cache hit rate, backend health, retry/shed rates). Alerting (alerts.yaml): infr_queue_depth > 500 for 2min → PagerDuty; infr_dispatch_circuit_open_total rate > 0 → Slack; infr_backend_health==2 (Ejected) > 5min → PagerDuty; TTFT P99 > SLO → PagerDuty. INFR_OTLP_ENDPOINT env var (disabled by default, enable in staging/prod). Log levels: INFO in production, DEBUG in debug builds, never TRACE in production (token-relay volume). Rolling restarts: Prometheus staleness protocol handles scrape gaps, no migration needed. Compatibility: metric names stable once published (additive-only; label renames need a dual-emit deprecation period). Capacity: 10k active series × 15d retention ≈ 2GB Prometheus storage. Rollback: telemetry is purely additive — INFR_TELEMETRY_ENABLED=false removes overhead with no functional regression.

25. Phase 13 — Conformance, load + chaos testing, benchmarks — slow/failed backends, differential vs naive LB, throughput/tail-latency benches

25.1 Objectives

Validate correctness of all invariants (health, order, at-most-once, bounded-queue, lease, decision auditability) under adversarial conditions; quantify throughput ceiling and P99/P999 tail latency vs. a naive round-robin baseline; expose differential behavior (prefix-cache-aware vs. random, least-load vs. round-robin, admission control on/off); run a chaos suite (slow/failed backends, network partition, backend restart, cascade overload, clock skew); establish CI regression gates (block merge if throughput drops >5% or P99 rises >10%). Scope: primarily infr-bench (chaos.rs, differential.rs, conformance.rs) plus workspace-level tests/. Constraints: all chaos tests hermetic (mock backend pool, no real GPUs); benchmark numbers reproducible ±5% (pinned CPU affinity, isolated machine); no test >10 min wall-clock in CI (tokio::time::timeout).

25.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: ADR-19 (criterion micro-benchmarks + infr-bench load/chaos), ADR-18 (proptest + cargo-fuzz for invariant fuzzing), ADR-20 (GitHub Actions CI performance regression gate), B1 (Systems Performance — benchmark methodology, percentiles), P11 (Network-Load Imbalance — benchmark differential validates routing fairness), I4/I5/I3 (invariants verified under chaos conditions)

File Responsibility
mock_backend.rs MockBackend{latency_dist, failure_rate, kv_util, queue_depth, inject_slow_after}; LatencyDist enum (Fixed/Normal/Bimodal/Spike); OpenAI-compatible streaming HTTP server via axum, admin API set_latency/set_failure_rate; tracks tokens/requests/drops via GET /admin/stats
load_gen.rs LoadGen{rps, duration, prompt_dist, model, stream}; PromptDist enum (Uniform/ShareGPT/PrefixHeavy); async reqwest client pool; records per-request TTFT/TPOT/E2E/status into BenchResult (percentiles + error_rate + shed_rate)
conformance.rs Starts router + mock pool, fires targeted sequences, asserts the 10 invariant tests (table below)
chaos.rs ChaosScenario enum; ChaosRunner orchestrates scenario + load gen + invariant assertions (variants below)
differential.rs DifferentialBench runs two router configs in parallel on identical mock backends/seed, compares BenchResult across 5 hypothesis pairs (below)
throughput.rs Throughput sweep 10→10000 RPS (goodput/error/knee point); tail-latency bench at 80% knee (P50/P95/P99/P999 TTFT/TPOT); memory bench via /proc/self/status/sysinfo every 5 s
regression.rs Stores BenchResult JSON to baseline/results.json on --update-baseline; CI fails if p99_ttft > baseline×1.10 or rps_actual < baseline×0.95
tests/integration_conformance.rs tokio::test spins up router + mock pool, runs conformance suite, teardown on panic via Drop
tests/chaos_integration.rs Fast chaos-scenario subset for CI; full suite runs nightly
Test name Invariant checked Assertion
health_invariant_ejected_never_dispatched Health invariant 0 requests dispatched to ejected backend after ejection
order_invariant_stream_bytes Order invariant Client receives tokens in exact backend emission order
at_most_once_non_idempotent At-most-once Non-idempotent request reaches ≤1 backend even with retry enabled
at_most_once_hedge_idempotent Hedging safety Idempotent request may reach 2 backends; only first response returned
bounded_queue_no_oom Bounded-queue Queue at capacity + 1000 excess requests → all shed; RSS stable
lease_release_on_timeout Lease invariant Backend accounting freed within 1 s of deadline
route_reason_audit Decision auditability Every RoutingDecision has non-Unknown RouteReason
prefix_cache_hit_routing Prefix routing Requests with shared prefix routed to backend with prefix hit ≥ 80%
adapter_resident_routing Adapter routing Requests with adapter routed to backend with adapter loaded = 100%
session_affinity Affinity Consecutive requests same session → same backend ≥ 95%

ChaosScenario variants (orchestrated by ChaosRunner): SlowBackend(frac,ms) — X% of backends respond with latency +ms via admin API set_latency; FailedBackend(frac) — X% backends return 503 via set_failure_rate=1.0; NetworkPartition(backend_ids) — router cannot reach backends via iptables rule or mock TCP drop; BackendRestart(id,after_ms) — backend process restarts after N ms (kill + restart mock HTTP server); CascadeOverload — all backends saturated simultaneously (queue_depth > max on all); SlowDrainDeath — backend accepts connections but never responds (TCP accept, no write); PartialStream — backend sends 50% tokens then hangs (mid-stream hang injection); ClockSkew(delta_ms) — system time jumps forward/backward via a mock SystemTime injected-clock trait.

DifferentialBench hypothesis pairs (both configs run in parallel, same seed): prefix-cache-aware vs. random (cache hit rate +X%, TTFT improvement ≥ 15%); least-load vs. round-robin (P99 E2E improvement ≥ 10% under imbalanced load); admission control ON vs. OFF (under overload: error_rate lower, shed_rate higher, no OOM); hedging ON vs. OFF, idempotent (P999 TTFT improvement, throughput cost ≤ 5%); disaggregated prefill/decode vs. unified (TTFT improvement ≥ 20% for long prompts).

25.3 Deliverable

  • infr-bench binary compiles; subcommands conformance (all 10 tests, exits 0), chaos --scenario all (pass/fail per scenario), differential --pair <name> (DifferentialResult JSON), throughput --ramp (sweep CSV + knee point), tail-latency --duration 600s (P50/P95/P99/P999 TTFT/TPOT).
  • Regression baseline stored in infr-bench/baseline/results.json; CI gate compares on every PR.
  • Grafana dashboard infr-bench/dashboards/bench-results.json visualizes historical benchmark trends.

Check Threshold
All 10 conformance tests pass 100% pass rate; 0 flaky in 3 runs
health_invariant_ejected_never_dispatched 0 dispatches to ejected backend in 10k-request run
at_most_once_non_idempotent 0 duplicate dispatches in 10k-request run
bounded_queue_no_oom RSS increase < 10 MB at queue saturation

P999 TTFT < 500 ms at 80% of knee RPS

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Fix
Flaky conformance tests from timing deps health_invariant test occasionally dispatches to ejected backend Ejection propagates async; small window before health update applied Arc<RwLock<Health>> read fence before dispatch, or assert with ≤1 grace request; add retry counter to output
Mock latency unrepresentative of real GPU Differential gains diverge from production; TPOT too uniform Synthetic latency vs. real CUDA scheduling variance/bandwidth saturation Parameterize LatencyDist::Bimodal from production traces; document assumption
Throughput knee shifts with OS scheduling CI knee 4000 RPS vs. dev machine 7000 RPS; gate misfires tokio thread count, CPU affinity, socket buffers differ Per-machine-class baseline (results-{class}.json); dedicated pinned CI runner
Chaos NetworkPartition leaves stale iptables rules Subsequent tests fail connection refused; intermittent CI failures Drop cleanup not reached on panic scopeguard::defer! cleanup, per-test network namespaces, or mock TCP drop
Load-gen connection pool exhaustion Errors at >2000 RPS though router isn't saturated Default reqwest::Client pool too small (10/host) Set pool_max_idle_per_host(200); document client config
Differential bench ordering bias Config A always warm (PrefixTree, conn pool) vs. B Sequential execution warms caches for A first Randomize order; run each pair 3× and average; reset backends via admin API
RSS measurement noise from allocator RSS spikes 50 MB at load-gen startup, false OOM assertion jemalloc/mimalloc eager arena reservation Measure after 60 s warm-up; use delta RSS vs. stable baseline
PartialStream hang test leaks FDs After 100 runs, process hits FD limit, EMFILE Mock holds TCP conn open; OS keeps CLOSE_WAIT until mock drops Mock closes connection on timeout; assert FD count in teardown

25.7 Performance Concerns

Load-gen (reqwest) overhead (~50 µs/request) and mock-backend (axum) overhead (~20 µs/request) are both negligible vs. simulated latency but should be subtracted when comparing to direct backend measurement. Histogram percentile computation (hdrhistogram) is O(1) per sample, O(buckets) at report time — no hot-path concern. Chaos injection latency (~200 µs admin API round trip) should precede load-gen start to avoid a startup transient. Running two router instances for differential benchmarks competes for CPU on one machine — pin each to separate cores via taskset, or run sequentially with identical mock state. Regression baseline JSON (~50 KB) is committed directly (no LFS needed). Conformance tests parallelize via tokio::join! with distinct port ranges to avoid bind conflicts. Memory-bench RSS polling (every 5 s, 120 samples over a 10 min run) uses linear regression to detect growth slope > 1 MB/min.

25.8 Edge Cases

Case Handling
All backends ejected simultaneously Router returns 503 for all new requests; queue drains; infr_backend_health_all_ejected alert fires
Load gen sends 0-token prompt Request rejected at ingress with 422; counted in infr_request_total{status="invalid"}
Chaos scenario: backend restarts mid-stream Dispatch detects broken connection; stream error propagated to client; lease released; retry if idempotent
Clock skew injection: deadline in past Request immediately shed at admission; infr_queue_shed_total{reason="expired_deadline"}
Differential bench: backends return different token sequences for same prompt Expected (stochastic models); order invariant still holds per-stream
Throughput sweep at 0 RPS Load gen emits warning; skips measurement; no division-by-zero
Conformance test port collision Each test binds 0.0.0.0:0 (OS assigns port); extract bound port from TcpListener::local_addr()
Benchmark run interrupted mid-sweep Partial CSV written; regression gate skips incomplete run; logs warning
Mock backend admin API rate-limited Chaos runner retries with backoff; logs warning if injection delayed >100 ms
RSS measurement on non-Linux sysinfo crate cross-platform; RSS unavailable on some targets → skip OOM assertion, log WARN

25.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius / Root Cause Recovery
Conformance suite hangs tokio::time::timeout(60s) per test; failure = Elapsed CI blocked, no production impact; deadlock (queue full + no shedding + lease not released) Kill process; inspect tokio-console for blocked tasks; RUST_LOG=debug; add infr_conformance_test_timeout_total metric
Regression gate false positive CI blocks PR; P99 TTFT > baseline×1.10 from bench-machine load, not code Blocks merge until triaged Manual override SKIP_BENCH_REGRESSION=1 + sign-off; re-run on clean machine; update baseline if confirmed regression-free
Chaos test leaves system in bad state Subsequent conformance tests fail (connection refused, health-probe failures) Isolated per-test process with full teardown in Drop cargo test re-runs from clean state; no persistent side effects
Load-gen OOM Process killed by OOM killer; no results written RPS too high + unconsumed reqwest response buffering Consume response stream token-by-token; memory-bounded per connection
Differential bench inconclusive P95 TTFT improvement < 5% (below noise floor) Mock latency distribution too narrow; unrealistic prefix-cache simulation Increase workload realism (ShareGPT dist); document minimum detectable effect size

25.10 Operational Notes

CI runs make bench-ci (conformance + fast chaos subset + regression gate, ≤10 min); nightly runs the full throughput sweep, chaos suite, and differential bench, posting results to the Grafana historical dashboard. The dedicated bench runner (16-core, 64 GB RAM, 10 Gbit NIC, CPU governor performance, NUMA pinning) is documented in infr-bench/README.md. Baseline updates require a PR with --update-baseline and 2 reviewer approvals. chaos_slow_backend and chaos_failed_backend scenarios replay weekly against staging to validate production config, with a Grafana annotation on each baseline update. The load-gen ShareGPT replay fixture is trimmed/anonymized with its license documented in infr-bench/fixtures/README.md; each major version publishes a stable BENCHMARKS.md differential-results table for external comparison. Failed chaos runs are debugged via INFR_CHAOS_LOG=debug cargo run -p infr-bench -- chaos --scenario slow_backend piped to jq. The mock backend admin API is versioned (/admin/v1/) for cross-version benchmarking compatibility.

Threat Model

Trust Boundaries

Boundary Direction Trust Level Enforcement Point
External clients → infr-ingress inbound untrusted TLS termination, rate-limit, size checks
infr-ingress → infr-queue internal trusted in-process channel, bounded capacity
infr-dispatch → backend replicas outbound semi-trusted mTLS, per-backend budget, circuit breaker
operator → infr-cli / config mgmt plane privileged local Unix socket or RBAC-gated HTTP
infr-registry config reload file/API trusted after auth SHA-256 digest + manifest check
infr-sched → orchestrator (autoscale) outbound trusted after auth signed webhook; HMAC-verified receiver
infr-telemetry → collector outbound trusted TLS; no secret data in spans/metrics

Attack Surface Analysis

Vector Target Mechanism Mitigation Detection Metric Recovery Strategy
Oversized prompt infr-ingress prompt_tokens flood → OOM routing metadata Hard cap max_prompt_tokens per model; 413 on exceed request_rejected_total{reason="oversized"} Shed; no state held
Request flood (DDoS) infr-queue saturate bounded queues → shed + 503 storm Per-IP / per-model token-bucket rate limiter pre-queue; global RPS ceiling admission_shed_total spike Backpressure propagates upstream; rate limiter absorbs burst
Slow-read client (slowloris-style) infr-ingress relay hold open connection while token stream stalls; occupies fd + task Per-stream write deadline; idle timeout stream_idle_timeout; max active streams cap stream_idle_timeout_total Close socket; Lease released; backend conn pool reclaimed
Prompt injection via headers infr-ingress inject X-Backend-Id / X-Infr-Priority to manipulate routing Strip all X-Infr-* hop headers from client requests before parse access log diff shows stripped headers N/A — strip-then-log
Replay / duplicate non-idempotent request infr-dispatch POST replayed → double inference; billing anomaly idempotent: bool gate; hedge disabled for non-idempotent; RequestId dedup cache (10 s TTL, LRU eviction) duplicate_request_total counter 409 Conflict on second arrival
Malformed SSE / chunked frame infr-dispatch relay partial chunk → corrupt token stream to client Frame parser returns Err(InferError::StreamCorrupt); close + conditional retry (idempotent only) stream_parse_error_total Retry or send error frame; terminate cleanly
Backend impersonation infr-dispatch → replica MITM substitutes legitimate backend addr mTLS with cert pinned in registry per-replica; cert rotation via infr-registry push cert fingerprint mismatch → Health::Ejected Remove replica entry; operator alert
Config file poisoning infr-registry reload attacker writes malformed TOML to watched path SHA-256 digest checked against operator-signed manifest; reject on mismatch config_reload_rejected_total Keep previous config active
Resource exhaustion via unbounded max_tokens infr-sched backend held indefinitely by long-running decode Per-request absolute timeout = slo.deadline + grace_ms; Lease timeout forces eviction lease_timeout_total Lease forcibly dropped; backend accounting freed
PrefixTree skew attack infr-route craft requests with rare prefix hashes to monopolize cache-hit routing to one backend PrefixTree entries expire on TTL; hash is SHA-256 of token IDs (content-addressed, not client-controlled) prefix_tree_eviction_total; per-backend hit concentration Eviction clears stale; hit concentration alert
Supply-chain (crate compromise) build pipeline compromised transitive dep injects malicious code cargo audit gate in CI; --locked Cargo.lock; deny.toml advisory block list; reproducible builds CI gate failure; RUSTSEC advisory Pin to last-clean commit; fast-patch release
Operator mistake: all replicas Ejected infr-health aggressive ejection threshold drains entire fleet Min-healthy floor: ejection blocked when would leave < min_healthy_replicas; floor configurable per model ejection_blocked_total; healthy_replicas_gauge Alert fires; operator manually unjects after investigating
Memory unsafety via TLS/HTTP2 dep unsafe surface in deps CVE exploited via malformed TLS record #![deny(unsafe_code)] in all infr-* crates; unsafe isolated to OS I/O in deps; RUSTSEC scanning RUSTSEC advisory; CVE feed Patch dep + redeploy; no router state to migrate
Privilege escalation via mgmt API infr-cli / admin endpoint unauthenticated remote access to eject/uneject, config reload Mgmt API bound to localhost / Unix domain socket only; no ambient Linux capabilities in container Unexpected remote TCP connect → alert Firewall egress rule; no-network-namespace pod
Persistence corruption (config) infr-registry config file disk corruption or torn write leaves partial TOML Atomic write (write-tmp + rename(2)); checksum validation on read config_checksum_fail_total Fall back to last-good config; atomic swap guarantees no partial state
Amplification via streaming infr-dispatch one small request generates large token stream → bandwidth exhaustion Per-stream max-token hard cap; total bandwidth budget per connection tokens_generated_total per client Truncate stream at cap; 429 on budget exceed
Clock skew / SLO deadline manipulation infr-queue client sets far-future deadline to bypass priority shedding slo.deadline clamped to now + max_slo_window_ms at ingress; never trust client clock slo_deadline_clamped_total Clamp enforced; no bypass possible

Threat Telemetry Summary

  • security_event_total{type} — categories: oversized, replay, cert_mismatch, config_reject, ejection_blocked, slo_clamp, amplification.
  • Structured log fields: client_ip (truncated to /24), request_id, model_id, rejection_reason — never log prompt or response content.
  • Trace spans: route.reason, backend.id, admission.outcome; no PII in span attributes.
  • Security events route to separate log stream for SIEM ingestion; 30-day retention minimum.

Failure Modes & Recovery

Taxonomy Overview

Class Scope Auto-Recovery Key Detection Signal
Single backend crash replica Yes — ejection + reroute health probe fail / TCP reset
All backends for model offline model-wide No — shed + alert healthy_replicas_gauge{model}==0
Network partition (router ↔ backend subset) partial fleet Partial — circuit break survivors circuit_open_total + probe timeout
KV-cache eviction storm backend(s) Signal only — no auto-scale kv_util spike + TTFT regression
Queue saturation per-model queue Yes — shed + backpressure queue_depth_gauge == queue_max
Lease accounting leak global accounting Partial — TTL expiry inflight_gauge diverges from actual
Registry config bad reload control plane Yes — rollback to last-good config_reload_rejected_total
Backend slow degradation (not crash) single replica Yes — EWMA TTFT ejection ewma_ttft_ms > ejection_threshold
Streaming relay stall single stream Yes — per-stream idle deadline stream_stall_total
PrefixTree logical corruption routing layer Yes — periodic flush + rebuild routing anomaly in audit log
Scheduler fairness violation per-tenant queue No — manual quota adjust queue_wait_p99 divergence by tenant
Disaggregated prefill/decode split failure per-request Yes — retry on combined backend disagg_split_fail_total
Telemetry pipeline loss observability N/A — best-effort delivery metrics gap / scrape failure
Operator config drift config state No — detect + alert config checksum change without deploy event
New replica thundering herd single new backend Partial — admission slow-start ramp admission_rate_limit_total spike
Hedging storm dispatch layer Yes — hedge budget cap hedge_request_total rate spike
Autoscale signal dropped orchestrator No — signal re-emitted on next tick autoscale_signal_total stale

Per-Class Detail

Single Backend Crash

  • Symptoms: TCP reset or HTTP 502 on backend connection; in-flight requests fail with InferError::BackendUnavailable.
  • Root cause: OOM-kill, process crash, kernel panic, deployment restart.
  • Detection: infr-health consecutive probe failures → Health::Ejected; health_probe_fail_total increments.
  • Auto: infr-dispatch catches error; retry on idempotent requests to next healthy replica from infr-route; non-idempotent → error to client.
  • Manual: infr-cli replica status <id>; infr-cli replica uneject <id> after backend restarts; verify health probe succeeds.
  • Forensic artifacts: ring buffer of last-N request IDs dispatched to replica (in infr-dispatch); backend process stderr; probe latency timeseries.
  • Long-term mitigation: min-replica floor policy; circuit breaker halts retry avalanche; backend liveness probe tuning.

All Backends for Model Offline

  • Symptoms: every replica for a model is Ejected or absent from registry; shed rate = 100%; TTFT not measurable; 503 on all requests.
  • Root cause: coordinated deployment restart, network zone outage, model version rollback removing all replicas.
  • Detection: healthy_replicas_gauge{model_id}==0 → Tier-1 page within 1 min.
  • Auto: infr-queue holds requests up to SLO deadline, then deadline-sheds them; no dispatch attempted (Health invariant enforced).
  • Manual: deploy / restart replica set; confirm registry sees replicas; infr-cli model status <id>; drain queue after replicas become healthy.
  • Forensics: registry snapshot at failure time (infr-registry audit log); ejection event sequence; autoscale signal history.
  • Long-term mitigation: minimum replica count enforced by orchestrator; autoscale-signal emission provides leading indicator.

Network Partition (Router ↔ Subset of Backends)

  • Symptoms: circuit breaker opens on partitioned replicas; healthy subset serves at reduced capacity; circuit_open_total elevated; TTFT p99 may spike due to load concentration.
  • Root cause: AZ network fault, BGP flap, pod network policy misconfiguration.
  • Detection: consecutive probe failures exceed ejection_threshold; Health::Ejected; circuit breaker OPEN state.
  • Auto: traffic reroutes to healthy replicas; circuit half-opens after recovery_window_s; probes restart; re-admits if probes pass.
  • Manual: infr-cli health probe <backend_id> to force probe; network path verification; infr-cli replica uneject after connectivity restored.
  • Forensics: probe latency histogram; TCP connection error type in dispatch log; backend address in structured log entry.

KV-Cache Eviction Storm

  • Symptoms: kv_util_gauge → 1.0 on backends; prefix-cache hit rate drops sharply; TTFT spikes across model; PrefixTree hits stale entries.
  • Root cause: sudden large-prompt workload; model loaded on insufficient GPU memory; high concurrent batch sizes.
  • Detection: kv_util_gauge > 0.9 for > 2 min; ewma_ttft_ms regression > SLO threshold → Tier-2 ticket.
  • Auto: infr-sched emits autoscale-up signal; infr-route falls back from prefix-aware to least-load routing.
  • Manual: reduce max_batch_size config; scale backend fleet; audit large-prompt request sources; PrefixTree flush to clear stale entries.
  • Long-term mitigation: admission control caps concurrent large-prompt requests; PrefixTree TTL eviction prevents stale routing.

Queue Saturation

  • Symptoms: queue_depth_gauge == queue_max; shed rate spikes; clients receive 503; backpressure propagates to ingress; H2_FLOW_CONTROL signals to upstream.
  • Root cause: traffic surge, backend fleet reduction, or SLO deadline too generous (long queue dwell).
  • Detection: admission_shed_total rate spike; queue depth gauge at ceiling; backpressure_event_total increments.
  • Auto: SLO-deadline eviction removes expired waiters; backpressure signal halts new ingress accepts; high-priority requests preempt low-priority slots.
  • Manual: increase queue_max if memory allows; scale backend fleet; reduce per-model RPS from rate limiter.
  • Long-term mitigation: per-priority lane queues (high/normal/low) prevent starvation; bounded-queue invariant enforced — no unbounded growth.

Lease Accounting Leak

  • Symptoms: inflight_gauge drifts upward over time; backends underutilized but router believes them at capacity; routing avoids over-estimated backends.
  • Root cause: code path drops Lease without decrementing counter (panic path, early return); Lease RAII guard not used.
  • Detection: inflight_gauge vs backend-reported inflight diverges > leak_threshold; Lease age histogram shows outliers.
  • Auto: Lease TTL expires after lease_max_age_ms and forcibly decrements counter.
  • Manual: infr-cli lease list --stale; force-expire individual leases; identify culprit request pattern from forensic log.
  • Forensics: Lease registry dump (id, backend, age, request_id); per-backend inflight histogram; code-path panic log.
  • Long-term mitigation: Lease invariant enforced via RAII wrapper type LeaseGuard — drops counter on all exit branches including panic.

Registry Config Bad Reload

  • Symptoms: config_reload_rejected_total increments; running config unchanged; new config silently not applied.
  • Root cause: TOML syntax error, missing required field, checksum mismatch (partial write or tampering).
  • Detection: reload inotify event fires; infr-registry deserialize fails; structured log records rejected config path + checksum diff.
  • Auto: previous config remains fully active; new config discarded; no service disruption.
  • Manual: infr-cli config validate <path> to check syntax; fix config file; re-trigger with infr-cli config reload.
  • Forensics: diff of rejected config vs active config; checksum mismatch detail in structured log; inotify event timestamp.

Streaming Relay Stall

  • Symptoms: client TPOT spike (no tokens arriving); stream appears frozen but connection open; stream_stall_total counter increments.
  • Root cause: backend token generation paused (OOM, GIL contention in vLLM, swap); network congestion; backpressure from slow client.
  • Detection: per-stream idle timer fires when no bytes forwarded in stream_idle_timeout_ms window.
  • Auto: infr-dispatch closes backend connection; sends stream-error frame to client; Lease released.
  • Manual: check ewma_ttft_ms on affected replica; verify backend process health; adjust stream_idle_timeout_ms config.
  • Forensics: request_id + backend_id + stall duration logged at ERROR level; backend response latency histogram.

Disaggregated Prefill/Decode Split Failure

  • Symptoms: prefill-phase backend unreachable after dispatch; decode backend holds stale KV memory slot; request stalls.
  • Root cause: prefill backend crashes mid-request; network partition between prefill and decode backend pools; KV transfer channel failure.
  • Detection: disagg_split_fail_total increments; prefill timeout fires; decode backend does not receive KV transfer.
  • Auto: retry entire request on a non-disaggregated (combined) backend if available; Lease on both prefill/decode backends released.
  • Manual: infr-cli sched disagg-status; inspect prefill/decode fleet health separately; verify KV transfer network path.
  • Long-term mitigation: disaggregation enabled only when both prefill and decode fleets independently healthy; failure falls back to combined-backend mode.

Hedging Storm

  • Symptoms: hedge_request_total rate spikes; backend load doubles; TTFT improves but throughput drops; non-idempotent requests mis-hedged.
  • Root cause: hedging enabled for non-idempotent requests by misconfiguration; TPOT baseline too aggressive triggering constant hedges.
  • Detection: hedge_request_total / request_total > 0.1 threshold alert; at-most-once invariant check (hedge only allowed on idempotent: true).
  • Auto: hedge budget cap limits concurrent hedges per model to max_hedge_fraction * inflight.
  • Manual: review hedge trigger threshold in config; verify idempotent flag on request types; disable hedging per-model if necessary.

Operational Readiness

Metrics Strategy

Metric Type Labels Alert Threshold
request_total Counter model, adapter, status_class —
request_duration_ms Histogram model, route_reason, backend p99 > SLO ttft_ms
ttft_ms Histogram model, backend p95 > SLO ttft_ms
tpot_ms Histogram model, backend p95 > SLO tpot_ms
queue_depth_gauge Gauge model, priority > 0.8 × queue_max
queue_wait_ms Histogram model, priority p99 > slo.deadline × 0.5
admission_shed_total Counter model, reason rate > 1% of RPS for 5 min
healthy_replicas_gauge Gauge model < min_healthy → page
kv_util_gauge Gauge backend > 0.9 sustained 2 min
prefix_cache_hit_rate Gauge model drop > 20% from baseline
circuit_open_total Counter backend any increment → ticket
ewma_ttft_ms Gauge backend > ejection_threshold → eject
inflight_gauge Gauge backend > backend_capacity
lease_timeout_total Counter model rate > 0 sustained
autoscale_signal_total Counter model, direction (up/down) —
config_reload_rejected_total Counter — any → ticket
security_event_total Counter type any cert_mismatch → page
disagg_split_fail_total Counter model rate > 0
hedge_request_total Counter model > 10% of request_total
tokens_generated_total Counter model, backend — (capacity planning)
  • Scrape interval: 5 s Prometheus pull; push-gateway for short-lived components.
  • Cardinality budget: < 50 k active time series; no high-cardinality labels (no request_id in metric labels).
  • Exemplars: every Histogram bucket links one TraceId for p99 exemplar drill-down in Grafana.
  • Retention: 15 days in Prometheus; 13 months in long-term store (Thanos/Cortex).

Tracing Strategy

  • Instrumentation: tracing crate + opentelemetry-otlp exporter (gRPC to OTel collector).
  • Span hierarchy per request:
  infr-ingress:parse
    └─ infr-queue:admit
         └─ infr-route:decide
              └─ infr-dispatch:forward
                   └─ infr-dispatch:stream_relay
  • Mandatory span attributes: request_id, model_id, adapter_id, backend_id, route_reason, queue_wait_ms, ttft_ms, admission_outcome.
  • Optional (debug-only): prefix_hash, kv_util_at_dispatch, ewma_ttft_at_dispatch.
  • Sampling policy: 100% for status=error; 10% for TTFT > SLO × 0.8; 1% baseline for success.
  • Head-based sampling decision at infr-ingress; propagated downstream via W3C TraceContext headers.
  • Backend propagation: TraceContext injected into forwarded HTTP/2 request; backends echo back in response headers for correlation.

Structured Logging

  • Format: JSON, one object per line; mandatory fields: ts (RFC3339+ns), level, request_id, model_id, backend_id, event, latency_ms, error_code.
  • Log level policy:
    • ERROR: ejections, shedding events, circuit-open, stream parse failure, cert mismatch, lease timeout.
    • WARN: retries, deadline misses, config reload rejected, hedging triggered.
    • INFO: config reload success, replica registered/removed, autoscale signal emitted.
    • DEBUG: individual routing decisions, queue admit/reject per request (sampled 1%).
  • Privacy rules: never log prompt content, response content; truncate client_ip to /24.
  • Sink: stdout → Fluent Bit/Vector → Loki or Elasticsearch; indexed fields: request_id, model_id, backend_id, event.
  • Security log stream: security_event_total emitters also write to separate structured stream → SIEM; 30-day retention minimum.

Dashboard Design (Grafana)

Dashboard Key Panels
Ingress Overview RPS, error rate (4xx/5xx), p50/p95/p99 TTFT, active streams, connection count
Queue Health Per-model queue depth %, shed rate by reason, priority lane breakdown, queue_wait p99
Backend Fleet Per-replica health state heatmap, kv_util heatmap, ewma_ttft timeseries, inflight vs capacity
Routing Analytics Prefix-cache hit rate, route_reason distribution pie, circuit breaker state table
SLO Burn Rate TTFT + TPOT + availability burn at 1h/6h/24h windows; error budget remaining gauge
Scheduler Autoscale signal history, per-tenant fairness quota utilization, disagg split success rate
Security Events security_event_total by type, cert_mismatch timeline, admission shed by IP bucket

Alerting Philosophy

  • Page (Tier 1): customer-visible SLO breach or fleet-health emergency only.
  • Ticket (Tier 2): leading indicators, internal degradation not yet SLO-impacting.
  • Tier 1 (page, 5-min eval): healthy_replicas_gauge{model}==0; TTFT p95 > SLO × 1.5; admission_shed_rate > 5% for 5 min; security_event{type=cert_mismatch} any occurrence.
  • Tier 2 (ticket, 10-min eval): kv_util_gauge > 0.9 sustained; circuit_open_total increment; config_reload_rejected_total > 0; lease_timeout_total rate > 0.
  • Fatigue prevention: group by model_id; 5-min evaluation windows; inhibit Tier 2 while Tier 1 firing for same model.
  • Noise floor: alert only if sustained for 2 consecutive eval windows (prevents flap-alerts).

SLO Definitions

SLO Objective Measurement Window Error Budget
TTFT p95 ≤ model-specific (e.g. 500 ms) rolling 28d 0.1% requests
TPOT p95 ≤ model-specific (e.g. 50 ms/token) rolling 28d 0.1% requests
Availability (non-5xx / total) ≥ 99.9% rolling 28d 43.2 min/month
Admission shed rate ≤ 0.1% of requests rolling 1h —
Config reload latency ≤ 5 s from file change to active per-event —
Routing decision latency (p99) ≤ 2 ms (excl. queue wait) rolling 1h — (internal SLO)
  • SLO windows tracked via Prometheus recording rules at 5-min resolution.
  • Error budget alerting: burn > 5× budget rate for 1h fires Tier-1 page.

Incident Response Procedure

  1. Alert fires → on-call acknowledges within 5 min (PagerDuty escalation on no-ack).
  2. Check SLO burn dashboard: latency or availability burn? → determines triage path.
  3. Latency path: check TTFT/TPOT p99 → kv_util_gauge, ewma_ttft_ms, inflight_gauge; identify overloaded backend.
  4. Availability path: check admission_shed_total, healthy_replicas_gauge; circuit breaker state table.
  5. Isolate: infr-cli replica status; pull trace exemplar from p99 histogram bucket; correlate with request_id in logs.
  6. Mitigate: infr-cli replica eject/uneject; hot config reload (infr-cli config reload); manual autoscale.
  7. Communicate: update status page; notify impacted tenants if SLO breached.
  8. Resolve: confirm SLO recovery on burn dashboard; maintain observation 30 min post-fix.
  9. Postmortem: within 48 h; root cause, timeline, contributing factors, prevention actions.

Runbooks (ops wiki links; titles below)

ID Title Trigger
RB-01 All replicas ejected for model healthy_replicas_gauge==0
RB-02 Queue saturation / mass 503 shed_rate > 5%
RB-03 TTFT regression (no crash) TTFT p95 > SLO × 1.5
RB-04 Circuit breaker stuck open circuit_open_total not clearing
RB-05 Config reload rejected loop config_reload_rejected_total > 0
RB-06 Lease accounting drift inflight divergence > threshold
RB-07 New replica thundering herd admission_rate_limit_total spike on new replica
RB-08 Disaggregated split failure storm disagg_split_fail_total rate spike
RB-09 Hedging storm hedge_fraction > 10%
RB-10 Security event: cert mismatch security_event{type=cert_mismatch}

Deployment Topology

  [Ingress LB]
      |  HTTP/1.1 + HTTP/2 + gRPC (TLS terminated)
  [infr-ingress pods] × N  (stateless, anti-affinity spread)
      |  in-process
  [infr-queue / infr-route / infr-sched] (same process; in-process channels)
      |  HTTP/2 + mTLS
  [backend replicas: vLLM / SGLang / TGI] (separate pods, GPU nodes)
      |
  [infr-telemetry → OTel Collector → Prometheus / Loki / Jaeger]
  • Stateless: infr-ingress / infr-queue / infr-route / infr-dispatch / infr-sched colocated per pod; no shared mutable state between pods except registry (ConfigMap) and health state (per-pod probing, no cross-pod sync needed).
  • Config: TOML ConfigMap-mounted at /etc/infr/config.toml; infr-registry watches inotify for reload within 5 s.
  • Autoscaling: HPA on queue_depth_gauge + RPS; VPA disabled (memory profile predictable; bounded-queue invariant ensures no OOM).
  • Placement: router pod anti-affinity from backend GPU pods; route via cluster-internal DNS; no NodePort (ClusterIP only for backends).

Canary + Rolling Upgrade Strategy

  • Canary: 5% traffic weight via ingress load-balancer split; monitor SLO burn for 30 min before expanding.
  • Metric gate for canary promotion: TTFT p95 ≤ baseline × 1.05; shed rate ≤ baseline × 1.1; error rate ≤ baseline × 1.05.
  • Automated gate: Argo Rollouts analysis template on SLO burn rate; auto-abort if gate fails.
  • Rolling: maxUnavailable=0, maxSurge=25%; readiness probe (/healthz returning 200) gates each new pod before old pod terminates.
  • Drain: SIGTERM → infr-ingress closes listen socket (stops accepting); in-flight requests complete or hit drain_timeout_s (default 30 s); process exits.

Rollback Strategy

  • Automated trigger: canary metric gate failure → Argo Rollouts auto-promotes previous revision.
  • Manual trigger: kubectl rollout undo deployment/infr-ingress; propagates within one rolling window.
  • Config rollback: kubectl rollout undo configmap/infr-config; registry inotify triggers reload within 5 s.
  • State: zero persistent state in router; rollback instantaneous once old pods pass readiness.
  • Registry rebuild: model → replica map rebuilt from backend probes within one probe interval (default 10 s).

Migration Strategy (Major Version)

  • Old (N-1) and new (N) router versions coexist behind ingress weight split; no API version negotiation needed for minor changes.
  • Feature flags in config TOML ([features] disaggregated_prefill = false) control new behaviors; disabled by default on upgrade.
  • Weight shift: 5% → 20% → 50% → 100% over 4h; each step gated on SLO burn metrics.
  • Rollback window: 24h; after clean run, old version decommissioned.
  • Config migration tool: infr-cli config migrate --from v1 --to v2 generates new TOML from old; operator reviews diff before applying.

Compatibility & Versioning

Wire Compatibility

Interface Protocol Stability Class Change Policy
Client HTTP/1.1 + HTTP/2 JSON OpenAI-compatible REST Stable Additive-only; no field removal without 2-release deprecation
Client gRPC proto3 Stable Field additions OK; no field renumber, no removal without major bump
SSE token stream data: <json>\n\n Stable New fields in token object; clients MUST ignore unknown fields
infr-ingress → backend HTTP/2 OpenAI-compat + vLLM extensions Semi-stable Backend API version via Accept: application/vnd.infr.v<N>+json negotiation
Metrics /metrics Prometheus text v0.0.4 Stable New metrics added freely; label additions require 1-release notice; renames require 2-release parallel
Tracing OTLP/gRPC OTLP 1.x Stable Span attribute additions OK; attribute removal in minor requires 1-release notice
Mgmt API (infr-cli → Unix socket) JSON over Unix domain socket Internal Breaking changes allowed at any major version; no external stability guarantee
Autoscale webhook HTTPS POST JSON Semi-stable Payload schema versioned; schema_version field in body
Health probe endpoint HTTP GET /healthz, /readyz Stable Response fields additive-only

Config Schema Evolution

  • Config format: TOML; serde-derived structs with #[serde(default)] on all new fields → backward-compatible deserialization on upgrade.
  • Removed keys: emit WARN log "unknown config key X ignored" rather than hard-error; #[serde(deny_unknown_fields)] NOT used in production.
  • Validation: infr-cli config validate runs full deserialization + semantic checks; use before applying.
  • No persistent store beyond config file; no migration scripts needed for router state itself.
  • PrefixTree: in-memory; rebuilt from live traffic on restart; no storage schema versioning.
  • Registry replica map: rebuilt from probe results; no schema version needed.
  • Metrics label renames: recording rules in parallel for 2 releases; old label deprecated in release N, removed in N+2.

API Type Stability (infr-core)

  • infr-core crate types: stable at v1.0; breaking changes force workspace MAJOR bump.
  • RequestId(Ulid): ULID string encoding frozen; lexicographic sort ordering guaranteed.
  • InferError enum: #[non_exhaustive]; new variants addable in MINOR; removal requires MAJOR.
  • RouteReason enum: #[non_exhaustive]; new variants in MINOR; telemetry consumers must handle unknown string values.
  • RoutingDecision, LoadState, Lease, Slo: new fields addable in MINOR (with Default); field removal or rename in MAJOR only.
  • Priority enum: ordinal ordering stable; new priority levels require MAJOR if they shift existing orderings.
  • Health enum: variants Healthy/Degraded/Ejected stable; no addition/removal without MAJOR.

Semantic Versioning Policy

Bump Conditions
MAJOR Breaking wire protocol change; removal of public API field or type; InferError variant removal; MSRV increase
MINOR New crate in workspace; new API field (with default); new RouteReason/InferError variant; new config key; new metric; MSRV increase announced (effective next MAJOR)
PATCH Bug fix; performance improvement; no behavioral or API change; doc update
Pre-release (-alpha, -beta) No stability guarantee; API may break between pre-releases; not for production
  • Workspace versioned as one unit (single [workspace.package] version); all infr-* crates share version.
  • MSRV (1.78) increase: announced in MINOR release notes; effective in next MAJOR; minimum 2-release (60-day) notice.
  • CHANGELOG.md: maintained per SemVer convention; every release entry includes: breaking changes, deprecations, new features, bug fixes.

Rolling-Upgrade Compatibility Guarantees

  • Router pods at version N-1 and N coexist safely during rolling upgrade; no cross-pod coordination protocol.
  • Request struct: new fields are #[serde(default)]; old sender sends defaults; new receiver accepts; no breakage.
  • Health probe JSON: new fields ignored by old probers via #[serde(default)] + deny_unknown disabled.
  • Config hot-reload: new config keys with defaults silently accepted by running process; reload does not restart process.
  • Guarantee during rolling upgrade: zero requests lost (drain invariant enforced); SLO compliance may degrade < 5% transiently during pod churn.
  • In-process channel protocol between infr-* components: all message types use #[non_exhaustive] enums; receivers handle unknown variants gracefully.

Deprecation Policy

Item Type Deprecation Signal Deprecated Duration Removal
Config key deprecated = true annotation in schema doc; WARN on load 2 MINOR releases Next MINOR after duration
Rust struct field #[deprecated] attribute; still deserialized 2 MINOR releases MAJOR
Metric name/label # HELP doc marks as deprecated; recording rule aliases old to new 1 MINOR release Next MINOR
RouteReason variant #[deprecated] variant; maps to new variant in telemetry layer 1 MAJOR release Next MAJOR
InferError variant #[deprecated]; still matchable 1 MAJOR release Next MAJOR
CLI subcommand deprecated flag in clap definition; usage prints migration hint 2 MINOR releases MAJOR
  • Removal timeline communicated in CHANGELOG.md with minimum 60-day advance notice for all stable interfaces.
  • Emergency removals (security CVE): may skip deprecation period; communicated via security advisory + 7-day notice minimum.

Migration Path Guarantees

Upgrade Path Downtime Required Config Migration State Migration
Patch → Patch Zero (rolling) None None
Minor → Minor Zero (rolling) Automatic (new keys default) None
Major → Major Zero (parallel run) infr-cli config migrate required None (no persistent state)
Major rollback Zero (weight-shift) Revert to old ConfigMap None
Downgrade (same MAJOR) Zero (rolling) Old config silently ignores new keys None
  • Config migration tool (infr-cli config migrate --from v1 --to v2) is idempotent; safe to re-run.
  • Generated migrated config diff is printed for operator review before --apply.
  • Post-upgrade validation: infr-cli config validate + infr-cli health check must both succeed before decommissioning old version.

Testing & Verification Strategy

Unit Testing — Per-Crate Contracts

Crate Focus Key Fixtures
infr-core type serialization, Ulid uniqueness, PrefixHash collision roundtrip serde, hash stability across restarts
infr-route PrefixTree longest-match, routing policy selection, RouteReason assignment synthetic token sequences with shared prefixes, adapter/model combos
infr-queue bounded-queue invariant: enqueue at cap → shed/backpressure, never grow fill to N, assert next enqueue returns Err::Shed or blocks
infr-health EWMA convergence, ejection state-machine transitions (Healthy→Degraded→Ejected→Healthy) mock LoadState stream, inject latency spike
infr-dispatch circuit-breaker open/half-open/closed transitions, timeout enforcement, token stream ordering mock backend socket, inject partial/delayed writes
infr-sched fairness quota enforcement, autoscale-signal threshold crossing synthetic load curves, quota table
infr-ingress request parsing (HTTP/1.1, HTTP/2, gRPC), prefix_hash stability, SSE framing corpus of real/malformed request bytes
infr-telemetry metric label cardinality, trace span parent linkage, no-double-emit mock recorder, assert label set
  • Every public function has at least one success + one failure unit test.
  • Invariant tests are #[should_panic]-annotated where violation must be caught at dev time.
  • proptest / quickcheck for: PrefixTree (any token sequence → longest match ≤ input length), EWMA monotone on constant input, queue depth never exceeds cap under random enqueue/dequeue.

Integration Testing — Multi-Crate Scenarios

  • Harness: infr-bench spawns mock backends (axum echo servers returning synthetic token streams).
  • Scenario matrix:
Scenario What is exercised
single backend, one model, 100 req full path ingress→queue→route→dispatch→telemetry
3 backends, prefix-cache hit routing PrefixTree longest match selects correct BackendId
backend ejection during stream infr-health ejects mid-flight; dispatch retries to healthy replica
queue saturation + shed Bounded-queue invariant: assert zero OOM, shed count matches
adapter failover adapter removed from registry mid-batch; route falls back to adapter-free backend
SLO deadline expiry queued request TTL exceeded → shed before dispatch
disaggregated prefill/decode infr-sched assigns prefill→replica-A, decode→replica-B; stream reassembly correct
  • Integration tests use tokio::test with #[timeout] guards; no wall-clock sleeps.
  • Assertions: response body byte-order preserved (Order invariant), Lease released on completion, BackendId accounting matches telemetry counters.

Differential Testing

  • Compare infr-route output against reference Python router (vLLM production-stack) on shared request logs: same routing decision ≥ 95% (delta only from timing).
  • Differential stream validator: byte-by-byte compare streamed tokens from infr-dispatch vs direct backend for identical prompts.
  • Schema diff on RoutingDecision JSON between releases: no field removal without migration.

Property-Based Testing (proptest / arbitrary)

  • PrefixTree: insert N random sequences, query each — returned BackendId set ⊆ inserted set; longest match never returns prefix longer than query.
  • Slo + Priority: any combination admits or sheds deterministically (no NaN/panic from f32 arithmetic).
  • LoadState EWMA: random f32 stream → ewma always in [0.0, 1.0]; never NaN.
  • infr-queue: random sequence of push/pop/drain under concurrent threads → queue_depth never > cap.

Fuzzing (cargo-fuzz / libfuzzer)

Target Corpus seed Invariant checked
fuzz_request_parse real HTTP captures no panic, no memory growth
fuzz_prefix_hash byte sequences of varying length no collision assertion fires
fuzz_grpc_decode gRPC framing bytes graceful InferError, no UB
fuzz_routing_decision serialized RoutingDecision JSON deser never panics
  • CI runs fuzz targets for 60 s per PR; nightly runs 1 h; corpus stored in repo under fuzz/corpus/.

Chaos and Fault Injection

  • Fault injection points: backend TCP drop mid-stream, backend returns 500, backend stalls (no bytes for 10 s), backend sends wrong model tokens, clock skew in SLO deadline.
  • Chaos harness (infr-bench --chaos): randomly kills/restores mock backends; asserts router never dispatches to Ejected backend (Health invariant), queue depth stays bounded.
  • Network partition: isolate registry from health probes; assert degraded→ejected after missed heartbeats, not panic.
  • Memory pressure test: ulimit -v-capped process; send 10k requests; assert no OOM kill, graceful shedding.

Deterministic Replay

  • Record: request log (RequestId, timestamp, model, prefix_hash, slo, backend responses) serialized as ReplayTrace CBOR.
  • Replay: feed trace into router with mocked time (tokio::time::pause) and mocked backends; assert identical RoutingDecision sequence.
  • Use case: reproduce production routing anomalies locally without live backends.

Soak and Endurance Testing

  • 72-hour soak at 70% target throughput; assert: no memory growth >5% over baseline, no fd leaks, no thread count growth, P99 TTFT ≤ 2× P50 TTFT throughout.
  • Metrics collected every 60 s; alert on regression >10% vs hour-1 baseline.

Performance Regression Testing

  • Criterion.rs benchmarks in infr-bench: bench_route_decision (1k req/iter), bench_prefix_tree_lookup (10k lookups), bench_queue_admit (saturated queue).
  • CI enforces: no >5% regression on any Criterion benchmark vs main; fails PR if exceeded.
  • Flamegraph captured on benchmark regression for triage.

Conformance and Compatibility

  • OpenAI-compatible /v1/chat/completions conformance suite: schema validation, SSE stream format, error code mapping.
  • gRPC: proto descriptor compatibility check across releases (no breaking field removal).
  • Adapter routing compatibility: every supported adapter type (LoRA, prefix-tuning) has a named test fixture.

Build & Release Engineering

Reproducible Builds

  • Cargo workspace with pinned Cargo.lock committed; lockfile changes require explicit PR review.
  • SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH set in CI; build timestamps injected only via infr-cli --version (not embedded in binary hotpath).
  • Docker image: --platform linux/amd64,linux/arm64 multi-arch via docker buildx; base image pinned by digest (FROM rust:1.78-slim@sha256:<digest>).
  • Hermetic build: no network access during cargo build; vendored deps via cargo vendor or Artifactory mirror.

Artifact Signing and Binary Provenance

  • All release binaries signed with cosign (keyless, Sigstore OIDC); .sig attached to GitHub Release and OCI image.
  • SLSA provenance level 2: GitHub Actions provenance attestation on every release artifact.
  • Reproducibility check: two independent CI workers build same tag; sha256sum diff must be empty before publish.

SBOM Generation

  • cargo sbom (CycloneDX format) generated per release; attached to GitHub Release as sbom.cdx.json.
  • SBOM includes: crate name, version, license SPDX expression, source URL, checksum.
  • Dependency audit: cargo-deny (license policy, advisory DB) runs on every PR; blocks on unmaintained or vulnerability findings.

Supply-Chain Verification

  • cargo-vet (Mozilla) audits: all first-use and version-bump of third-party crates require an audit entry or exemption.
  • Dependabot / Renovate: automated PRs for dependency updates; auto-merge if CI green + no advisory.
  • cargo-geiger run on every release; unsafe code delta reviewed manually.

CI/CD Topology

PR branch → [lint: clippy --deny warnings, rustfmt --check]
          → [test: cargo test --all-features --workspace]
          → [fuzz: 60s per target]
          → [bench: Criterion regression gate]
          → [deny: cargo-deny check]
          → [vet: cargo-vet check]
          → merge to main
main      → [release-build: cross-compile linux/amd64, linux/arm64]
          → [SBOM generation]
          → [sign: cosign + SLSA provenance]
          → [integration test: mock-backend suite]
          → [publish: OCI push + GitHub Release]
  • Matrix: [stable, MSRV 1.78] × [linux/amd64, linux/arm64]; MSRV build uses locked Rust version.
  • Cache: sccache (S3 backend) for incremental compilation; cache key = Cargo.lock SHA.

Version Stamping

  • Semantic versioning: MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH; workspace version field in root Cargo.toml drives all crates.
  • Git tag v{version} triggers release pipeline; pre-release tags (-rc.1) trigger staging-only publish.
  • infr-cli --version prints: infr-router vX.Y.Z (git: <SHA>, built: <ISO8601>, rustc: <version>).
  • Feature flags: infr-sched/disaggregated gated behind --features disaggregated; off by default until GA.

Release Channels

Channel Trigger Artifact target Promotion gate
nightly merge to main OCI :nightly tag CI green
rc v*-rc.* tag OCI + GitHub pre-release manual QA sign-off
stable v* tag (no pre) OCI :latest + versioned SLSA provenance + SBOM

Dependency Auditing Checklist (per release)

  • cargo audit — no high/critical advisories.
  • cargo-deny — license list clean (MIT/Apache-2.0/ISC/MPL-2.0 allowed; GPL blocked).
  • cargo-vet — all new crates audited or exempted with justification.
  • cargo-geiger — unsafe delta reviewed; new unsafe blocks require comment explaining necessity.

Release Automation

  • Release notes: auto-generated from git log --oneline since last tag, grouped by conventional-commit type; human-reviewed before publish.
  • Rollback: OCI tags are immutable; rollback = re-pin deployment to previous versioned tag; no latest in production manifests.
  • Hotfix channel: branch hotfix/vX.Y.Z+1 from tag, cherry-pick, re-run full pipeline.

Performance Engineering

Benchmarking Strategy

Benchmark Crate Metric Target
bench_route_decision infr-route decisions/s ≥ 500k/s single-core
bench_prefix_tree_lookup infr-route lookups/s ≥ 2M/s (hot path)
bench_queue_admit infr-queue admits/s ≥ 1M/s
bench_dispatch_relay infr-dispatch token bytes/s ≥ 1 GB/s relay throughput
bench_health_ewma infr-health updates/s ≥ 5M/s
bench_telemetry_record infr-telemetry spans/s ≥ 200k/s
bench_ingress_parse infr-ingress req/s ≥ 200k/s HTTP/2
  • Criterion.rs: 100-iteration warm-up, 1000-iteration sample; statistical regression threshold 5%.
  • Benchmarks run on dedicated c2-standard-16 bare-metal CI runner; isolate CPU governor to performance.
  • Micro-benchmarks cover hot path only; end-to-end throughput measured separately via infr-bench harness.

Throughput and Latency Targets

# Metric Target Phase Gate Condition
M1 Request routing overhead < 500 µs P99 Phase 4 excludes backend latency; measured in infr-route
M2 TTFT routing overhead < 200 µs P50, < 1 ms P99 Phase 7 measured at infr-dispatch egress
M3 Sustained throughput ≥ 10k req/s Phase 7 8-core host, 20 backends
M4 Queue admit latency < 50 µs P99 Phase 6 no contention; mpsc enqueue
M5 PrefixTree lookup < 1 µs P99 Phase 5 1M-entry tree; ArcSwap read
M6 Streaming relay bandwidth ≥ 1 GB/s aggregate Phase 7 zero-copy H2 DATA frame relay
M7 Health probe cycle time < 200 ms full fleet Phase 3 500 backends × 1 Hz scrape
M8 Registry reload latency < 5 ms Phase 2 atomic ArcSwap swap; no request stall
M9 Circuit breaker decision < 10 µs Phase 8 atomic state read; no lock
M10 Autoscale signal latency < 500 ms end-to-end Phase 11 from queue-depth breach to signal emit
M11 Lease acquire latency < 5 µs P99 Phase 3 AtomicU64 fetch_add
M12 Router RSS at 10k inflight < 256 MB Phase 6 Lease table + queue + PrefixTree combined

Profiling Methodology

  • CPU profiling: perf record -g + flamegraph.pl for on-CPU; perf record --call-graph dwarf for async stacks.
  • Async Rust: tokio-console for task wakeup/scheduling analysis; identify tasks with high poll duration.
  • cargo-flamegraph integration: cargo flamegraph --bin infr-router -- <load-args> for ad-hoc profiling.
  • Off-CPU profiling: bpftrace script tracing futex/epoll wait; identify lock contention and I/O blocking.
  • Sampling interval: 99 Hz to avoid lockstep with scheduler.

Flamegraph Workflow

1. Run load: infr-bench --rps 5000 --duration 60s &
2. Profile: perf record -F 99 -g -p $(pgrep infr-router) -- sleep 30
3. perf script | stackcollapse-perf.pl | flamegraph.pl > out.svg
4. Annotate: identify frames > 5% self-time; cross-ref with tokio-console task list
5. Iterative: change → benchmark → flamegraph → repeat
  • Async stack unwinding requires RUSTFLAGS="-C force-frame-pointers=yes" in profiling builds.
  • Flamegraphs stored per release in perf/ directory for regression comparison.

Allocator Analysis

  • Default allocator: jemalloc via tikv-jemallocator; enabled in release profile.
  • Allocation profiling: jemalloc heap profiling (MALLOC_CONF=prof:true,prof_prefix:/tmp/infr) + jeprof for call-graph.
  • dhat (Valgrind heap analyzer) for allocation frequency analysis in tests.
  • Hot-path zero-copy invariant: token stream relay must not allocate per-token; assert with dhat instrumented test.
  • Detect allocator pressure: monitor jemalloc.stats.allocated vs resident; alert if fragmentation ratio > 1.5.

Lock and Contention Analysis

  • parking_lot mutexes throughout (not std::sync); lower contention, no poison propagation.
  • Contention hotspots: infr-registry replica map (read-mostly → DashMap or RwLock), infr-queue per-model queue head (per-model lock, not global).
  • loom for concurrency model checking of infr-queue and infr-health state machines.
  • Lock hold-time invariant: no lock held across .await points.

Cache and Syscall Analysis

  • L3 cache miss rate: perf stat -e cache-misses,cache-references during bench_prefix_tree_lookup; target < 5% miss rate for hot tree.
  • PrefixTree node layout: compact arena-allocated nodes to maximize cache locality; benchmark before/after layout changes.
  • Syscall profile: strace -c + perf trace to count epoll_wait, read, write per request; target < 10 syscalls/request on hot path.
  • io_uring evaluation: benchmark vs epoll for streaming relay; adopt if P99 improves > 15%.

Tail Latency Analysis

  • Histogram: hdrhistogram (HDR) for TTFT/TPOT; resolution 1 µs; max 60 s.
  • P99.9 analysis: collect 1M-sample runs; plot CDF; identify bimodal distributions (GC pause absent in Rust — check for lock contention, OS jitter).
  • Coordinated omission: infr-bench uses open-loop (Poisson arrivals) not closed-loop to avoid masking tail latency.
  • Jitter sources: OS scheduler, TCP nagle, epoll batch size, jemalloc background thread; each individually disabled/tuned and P99 re-measured.

Memory Profiling

  • Baseline RSS at idle: < 50 MB.
  • RSS under 10k inflight requests: < 500 MB (dominated by connection buffers, not metadata).
  • Memory growth over 72 h soak: < 5% drift from hour-1 baseline (detect slow leaks via jemalloc epoch snapshots).
  • Bounded structures: infr-queue capped by max_queue_depth; PrefixTree capped by max_prefix_entries with LRU eviction; verified with heaptrack.

Scalability Analysis

Bottleneck Map

Component Bottleneck type Ceiling Mitigation
PrefixTree lookup CPU single-threaded read ~2M lookups/s per core sharded tree per model; read-parallel
infr-queue per-model lock contention at high RPS ~500k admits/s per queue per-priority sub-queue; lock-free ring
infr-registry replica map read-write contention ~1M reads/s DashMap; epoch-based snapshot for routing
infr-dispatch conn pool fd limits, TCP bandwidth OS fd ulimit; NIC bandwidth increase ulimit -n; connection multiplexing (HTTP/2)
infr-health probe interval backend count × probe freq 10k backends × 1 Hz = 10k req/s batched scrape, pull-based metrics
infr-telemetry emit metric cardinality explosion OOM if label sets unbounded label allowlist; per-model not per-request labels
infr-sched fairness loop O(models × replicas) scan degrades at ~1k models hierarchical scheduler; lazy evaluation

Horizontal Scaling

  • infr-router is stateless except for: PrefixTree (soft state, rebuilt from backend health probes) and infr-queue (per-instance queues).
  • Multi-instance deployment: L4 load balancer (ECMP/RSS) distributes connections; each instance maintains its own PrefixTree.
  • PrefixTree consistency: eventual (each instance learns prefix occupancy via health probes independently); divergence tolerated — worst case: suboptimal cache routing, not correctness failure.
  • Session affinity (consistent hashing on prefix_hash): routes same prefix to same router instance → same backend → cache hit. Required for prefix-cache routing to be effective at scale.
  • Scale-out ceiling: limited by L4 LB capacity and connection state per instance; at >100 router instances, consider gossip-based PrefixTree sync (future work).

Vertical Scaling

  • CPU: routing hot path (PrefixTree lookup + policy evaluation) parallelizes across tokio worker threads; scales linearly to ~32 cores before diminishing returns.
  • Memory: infr-queue memory bounded by max_queue_depth × request_size; PrefixTree bounded by max_prefix_entries; scale by increasing both limits.
  • NIC: streaming relay is bandwidth-bound; upgrade to 100 GbE before hitting CPU limits for large token streams.
  • Vertical ceiling: single-node at ~32 cores + 100 GbE handles ~50k req/s; beyond that, horizontal scale.

Sharding and Partitioning

  • Model-based sharding: route all requests for model_id to a dedicated router shard; eliminates cross-shard PrefixTree reads.
  • Adapter-based partitioning: within a model shard, sub-shard by AdapterId for LoRA-heavy workloads.
  • Shard assignment: consistent hash on ModelId; shard map stored in etcd/consul; reloaded on change.
  • Rebalancing: gradual drain (infr-ingress graceful drain) before shard reassignment; no hard cutover.
  • Pitfall: hot model (single model >> all traffic) → overloaded shard; mitigate with replica-level load balancing within shard.

Replication Pressure and Coordination Overhead

  • infr-health probe fan-out: N router instances × M backends × probe_interval = probe load; at N=20, M=500, 1 Hz → 10k probes/s to backend fleet. Backend must support scrape; use pull-based Prometheus scrape to amortize.
  • infr-registry config reload: O(replicas) broadcast on topology change; use versioned epoch + diff-only propagation.
  • infr-sched autoscale signal: emitted per-model; at 1k models × 10 Hz → 10k signals/s; batch and debounce to 1 Hz per model before emitting to autoscaler.
  • PrefixTree sync (future gossip): O(nodes × entries) message size; cap entry sync at top-K most-recently-used prefixes per model.

Network Fanout

  • Per-request fanout = 1 (single backend); hedging = 2 (only for idempotent requests, transient).
  • Health probe fanout = N_routers × N_backends; decoupled from data plane.
  • Autoscale signal fanout = 1 (router → autoscaler per model); not per-request.
  • Telemetry fanout: metrics pushed to aggregator (Prometheus remote-write); trace spans to collector (OTLP); log lines to stdout (structured JSON); all async, non-blocking.
  • Fanout amplification risk: if hedging is mis-applied to non-idempotent requests → double-inference; guarded by idempotent: bool on Request (at-most-once invariant).

Scheduler Saturation

  • infr-sched runs O(models) fairness scan per scheduling epoch (default 100 ms).
  • At 1k models: 10k fairness evaluations/s → < 1 ms on modern CPU; safe.
  • At 10k models: 100k eval/s → ~10 ms per epoch → scheduler becomes bottleneck (> epoch interval).
  • Mitigation at scale: hierarchical scheduler (tenant→model tree); only active models (non-zero inflight) enter per-epoch scan.
